[2026-07-28 18:05 IDT] session=restore-sessions-and-fm-path (review pass)
TASK: Phase 80 / 80.1 — fix the code-review + security-review findings on the branch
FILES:
  M app/src/App.tsx (connectPane ConnectOpts.restoring; killSession keeps the hint on a failed kill;
    restoreSessions re-checks workspace + pane liveness per iteration and prunes fm paths)
  M app/src/fmPaths.ts (saveFmPaths MERGES instead of replacing; shape gates on read; pruneFmPaths)
  M app/src/FileManagerPane.tsx (persist effect gated on pathsReady — no half-resolved writes)
  A app/src/sessionRestore.test.ts (15 tests)
  A app/src/fmPaths.test.ts (17 tests)
RESULT: compiles-untested for the app (tsc green) / 32 unit tests PASS for the two new modules
  (run under node 22.6+ with --experimental-strip-types; on this box they were transpiled with
  the repo's own esbuild and run with `node --test` — same source, all 32 green)
NOTES: three P1s came out of the review, all in the new code:
  (1) the "pane already connected" check ran once up front, so a pane the user connected MANUALLY
      while restore was still working through the queue got killed and replaced (pane_connect kills
      any prior session for the pane). Now re-checked immediately before each connect.
  (2) connectPane resolves its workspace from activeWs(), but restore holds a snapshot across a
      multi-second loop — switching workspace mid-restore sent pane ids of A with workspace B
      (guaranteed "no pane" error, and ti.workspaceId left mistagged, which decides which host an
      OSC-8 file:// link downloads from). Now the loop bails when the active workspace changes.
  (3) FM: the persist effect fired after setLocalPath but BEFORE the remote path resolved (SSH
      round-trip), writing {local, remote:""} and wiping the remembered remote directory — exactly
      the case 80.1 exists for. Fixed twice over: the effect waits for both columns (pathsReady),
      and saveFmPaths now merges, so an empty field means "no opinion", never "forget it".
  Security pass: no injection (tmux name goes through shell_quote at lib.rs:2708; FM paths reach
  SFTP/fs::read_dir, no shell), no host-key auto-accept, no PTY content logged. Two accepted:
  a UNC local path from tampered localStorage would have opened an outbound SMB handshake at mount
  → now rejected on read (drive-absolute only; UNC still reachable by clicking); and restore can no
  longer raise a modal — a failed restore connect shows on the pane, never as a password/fingerprint
  prompt at boot (ConnectOpts.restoring).
  BEHAVIOR WORTH KNOWING: restore does one SSH handshake PER restored pane and re-runs the
  workspace setup_command on each — a fail2ban-fronted host sees a burst of auths at app start.
  STILL NEEDS LIVE TEST (Windows) — the 3-point checklist in the previous entry is unchanged.

[2026-07-28 21:40 IDT] session=restore-sessions-and-fm-path (live-test round 1)
TASK: Phase 80 — Yossi's first live run: panes still showed [Connect]; restore only appeared to
  work when he clicked Connect himself (which is the PRE-EXISTING `new-session -A` behavior)
FILES: M app/src/App.tsx
RESULT: compiles-untested (tsc green, 32 unit tests green) — needs a second live run
NOTES: two changes.
  (1) DIAGNOSTICS: every exit path in restoreSessions now says WHY it took it, via diag_log
      ("[restore] ..." in %APPDATA%\winmux\debug.log, dlog = user-visible) AND console.log.
      Counts candidates vs skipped-local / already-connected / no-remembered-session, logs the
      live tmux session count, per-pane re-attach or hint-drop, and a final N/M summary.
      Restore is invisible when it works and looks exactly like "feature not built" when it
      doesn't — on a Windows-only app that meant a full CI round-trip per guess.
  (2) REAL GAP FOUND while wiring it: the gate was `if (!isRemoteWorkspace(ws)) return;`, and
      isRemoteWorkspace only looks at the WORKSPACE-level connection. A workspace whose PANES
      each carry their own SSH connection (exactly what isRemoteEffective exists for) has no
      workspace connection, so restore bailed before looking at a single pane. That setup could
      never restore. Now: candidates are collected first; the workspace-level pre-flight
      (workspace_ensure_connected + pane_list_tmux_sessions, both workspace-scoped —
      ensure_connected reads workspace.connection and no-ops without one) runs ONLY when the
      workspace declares its own connection; otherwise panes re-attach directly and their own
      connection authenticates. Trade-off logged in the code: without the pre-flight list, a
      session that died on the server gets recreated empty by `new-session -A` under the same
      name instead of falling back to [Connect] — recoverable, and better than not working.
  Still to confirm from Yossi's debug.log which path his setup takes; if the line says
  "server reported 0 tmux sessions", the cause is password-auth (the headless gate can't open
  it) and the fix is a deliberate decision about prompting at boot, not a bug.

[2026-07-28 22:35 IDT] session=restore-sessions-and-fm-path (dead-session rule)
TASK: Yossi: "if the session is dead, don't open anything — show the Connect buttons"
FILES:
  M app/src-tauri/src/lib.rs (NEW command pane_probe_tmux_sessions + list_tmux_sessions_via_handle
    extracted from pane_list_tmux_sessions; registered in the invoke_handler)
  M app/src/App.tsx (restore attaches ONLY against a confirmed-live session list)
RESULT: tsc green; NOT COMPILED LOCALLY — no Rust toolchain on this box. The CI windows job runs
  the full `tauri build`, so cargo errors surface there (~10 min), not here. First Rust change of
  this branch; everything before it was frontend-only.
NOTES: the previous commit's per-pane-connection path had no liveness pre-check, so a dead session
  would have been RECREATED empty by `tmux new-session -A` under the remembered name. That is
  exactly what Yossi doesn't want. pane_probe_tmux_sessions answers "is it still there?" over the
  PANE's own connection: reuses a live workspace handle if one exists, else does a headless
  agent/key-only connect (no password, never auto-accepts an unknown host key — same posture as
  workspace_ensure_connected), runs tmux list-sessions, disconnects.
  THREE-WAY return, and the distinction is the whole point:
    Some(list) = we asked, these exist (possibly none) -> a missing name means the session died:
                 drop the hint, leave [Connect].
    None       = we could NOT ask (not SSH / password-only / passphrase-locked / unknown host key
                 / host down) -> leave [Connect], KEEP the hint for next time.
    Never Err for an unreachable host — "couldn't ask" is an answer, not a user-facing failure.
  Frontend probes once per distinct connection (JSON of pane.connection ?? ws.connection), not per
  pane — each probe is a full handshake.
  NET EFFECT: nothing is ever attached on a guess. A pane is re-attached only when the server has
  just confirmed the session name is live.

[2026-07-29 00:40 IDT] session=restore-sessions-and-fm-path (live-test round 2)
TASK: Yossi turned both flags on, connected a terminal, closed + reopened — pane still did not
  re-attach. Flags themselves persisted correctly.
FILES: M app/src/App.tsx, M app/src/sessionRestore.ts, M app/src/sessionRestore.test.ts
RESULT: tsc green, 34 unit tests green. Rust untouched this round. Needs another live run.
NOTES: found a real bug by reading, not by guessing — and made the write path stop depending on
  one timer.
  (1) THE BUG: the candidate filter used `pane.pane_kind !== "terminal"` directly. types.ts:85-91
      says pane_kind is only non-optional in the GENERATED BINDING and ships `paneKindOf()` with a
      `?? "terminal"` fallback precisely because a layout written before Phase 35 has no such
      field. On those layouts every terminal pane looked like "not a terminal", so the candidate
      list came out empty and restore did nothing — on exactly the installs old enough to have
      sessions worth restoring. Now uses paneKindOf(), and the log counts not-a-terminal skips.
  (2) HARDENING: the hint used to be written in ONE place — a 100ms setTimeout after a successful
      connectPane. refreshPersistence() now records every mapping the backend reports, on every
      refresh. That covers the picker, the reconnect driver and CLI-triggered connects too, and a
      single missed write no longer costs the session. ADD-ONLY: the map lists LIVE sessions, so a
      merely-detached pane is absent from it while its tmux session is alive — removal stays with
      the explicit forget calls (kill / close / "server says it's gone").
  (3) DIAGNOSTIC: restore now logs the whole remembered map (count + paneId→session) right after
      pruning, so "the map is empty" and "the map is fine but nothing matched" can never be
      confused again. New allPaneSessions() export, 2 tests.

2026-07-16 · Redesign themes — pass 6b: the REAL terminal-contrast fix
TASK: Yossi re-tested — Claude Code's bullet was STILL pale on light
  ground. Root cause understood properly this time: the bullet is drawn
  with 256-colour/truecolor escapes, not the 16 ANSI slots — a theme
  palette can't remap those, and apps pick them assuming a dark ground.
FIX: xterm's built-in contrast floor — `minimumContrastRatio: 4.5`
  (WCAG AA, VS Code's terminal default). The renderer auto-adjusts any
  foreground that misses the ratio against the current background, which
  covers indexed + truecolor output on BOTH light and dark grounds.
  Set at construction + re-asserted in setTerminalTheme (HMR-safe).

TASK: Yossi reported 2 bugs in the LOCAL HOST WSL flow (v0.4.5-beta.1

  (sha 8b6276) stashed - beta.3-mega already carries a newer one.

BUG A - the premise was wrong. No PowerShell workspace was created.
  NOTHING was created. Live evidence from Yossi's machine:
  - debug.log 12:21:32 -> "step InstallWsl failed (exit 1)", no
    "finalize failed" line -> finalize_wsl_workspace never called.
  - workspaces.json mtime = Jul 16 (run was Aug 3) -> never written.
  - "Local PowerShell" (connection.type=local, shell="") pre-dates the
    run; the app just stayed on it.
  - wsl --status on the machine = exit 50, LxssManager service ABSENT,
    no reboot pending -> WSL genuinely not installed; needs UAC elevation.
ROOT CAUSE (silent): local_setup.rs:1118
    if input.create_workspace && ran_wsl_chain && !chain_failed
  chain_failed=true (set :1093) -> gate false -> no workspace, no error,
  and local_setup.rs:1133 emits "local-setup:complete" UNCONDITIONALLY.
FALSE-SUCCESS CHAIN (3 independent bugs):
  1. LocalSetupFlow.tsx:437 - done screen prints localSetup.done.message
     ("ההתקנה המקומית הסתיימה") with NO failure branch.
  2. LocalSetupFlow.tsx:198-204 stateBadge has no "skipped" case -> the 5
     backend-skipped chain steps (emitted "skipped" at :759) render as
     grey "pending", i.e. look not-yet-started.
  3. local_setup.rs:1079 - InstallHooksInWsl matches Ok(Ok((_code,out)))
     => exit code DISCARDED, always green; it is also missing from the
     is_wsl_chain list (:746-754, only 6 of 7) so it is never skipped ->
     it ran against a non-existent distro and the run ENDED on a green
     check. That is what "finished and installed everything" looked like.
LATENT (not the cause here): elevation detection local_setup.rs:881 is an
  English substring match (low.contains("elevat")||"0x80070005") - breaks
  on localized Windows. This machine is en-US so it is not the trigger;
  it fired StepFailed, not ElevationRequired. Also: no reboot-pending
  concept in inspect_wsl (:378-473); no ERROR_SUCCESS_REBOOT_REQUIRED
  (3010) check.
LOG GAP: local_setup.rs:1096 logs only err.user_message() -> stderr is
  dropped. The UI red card had the real reason; the log does not. This is
  why the exact wsl --install failure text is unrecoverable.
NOT A BUG: finalize_wsl_workspace (:1139-1195) correctly builds
  Connection::Wsl (:1152); there is no Connection::Local anywhere in
  local_setup.rs. spawn_wsl_pty is reachable (lib.rs:5479, :1804).
  wsl.exe UTF-16 output IS handled (WSL_UTF8=1 + clean_wsl_output :55).

BUG B - encoding, not font and not bidi. Symptom predicted = MOJIBAKE.
ROOT CAUSE: lib.rs:1636-1676 spawn_local_pty sets 5 env vars and NO args.
  Repo-wide grep for chcp|65001|OutputEncoding|InputEncoding = ZERO hits.
  lib.rs:1513-1526 pick_default_shell tries pwsh.exe -> powershell.exe ->
  cmd.exe. VERIFIED on Yossi's machine: pwsh.exe NOT INSTALLED -> he gets
  powershell.exe 5.1, whose [Console]::OutputEncoding is the OEM codepage
  (437/862) and whose $OutputEncoding is ASCII. Hebrew -> garbage / "?".
  "Local PowerShell" ws has shell="" -> hits exactly this path.
WHY REMOTE IS FINE: spawn_wsl_pty/spawn_ssh target Linux = UTF-8 by
  construction. winmux never sets LANG/LC_ALL anywhere; the remote paths
  just get it for free. Local is the only OEM-codepage transport.
RULED OUT: font (terminalInstance.ts:499 stack has no Hebrew face but
  ends in generic monospace -> WebView2 falls back; would be tofu in ALL
  panes, incl. the working SSH ones). bidi (applyRowDirections
  terminalInstance.ts:933-962 is connection-agnostic; tuiOwnsBidi does
  not exist on this branch - docs/DECISIONS.md:47 is stale).
SEPARATE LATENT BUG FOUND: lib.rs:1565-1576 emit_data - strict UTF-8
  decoder; if leftover starts with an invalid byte, valid_up_to==0 ->
  early return WITHOUT draining -> pane goes permanently silent and the
  buffer grows unbounded. No cap, no error branch. Not this bug's
  mechanism (ConPTY re-encodes from UTF-16), but real. -> FOLLOWUPS.
RESULT: investigation only, no code changed. Awaiting Yossi's go-ahead.

=== 2026-08-03 | P1 emit_data UTF-8 stall + SSH stream-splice ===
BRANCH: fix-emit-data-utf8 (off wsl-smart-install-fix, NOT main - main
  lacks 1bcb584, the local-shell UTF-8 fix that this decoder pairs with).

BUG 1 (P1, the reported one). emit_data's decoder held `leftover` and
  emitted only up to `valid_up_to()`, with `if valid_up_to == 0 { return }`
  and no other error branch. It never looked at Utf8Error::error_len()
  (unused in the whole repo - checked). Consequence: a byte that can never
  BEGIN a sequence (0x80-0xBF, 0xC0/0xC1, 0xF5-0xFF) pins valid_up_to at 0
  permanently, because it is never drained. From that byte on the pane is
  silent forever AND the Vec grows 8KB per read with no cap. Real triggers:
  cat on a binary, less on a binary, a legacy exe writing raw CP1255,
  a truncated download.
  NOT the bug: split multi-byte chars. The incremental reassembly was
  already correct for those - which is why from_utf8_lossy over the whole
  buffer (the "Option A" in the brief) was REJECTED: it would turn every
  Hebrew letter split across two reads back into U+FFFD, re-opening the
  mojibake 1bcb584 just closed. Yossi agreed, dropped A.

FIX. New module src/pty_decode.rs - `Utf8Stream` + `Decoded`. Branches on
  error_len(): None = truncated tail, hold it for the next chunk;
  Some(n) = invalid, push U+FFFD and drain valid+n. Draining >=1 byte per
  invalid iteration is the anti-stall guarantee. Because the loop only
  ever leaves a truncated-valid prefix behind, leftover is <=3 bytes by
  construction - the unbounded growth is gone without needing a runtime
  cap (asserted in a test instead of a dead branch). The old `.unwrap()`
  at :1603 (Rule #4) is gone too - from_utf8_lossy on a known-valid slice
  is an exact decode.
  Logging: log_warn("PTY", ...) ONCE per stream, pane_id + session_id +
  byte count only, never the bytes (Rule #1). Once-per-stream so a binary
  dump can't flood debug.log.

BUG 2 (the P3 I flagged in the investigation, fixed same commit). The SSH
  io loop passed ONE `leftover` and ONE OscNotifyParser to BOTH
  ChannelMsg::Data and ChannelMsg::ExtendedData - stdout and stderr
  reassembled into a single buffer, so stdout's truncated tail could be
  completed by stderr's head (and vice versa for a partial OSC). Now two
  Utf8Streams + two parsers. Rare because request_pty is called on that
  channel, but silent when it fires.
  DELIBERATELY LEFT SHARED: bidi_filter, which is keyed by pane_id. A
  synthetic per-stream key would miss the per-pane smart_bidi toggle and
  silently leave stderr unfiltered - worse than the escape-splice it
  would prevent. Logged as the new P3 in FOLLOWUPS with that reasoning.

CHECKS: cargo check clean (only the 2 pre-existing warnings - unused
  Duration import, renumber_group_list dead code). 138/138 lib tests
  (124 before + 14 new in pty_decode): Hebrew + 4-byte emoji split at
  EVERY byte boundary, byte-at-a-time feed, each invalid lead byte
  proven not to stall, garbage-then-Hebrew recovery, leftover<=3 over
  64x8KB of junk, report-once, truncated-tail-is-not-a-report,
  two-streams-do-not-splice.
NOT VERIFIED LIVE. Compiles + unit-tested only. The live smoke that
  actually proves it: open a local pane, `cat` any .exe (or
  `Get-Content winmux-DEBUG-test.exe`), then type `echo שלום` - before
  the fix the pane is dead from the first bad byte; after it, garbage
  renders as replacement chars and the Hebrew still arrives. Expect ONE
  [WARN] [PTY] utf8: line in debug.log per pane.
Reverted the ts-rs trailing-whitespace churn in bindings/Settings.ts
  again (cosmetic, unrelated - same as last session).
------------------------------------------------------------------
[2026-08-03 ~15:10 IDT] session=wsl-smart-install-fix (install path)
TASK: Yossi: "שיוסיף תהליך התקנה נכון" — preflight + exit-code elevation
  detection + 3010 reboot handling + self-elevation. COMMIT: 65d6fdc.

PUSHED BACK ON ONE ITEM: the spec asked for `code == 740`
  (ERROR_ELEVATION_REQUIRED) on wsl.exe's exit. That check can never fire
  — 740 comes from CreateProcess refusing to launch a manifest-elevated
  binary, and wsl.exe launches fine unelevated then elevates INTERNALLY.
  A declined UAC surfaces as ERROR_CANCELLED (1223) from ShellExecuteEx
  itself. 740 is still mapped (policy-blocked ShellExecuteEx can raise
  it) but 1223 is the code that actually does the work.

WHAT LANDED
- is_elevated() via OpenProcessToken + TokenElevation (windows-sys, now a
  direct dep — was already transitive via tauri; needed the
  Win32_System_Registry feature too because SHELLEXECUTEINFOW carries an
  HKEY field).
- local_setup_preflight command -> WslPreflight {platform_ready,
  distro_present, elevated, needs_elevation}. needs_elevation is false
  once the platform is up: adding a further distro to a live platform
  does NOT need admin.
- InstallWsl branches: elevated -> inline wsl_cmd (output still reaches
  the UI); unelevated -> ShellExecuteEx("runas") on spawn_blocking. The
  runas path loses stdout (no pipes) and buys a trustworthy exit code.
- classify_wsl_install(code, output): code first, text only as fallback
  for old builds that always return 1. 3010 -> new RebootRequired
  variant, deliberately NOT ElevationRequired (retry can't work, so the
  card offers a restart, not a retry).
- restart_windows command: shutdown /r /t 0 via args array, behind TWO
  explicit clicks + copy saying other apps will close. Never automatic.
- Frontend: admin banner in the WSL group, "UAC will appear + maybe a
  reboot — continue?" gate before startRun, RebootRequired card.

TWO THINGS I INTRODUCED AND THEN FIXED IN THE SAME PASS
1. WaitForSingleObject(INFINITE) silently dropped the 1800s ceiling
   run_capture gives the inline path -> a stuck install would have hung
   the wizard on "running" forever. Now WSL_INSTALL_TIMEOUT_MS (1800s)
   with a clear timeout error. The process is deliberately NOT killed:
   it holds an elevated token we cannot re-acquire and interrupting
   Windows servicing mid-flight is worse than leaving it.
2. Restart buttons were wired backwards (the "later" primary armed the
   confirm). Rewritten: [Restart now] -> [Cancel][Yes, restart].

CHECKS: 146/146 lib tests (7 new in wsl_elevation_tests, incl. a Hebrew
  string that proves a localized failure no longer masquerades as a
  generic StepFailed), tsc clean, i18n 981 keys x4. Reverted ts-rs
  whitespace churn in bindings/Settings.ts again.
UNVERIFIED: the success path. This box has no WSL (wsl --status exit 50,
  LxssManager absent), so the UAC prompt, the 3010 card and the workspace
  that should follow are all compile+unit-test only. Needs a live run.
NOTE: winmux-utf8-test.exe appeared untracked in the repo root during
  this session — not mine, left alone (assumed Yossi testing the Bug B
  build).

WORKTREE: .claude/worktrees/font-detect-install, branch
  font-detect-install, based on origin/main 98f0c6d (local main was 82
  commits behind).
ROOT CAUSE (two independent bugs, both in the font path):
  1) settings.rs list_system_fonts() merges a curated baseline over the
     enumerated set and returned bare strings, so JetBrains Mono / Inter
     were offered as if installed. Picking one falls through the CSS
     fallback chain in settings.ts quoteFamily() back to Cascadia Mono =
     the default, so the screen is byte-identical and nothing signals
     failure.
  2) enumerate_windows_fonts() read HKLM only. Per-user installs land in
     HKCU (the no-admin path, and where our own installer will write) so
     they were invisible. Measured here: 409 HKLM vs 5 HKCU unseen.
FIX (fca8b54 + 73505cc):
  - FontFamilies now carries FontEntry{name, installed}; CSS generics
    always installed; enumeration failure => report all installed rather
    than unsubstantiated badges.
  - Enumerate HKLM+HKCU, split packed .fon names, strip more format tags.
  - extends_family() requires the trailing words to be known style words,
    else "Courier New" satisfies "Courier" (a test caught this).
  - strip_style_suffix() replaces a 6-suffix hardcode that omitted
    "Regular" => "Cascadia Code Regular" was a duplicate row.
  - looks_monospace() excludes "Monotype Corsiva" (matched on "Mono"type).
  - Settings: OK/MISS badge per family + inline notice under the select
    when the SELECTED family is known-missing. i18n en/he/ar/ru.
  - list_system_fonts now returns Result (Rule #6); dropped an `as any`
    in settings.ts (Rule #5).
VERIFIED: real run of the command, not just a compile. mono list 13->10
  rows, duplicates + script face gone, JetBrains Mono/Inter/Roboto flagged
  MISS, the 5 HKCU "Fb Vayikra" families now appear. cargo test 127 passed
  (10 new), tsc clean. NOT yet run in the live app - no UI smoke test.
NEXT: install flow (guided vs silent per-user) - awaiting Yossi on catalog
  scope. Decided to SKIP the standalone canvas-detection layer: the Rust
  enumeration covers it, canvas only pays off as a post-install check.

[2026-08-03] Phase 81 — file-transfer progress bar + cancel
TASK: Yossi reported a 60 MB upload in the File Manager shows nothing —
  no bar, no percent, no sign of life. Same suspected for downloads.
BRANCH: file-transfer-progress (worktree, from main @1614af7)

ROOT CAUSE (two separate bugs):
  1. file_upload / pane_upload_dropped did std::fs::read() into a Vec<u8>
     then one write_all() — no chunking at all, so progress was not just
     unreported but impossible. Downloads already streamed (Phase 75.3)
     and DID emit fm-download-progress; the UI just rendered it as a line
     of text in the status bar, and only inside the pane that started it.
  2. The "Upload from disk" picker used <input type="file"> and shipped
     the bytes over the Tauri IPC bridge as Array.from(Uint8Array) — a
     JSON array of one number per byte. 60 MB became ~250 MB of JSON and
     hung the webview before any transfer began.

FILES:
  M app/src-tauri/src/file_manager.rs  (stream_upload, ProgressEmitter,
      transfer registry + fm_transfer_cancel, both commands rewired,
      file_upload_bytes deleted, unit tests)
  M app/src-tauri/src/lib.rs           (command registration)
  A app/src/transferStore.ts           (global store + root listener)
  A app/src/TransferBar.tsx            (progress strip)
  M app/src/FileManagerPane.tsx        (mount bar, drop old listener,
      native dialog picker instead of <input type="file">)
  M app/src/App.tsx / App.css / i18n/{en,he,ar,ru}.json
  M app/src-tauri/capabilities/{default,popout}.json (dialog:allow-open)

EVENTS (new contract):
  fm-transfer-progress { id, direction, name, path, done, total, speed_bps }
  fm-transfer-done     { id, direction, name, ok, canceled, error }
  fm-download-progress REMOVED (was Phase 75.3, download-only).

RESULT: compiles-only — NOT verified live.
  Verified: cargo check + cargo build clean; tsc --noEmit clean; vite
  build clean; 4/4 new Rust unit tests pass (basename, cancel targeting,
  cancel-after-finish returns Err, registry cleanup); app shell boots in

  - SFTP transfer bar: no sshd and no WSL on this machine, so no remote
    to transfer against. The 60 MB upload, live readout and
    cancel-mid-transfer are all still unproven.

=== 2026-08-09 — elevated wsl --install was undiagnosable (fd1f537) ===

TRIGGER: Yossi ran the WSL wizard. It reported "ההתקנה לא הושלמה" and
debug.log had only:
  12:15:32 preflight: platform_ready=false distro_present=false
           elevated=false needs_elevation=true
  12:15:45 InstallWsl: no elevated token — requesting UAC consent
  12:16:08 step InstallWsl failed (exit 1)
23s between consent and failure — the elevated child did real work, then
exited 1. No reason anywhere.

ROOT CAUSE (of the blindness, not of the install):
  local_setup.rs:1195 `Ok(Ok(code)) => Ok((code, String::new()))`.
  ShellExecuteEx("runas") is the only way to elevate a child and it gives
  no stdout/stderr pipes, so the elevated branch passed an EMPTY string
  to classify_wsl_install. Its text fallbacks (contains "elevat" /
  "reboot") are therefore dead code on that path, and any code outside
  0/3010/1223/740 collapses to StepFailed{stderr:""}. Generic 1 is the
  common case. The doc comment called the lost pipes "an acceptable trade
  because the codes it does give us are unambiguous" — but 1 is exactly
  the ambiguous one.

FIX: elevate `cmd /s /c` instead of wsl.exe and redirect into a per-run
  temp file we own, read it back after the wait, delete on every exit
  path. `/s` makes cmd strip exactly our outer quote pair instead of
  rebalancing. WSL_UTF8=1 for the same reason wsl_cmd sets it (no space
  before `&&`, or the value becomes "1 " and wsl.exe reverts to UTF-16).
  Rule #3: a command string has no argv array, so both interpolated
  values are pre-validated — distro by the existing valid_distro_name,
  the log path by the new cmd_safe_path allowlist. An unrepresentable
  temp path drops the redirect and still installs. Exit code is now
  logged on success too, so a 3010 is in debug.log after the fact.

FILES: app/src-tauri/src/local_setup.rs (+cmd_safe_path,
  +wsl_install_cmd_params, run_wsl_install_elevated now returns
  (u32,String), caller logs it), 5 new tests in wsl_elevation_tests.

VERIFIED: the exact cmd parameter string run against a real cmd.exe with
  --status swapped in for --install — cmd exit 0, redirect file written,
  0 NUL bytes (UTF-8, no UTF-16 leak), real wsl.exe text captured.
  cargo test 266/0 · tsc exit 0 · vite ok · go 10/10.

WHAT THE ORIGINAL exit 1 WAS: unrecoverable — the message was discarded,
  which is the whole bug. But probing the machine afterwards with the new
  capture method showed WSL is now UP: `wsl -l -v` = "* Ubuntu Stopped 2",
  `wsl --version` = WSL 2.7.11.0 / kernel 6.18.33.2, and `wsl --status`
  exits 0 with 195 bytes — so inspect_wsl would now set wsl_ready=true.
  Either Yossi's manual elevated run landed it, or winmux's own elevated
  run did the work and still returned 1. Cannot tell retroactively.
  NOTE the WSL1 line in `wsl --status` output ("Please enable the Windows
  Subsystem for Linux optional component to use WSL1") is about WSL1 only
  and is harmless — wsl_ready keys off exit code + non-empty output, not
  that text, so it does not mis-trip.

NEXT: re-run the wizard. If it fails again the log will now carry the
  real message. WSL being up also unblocks the Phase 81 transfer bar,
  which had no SSH target on this machine.

=== 2026-08-09 — the release exe was built wrong (my error) ===

SYMPTOM: Yossi launched app.exe and got a WebView2
  "ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED — localhost" page INSIDE the winmux window.
  He reported it four times; I misread it three times as a leftover
  Claude preview pane and sent him chasing a browser tab. It was the app
  itself the whole way. His screenshot of the winmux title bar is what
  finally settled it.

CAUSE: I built with `cargo build --release`. That compiles and links
  fine, but tauri-build only embeds `frontendDist` when the build runs
  through the Tauri CLI, which injects the env that selects production
  mode. Without it the binary stays in dev mode and loads devUrl
  (http://localhost:1420) — a server that does not exist on a user
  machine. Both exes I produced this session (Aug 3 and 13:32) were
  broken this way, and I twice reported "exe unified built" on the
  strength of a clean compile. CLAUDE.md says "Verified = real run, not
  compile"; I did not follow it.

  Nothing in the Rust log hints at it: boot runs normally through
  "rpc server spawned" and then stops, because no frontend loads to call
  back in. That silence is the tell.

FIX: rebuilt with `npm run tauri build -- --no-bundle` (--no-bundle
  skips NSIS, so the known os error 32 cleanup false-alarm cannot even
  arise and Yossi gets the exe he actually needs). RELEASING.md already
  points at scripts/build-release.ps1 for real cuts — the doc was right,
  I just did not read it.

VERIFIED (real run this time):
  - this build's exact asset hashes are inside the exe:
    index-BJhbBlwx.css, index-zVEv1mLi.js, logo-BtuLyPip.svg — all 1.
    The broken build had none. `localhost:1420` shows up in BOTH kinds
    (the config is embedded regardless), so it is not a usable signal.
  - launched it: log went past setup into
    [TUNNEL] tcpip_forward got remote port 40159 and
    [SSH] workspace_ensure_connected: headless session up (method=Key).
    Those only fire when the frontend calls into Rust, i.e. the UI is up
    and auto-connected the workspace.

DOCS: added a "Building a runnable exe" section at the top of
  docs/CONTRIBUTING.md — the trap, the two correct commands, and the
  grep that tells a production binary from a dev one.

UNAFFECTED: the merges, the 266 tests and the wsl --install capture fix
  are all source-level and were never in question. Only my packaging of
  the binary was wrong.

[2026-08-10 16:04 IDT] session=macos-ports-upload-fix
TASK: Yossi (first live run on a real Mac): ports list empty + file
  upload broken (both drag-drop and SFTP panel), SSH workspace to a
  remote Linux server.
DIAGNOSIS: two unrelated causes, neither of them in the SFTP/port
  transport itself.
  (1) UPLOAD = frontend Windows assumptions, never touched by the port.
      There was NO platform awareness anywhere in app/src (no
      navigator.platform, no plugin-os). Local paths were joined with a
      literal `\`, so fullLocal() produced `/Users/yossi/Downloads\f.txt`
      and std::fs::read failed. Drag-drop hit-testing divided the drop
      position by devicePixelRatio: verified against wry 0.54.4 that
      webview2/drag_drop.rs:167 gives PHYSICAL px (ScreenToClient on a
      DPI-aware HWND) but wkwebview/drag_drop.rs:40,57,85 passes
      draggingLocation through unscaled (logical points, y-flipped) — so
      on Retina every coord was halved, the hit-test missed, and the drop
      was swallowed with no upload and no error.
  (2) PORTS = NOT root-caused. The whole pipeline is platform-agnostic
      (remote /proc/net/tcp watcher -> SSH reverse tunnel -> rpc_server)
      EXCEPT the local IPC hop, the one thing the port replaced (named
      pipe -> Unix socket), so that was the suspect. 53cd284's Mac log
      REFUTES it: "handshake OK", "bridge done (b->a: Ok(46))" / "Ok(107)"
      — the Unix socket binds and RPC payloads flow. So the break is
      UPSTREAM of the socket: either the remote `winmux port-watch` never
      starts (bootstrap/exec/AcceptEnv for WINMUX_SOCKET_ADDR), or
      port.opened is filtered in rpc_server.rs:1969. What shipped here is
      hardening + diagnostics for that hop, which is worth having on its
      own (its failure mode was a lone log_warn + bare return: no UI
      signal, no doctor field, and SSH panes keep working because they
      use russh channels directly, so it read as "ports are broken").
      NEXT: grep the Mac log for `port-watch[` and `[BOOT]` lines.
FILES:

      //host/share so the UNC-replay rule survives)

  Windows equivalent — every Windows build was manual, which is exactly

  named pipe -> Unix socket in rpc_server.rs, tauri.macos.conf.json, the
  build:frontend script split, the macOS workflow). Merged into main
  (1f5c318). Only PROGRESS.txt conflicted — append vs append, resolved by
  the verified pure-append concat (710 + 685 + 21 = 1416 lines).
  rpc_server.rs merged clean: `git diff origin/main HEAD` on that file is
  empty, so the Unix-socket port came through byte-identical.
  Re-verified after the merge: cargo test 266/0, tsc exit 0, vite ok,
  go 10/10.

ADDED — .github/workflows/ci-windows.yml (tests, ~8 min):
  push + PR to main, plus workflow_dispatch. cargo test --workspace,
  tsc --noEmit, vite build, go test ./.... rust-cache keyed on the
  workspace, npm + go module caches, concurrency group cancels
  superseded runs.
  ORDERING TRAP baked in: `npm run build:linux-cli` must run BEFORE any
  cargo step. It stages src-tauri/resources/winmux-cli.exe, which is
  gitignored, and Tauri's build script hard-fails without it — a fresh
  CI checkout is exactly the FOLLOWUPS P3 case.
  typescript is installed with `npm i --no-save typescript@5` because it
  is still not a project dependency (BACKLOG P2). Step carries a note to
  delete it once package.json carries typescript.

ADDED — .github/workflows/build-windows.yml (bundles, dispatch + v* tags):
  Goes through app/scripts/build-release.ps1 rather than calling
  `npm run tauri build` directly — that wrapper injects the
  --remap-path-prefix RUSTFLAGS that scrub the builder's $HOME out of
  panic-location strings, and deletes the stale app.exe so Cargo cannot
  reuse an unscrubbed incremental artifact. Uploads MSI, NSIS setup, and
  the bare app.exe, with sha256s in the log. Publishing stays manual per
  RELEASING.md step 4.
  Cross-compile note: the bundled Linux CLI is static-musl linked with
  rust-lld (src-tauri/.cargo/config.toml), so a Windows runner needs no
  extra toolchain.

THE GUARD THAT MATTERS: build-windows.yml asserts the frontend is really
  embedded — it greps the freshly built dist/assets/index-<hash>.js
  filename out of app.exe and fails the build if absent. That is exactly
  today's failure; a dev-mode binary can no longer leave CI.
  `localhost:1420` is embedded in BOTH kinds of build (the whole config
  is), so it is useless as a signal and is not used.

VERIFIED (not just YAML-valid):
  - all three workflows parse with yaml.safe_load; every step has
    uses/run; every referenced path and npm script exists.
  - the embed assertion was run locally against the real app.exe:
    passes on 'index-zVEv1mLi.js', and a negative control
    ('index-DEADBEEF.js') correctly reports false — so it discriminates
    rather than always passing.
  - that test caught a real bug in my first draft: [System.IO.File]::
    ReadAllBytes resolves against the PROCESS working directory, not
    PowerShell's location, so the relative path threw
    DirectoryNotFoundException. Now uses Convert-Path.

NOT DONE: neither workflow has run on GitHub yet — nothing is pushed.
  First real run is the actual proof.

--- 2026-08-10 — both Windows workflows verified green on GitHub ---

ci-windows      run 31378991088  success  10m50s
build-windows   run 31379824868  success  15m54s
  artifacts: winmux_0.4.5_x64_en-US.msi (21MB, 8b3f10b8…),
             winmux_0.4.5_x64-setup.exe (15MB, 22c3c168…),
             app.exe (19MB, cf4dbe1e…)
  guard verdict: "OK: production build — 'index-zVEv1mLi.js' is embedded"
  — i.e. the check that would have caught yesterday's dev-mode binary
  actually runs and passes on a real build.

THREE FAILED RUNS BEFORE GREEN, and the middle one is the lesson.

Symptom: build-linux-cli.ps1 died at "Get-FileHash : The term is not
recognized" — a built-in cmdlet — one full minute INTO the musl
cross-build, which had already succeeded.

First fix was wrong. I assumed the runner's PSModulePath omitted the
Windows PowerShell 5.1 module dirs and set it at job level. The run
still failed — but the cheap precondition step I had added alongside it
PASSED, which proved the cmdlet was reachable from a directly-spawned
powershell and the problem lay elsewhere. Instead of guessing a third
time I printed PSModulePath from both paths: identical, and correct.

That made the real cause visible in a line I had been reading past:
  shell: C:\Program Files\PowerShell\7\pwsh.EXE
The default shell for `run:` on Windows runners is pwsh, and pwsh
rewrites PSModulePath for its children to its own module dirs. npm
inherits that and hands it to the Windows PowerShell 5.1 that
build:linux-cli spawns, which can then no longer autoload
Microsoft.PowerShell.Utility.

Fix: `shell: cmd` on the steps that shell out to Windows PowerShell —
in ci-windows.yml directly, and in build-windows.yml where the same 5.1
invocation hides four links down (build-release.ps1 -> npm run tauri
build -> beforeBuildCommand -> build:linux-cli). app/scripts/*.ps1 were
NOT touched; they are correct and work locally. The PSModulePath env is
kept only to make the value explicit, and both files' comments now name
the real cause rather than the guess.

Generalisable: a precondition step that fails in seconds is worth its
line count purely because a passing one narrows the search.

=== 2026-08-10 — CreateWslUser reported success without creating a user ===

TRIGGER: Yossi re-ran the WSL wizard on the repaired WSL. InstallWsl was
  correctly skipped (preflight: platform_ready=true distro_present=true),
  the user/env/tmux steps all reported success, and it failed at
  DeployWinmuxCliToWsl:
    finalize failed: SHA MISMATCH got
    WSL (414 - Relay) ERROR: CreateProcessParseCommon:1014: getpwnam() failed 0
    sha256sum: "/root"/.winmux/bin/...winmux-upload.tmp: No such file
  Note the empty "got" — the hash was never computed. (The message
  reaching the log at all is yesterday's fd1f537 working.)

THE FAILING STEP WAS NOT THE BROKEN ONE. Probing the distro:
    /etc/wsl.conf ->  [user]\ndefault=        <- empty
    uid>=1000 users -> none
    /home/          -> empty
    /root/.winmux/bin/ -> exists, empty
  So `wsl.exe` was calling getpwnam("") on every invocation, failing, and
  silently falling back to root. CreateWslUser had reported success while
  creating nothing.

TWO DEFECTS, both "present != correct", in the CreateWslUser script:
  A. `grep -q '^\[user\]' /etc/wsl.conf` treated the mere presence of the
     section header as proof a default user was set. A section with an
     empty `default=` — exactly the state on this machine — took the
     "already set" branch and left it empty forever.
  B. `else useradd ... && (...) && echo CREATED; fi` — when useradd
     failed, the && chain stopped, but the NEXT statement (the wsl.conf
     `if`) ran and ITS exit status became the script's. A failed useradd
     was swallowed whole. Same class as 6753759 (install reporting
     success on failure), different step.

FIX: `set -e`, plus parse the actual `default=` VALUE rather than the
  section header, plus an explicit verify before returning
  (`getent passwd "$U"` and a non-empty final default, else exit 1).
  An existing non-empty default is respected rather than overwritten.

VERIFIED LIVE, not just compiled: ran the exact generated script against
  Yossi's broken distro as root ->
    CREATED mlastudent371 / wsl.conf default set / VERIFIED
  then `wsl --terminate Ubuntu` (what the step does) and re-probed:
    uid=1000(mlastudent371) groups=...,27(sudo)  HOME=/home/mlastudent371
    getpwnam errors: gone
    /etc/wsl.conf: [user] default=mlastudent371
  His WSL is repaired in place — the wizard can be re-run as-is.

SCANNED for the same swallow pattern in the other generated scripts:
  only this one had it. fin_script (local_setup.rs:1026) looks similar but
  is correct — its `if` is the last statement and the else branch exits 1
  explicitly, so the status does propagate.

cargo test 266/0.

[2026-08-17 IDT] session=macos-local-shell (branch macos-build) — part 5
BUG (Yossi): Hebrew typing in a local mac pane shows `_`.
ROOT CAUSE: Finder-launched app has no LANG/LC_* — zsh ZLE + tmux in a
  non-UTF-8 locale render non-ASCII input as unprintable. Terminal.app
  sets LANG itself; we didn't.
FIX lib.rs spawn_local_pty (unix): if LANG/LC_ALL/LC_CTYPE are all unset,
  inject LANG=<AppleLocale>.UTF-8 when /usr/share/locale has it, else
  en_US.UTF-8 (default_utf8_locale(), probed once). Note: an already-
  running tmux server keeps its old env — `tmux kill-server` once.

[2026-08-17 IDT] session=macos-local-shell (branch macos-build) — part 6
BUG (Yossi): local Insights on mac says Docker socket exists but daemon
  doesn't answer, shows \\.\pipe\docker_engine.
ROOT CAUSE: unix connect_docker used bollard local defaults =
  /var/run/docker.sock only; Docker Desktop on recent macOS defaults the
  admin "default socket" option OFF, so the daemon listens only on
  ~/.docker/run/docker.sock. Card text was Windows-only.
FIX insights_local.rs — probe DOCKER_HOST (unix://), /var/run/docker.sock,
  ~/.docker/run, ~/.orbstack/run, ~/.colima, ~/.rd, podman machine sock;
  first that pings wins; connect_docker returns the answering path and
  the card shows it (both OSes).
=== 2026-08-10 — ROOT CAUSE of the whole WSL saga: wsl_exec lost every shell variable ===

Yossi re-ran the wizard on the repaired distro and CreateWslUser now
failed LOUDLY: "Step 'CreateWslUser' failed (exit 1) — EXISTS " followed
by "could not determine the linux username". That is yesterday's guard
working: $U was empty, and instead of writing garbage the step stopped.

But why was $U empty when `id -nu 1000` returns mlastudent371 by hand?

MEASURED, isolating one construct at a time through the exact transport
std::process::Command uses (no cmd.exe re-parsing in between):
    id -nu 1000                  -> mlastudent371     ok
    echo A[$(id -nu 1000)]B      -> A[mlastudent371]B ok
    X=$(id -nu 1000); echo [$X]  -> []                BROKEN
    X=hello;          echo [$X]  -> []                BROKEN
    echo [$HOME]                 -> [/root]           ok
    echo [$0]                    -> [/bin/bash]       <- the tell

Something between wsl.exe and the distro expands the script text ONCE
before our shell sees it. $HOME survives because it exists in the
expanding context too; $0 comes back as /bin/bash, i.e. that context is a
bash. Any variable the script itself assigns is expanded before it is
ever set, so it reads back empty.

THIS IS THE ORIGIN OF EVERYTHING:
  - `useradd -m -u 1000 -s /bin/bash "$U"` ran with an EMPTY username,
    failed, and the old code swallowed the status -> no user was created,
    while the step reported success.
  - `printf '\n[user]\ndefault=%s\n' "$U"` wrote a literal `default=`
    with no value -> exactly the /etc/wsl.conf we found.
  - WSL then called getpwnam("") on every invocation, failed, and fell
    back to root -> the CLI deploy wrote under one HOME and verified
    under /root -> "SHA MISMATCH got " with an empty hash.
  Three separate symptoms across two days, one line of cause.

FIX: wsl_exec now feeds the script on STDIN (`sh -ls`) instead of passing
  it as an argv element. Proven on the same probe:
    argv  : X=hello; echo [$X] -> []
    stdin : X=hello; echo [$X] -> [hello]
  and the real CreateWslUser script over the new transport returns
    EXISTS mlastudent371 / wsl.conf default already mlastudent371 /
    VERIFIED user=mlastudent371 default=mlastudent371   exit 0

  wsl_pipe_to_file's stage 1 keeps its argv script deliberately — its
  stdin is the file-bytes channel. That script assigns no variables; it
  only uses "$HOME" and $(dirname ...), both of which survive expansion
  with identical results.

NOT A CODE BUG, worth recording: the same run also failed
  InstallLocalHooks with "bundled winmux-cli.exe not found (dev build
  without staged resources?)". That is because the bare app.exe artifact
  was run standalone — the CLI is bundled by the installer, not carried
  by the exe. I had recommended the bare exe as the quick way to test;
  for anything touching setup, the MSI is the artifact to use.

cargo test 266/0. The live proof above is the real verification — this
interaction cannot be unit-tested.

=== 2026-08-10 — reset-local-setup.ps1 (test helper) ===

Yossi: "Smart Install shows everything as already installed - can we have
a script that resets the machine so the steps actually run again?"

Added app/scripts/reset-local-setup.ps1. Two levels:
  Soft (default) - removes only winmux's own artifacts: the winmux hooks
    block in ~/.claude/settings.json + ~/.claude/hooks.json on Windows,
    and ~/.winmux plus the distro's winmux hooks block inside WSL. Leaves
    the distro, the linux user, tmux and /etc/wsl.conf alone.
  Full - Soft plus `wsl --unregister`, the only way to retest InstallWsl
    and CreateWslUser. Deletes the whole linux filesystem, so it asks the
    user to type the distro name back before doing it.
Dry run unless -Apply. settings.json is backed up before any edit.
NEVER touches %APPDATA%\winmux\workspaces.json / settings.json.

Two things the writing of it taught, both recorded in the file:
  - Windows PowerShell 5.1 reads a .ps1 as ANSI when the file has no BOM,
    so the em-dashes in the first draft corrupted and broke the parser
    with errors pointing at unrelated lines. The script is ASCII-only now
    and asserts 0 bytes >127.
  - A shell heredoc nested inside a PowerShell here-string does not
    survive PowerShell's parser; the WSL-side JSON edit is a python3 -c
    one-liner instead.
  It pipes scripts to `sh -ls` on stdin for the same reason wsl_exec now
  does - argv loses shell variables.

DRY RUN ON THE CURRENT MACHINE was itself informative:
    no winmux hooks in C:\Users\...\.claude\settings.json  <- InstallLocalHooks never succeeded
    [plan] delete C:\Users\...\.claude\hooks.json          <- a leftover
    WSL state: NONE | user=mlastudent371 home=/home/mlastudent371
  i.e. ~/.winmux does not exist (the deploy steps have never completed),
  and the user/HOME fix from earlier today is holding.

  So there is nothing meaningful to reset: the wizard reports InstallWsl
  and CreateWslUser as done because they genuinely ARE done. What is
  blocking Yossi is that he is still launching the OLD binary - the
  20:39 log still shows "EXISTS " with an empty name, which is the argv
  transport, i.e. without 9bd44bc. The MSI from run 31413798581 is the
  one to install.

self-sufficient for anything that touches setup. My earlier
recommendation of the bare exe as "the quick way to test" was wrong and
cost several rounds.

ALSO CORRECTED: I claimed there were no parallel instances, reasoning
from boot-count in debug.log. Wrong method — the process list showed BOTH
running at once (target/release/app.exe from Aug 9 and the downloaded
9bd44bc one), sharing one %APPDATA%\winmux: one debug.log, one
workspaces.json, one named pipe. Yossi had said so and I talked past it.
Check the process list, not the log, for that question. WINMUX_CONFIG_DIR
is the right tool for running two builds side by side.

2026-08-11  Browser Dev Mode + Tickets (v2) — branch browser-tickets-v2

FOUND FIRST (this is the headline): the feature was already built and then
  LOST. 13 commits ending at aefbba4 ("Phase 54") implemented tickets +
  dev mode + browser tabs + popout, ~2267 lines. No branch contained them
  — `git branch -a --contains aefbba4` returned empty. They survived only
  as unreachable objects and would have been collected by `git gc`.
  Rescued to branch `browser-dev-mode-tickets` (archive, do not build on).

WHY PHASE 54 NEVER WORKED — the capture went nowhere. Its inspect script
  wrote the captured element to `localStorage.__winmux_ticket__` inside
  the child webview, and NOTHING read it back. One occurrence of that key
  in the whole tree: the write. `workspace_browser_eval` returns
  Result<(), String> (fire-and-forget) and the child webview was built as
  a bare `WebviewBuilder::new(label, External(url))` — no IPC, no
  initialization_script. So there was no channel back at all. The only
  path to the modal was manualCapture(), which opened a ticket with
  selector/xpath/html all EMPTY.

DECISION (Yossi): do NOT restore Phase 54. Don't touch the existing
  browser. Re-do tickets as a clean add-on; salvage only tickets.rs /
  TicketModal / TicketsPanel. Phase 54's browser tabs are superseded by
  main's own (0d4e9a4, landed 2h AFTER aefbba4 the same day).

WHAT SHIPPED (5 commits on browser-tickets-v2, off origin/main 9bd44bc):
  3025f5e  tickets.rs + 4 commands, storage at
           <config_dir>/tickets/<workspace_id>/ticket-*.json
  3f9b36e  winmux-ticket: navigation bridge  <- the gap, closed
  873e060  Dev Mode toggle + right-click capture
  59cdae3  capture modal wired to browser:ticket-captured
  7f7445b  tickets panel on the shared PanelSurface shell

THE BRIDGE (the part that was missing). Tauri 2.10.3's WebviewBuilder has
  no on_ipc — verified against the vendored crate source, not from
  memory. What it does have is on_navigation(Fn(&Url) -> bool). So:
    script:  location.href = "winmux-ticket:<base64url of JSON>"
    rust:    on_navigation -> decode -> emit browser:ticket-captured
                            -> return false (navigation never happens)
  Every other URL returns true, i.e. the exact pre-existing code path.
  ONE line added to the builder; no initialization_script, so ordinary
  pages get zero injection.

  Scheme is used WITHOUT "//" on purpose. Probed both forms:
    winmux-ticket://AAAA  -> host_str=Some("AAAA")  path=""   <- lost
    winmux-ticket:AAAA    -> host_str=None          path="AAAA"
  The handler reads path(), so the authority form would have silently
  dropped every capture. (A first draft of the code comment claimed the
  "//" form corrupts base64 case — probed it, that is FALSE for
  non-special schemes. Comment and test now state the real mechanism.)

NO WORKSPACE SCHEMA CHANGE. Phase 54 added dev_mode + project_path to the
  Workspace struct = workspaces.json migration + 4 initializers +
  provisioning.rs + rpc_server.rs. Dropped entirely:
    dev_mode     -> localStorage, the same pattern BrowserWindow already
                    uses for geometry/port/path/tabs
    project_path -> unnecessary; tickets live in config_dir like
                    settings.json. Also works for remote workspaces whose
                    cwd is on another machine, and kills the SFTP folder
                    picker + the dialog:allow-open it needed.

BLAST RADIUS ON THE EXISTING BROWSER: BrowserWindow.tsx +43/-1, and the
  single deletion is its icons import line. Tabs, port+path, navigation,
  geometry, show/hide untouched. All Dev Mode behaviour lives in the new
  browserDevMode.ts.

right-click (contextmenu), not Phase 54's hover+left-click — left-click
  made the page unusable while Dev Mode was armed.

Selector generation rewritten: walks up until querySelectorAll returns
  exactly one node, adding :nth-of-type on sibling collisions. The
  archive emitted `div.a.b.c` and hoped.

Dropped the archive's export: it opened a save dialog, discarded the
  path, then used a browser download-anchor. Replaced with Copy as
  Markdown (clipboard) + reveal folder via revealItemInDir — both already
  covered by opener:default, no new capability.

VERIFIED: 186 Rust lib tests (14 new: 8 tickets, 6 bridge), tsc clean,
  vite build clean, i18n 1026 keys x 4 locales zero drift.
NOT VERIFIED: nothing has run live. The open risk is whether WebView2
  fires on_navigation for an unregistered scheme — if it does not, the
  fallback is on_web_resource_request against a sentinel URL. Smoke steps
  are in the session report; per CLAUDE.md this stays "compiles,
  untested" until Yossi runs it.

Note for whoever picks this up: the worktree needed
  app/src-tauri/resources/winmux-cli.exe staged before any cargo step
  (gitignored). Copied it from the main checkout rather than rebuilding.

2026-08-11 (later, same session)  CORRECTION: tickets DO belong to a project
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The entry above says "project_path -> unnecessary" and stores everything
app-local. That was wrong and Yossi caught it: "why did you give up on the
folder — how can this be associated with the specific project in a smarter
way?" A ticket not attached to a repo is orphaned metadata; you cannot hand
it to Claude Code in the right project, which is the entire point of
capturing an element.

What I conflated: Phase 54's problem was NOT "associate with a project", it
was WHERE it put the field. `project_path` on the Workspace struct is what
forced the workspaces.json migration + 4 initializers + provisioning.rs +
rpc_server.rs + the SFTP folder picker. Putting it on the TICKET gets the
association with zero schema movement. Both goals were always available.

Fixed in c66703c. resolve_project() ladder, most specific first:
  1. explicit override (localStorage, per workspace)
  2. git_worktree
  3. git root walked UP from cwd   <- the "smarter" part
  4. cwd
  5. nothing -> app-local
Rung 3 matters: a pane usually sits in a subdir, so raw cwd would scatter
tickets across subfolders of one repo. No git-root walker existed —
diff_pane.rs:40 only checks `cwd/.git` without climbing — so it is new.

Phase 54 opened a folder BROWSER for information the app already had. The
workspace carries git_worktree + cwd + connection; asking was the bug.

Write target by connection:
  Local -> <project>/.winmux-tickets/
  Wsl   -> same via \wsl.localhost\<distro>\... when that share resolves;
           there is no path-translation helper in the tree, so this is new
           and degrades to app-local when the share is not reachable
  Ssh   -> app-local (project is on another machine); SFTP write deferred
Every fallback still records project_path, so a ticket is attributable even
when it could not be co-located.

The modal calls tickets_resolve_project BEFORE saving and shows the
destination on every capture — writing into a user's repo is never silent,
and a fallback shows its reason. .gitignore is never touched.

191 Rust tests (5 new: git walk from subdir, worktree .git-as-a-FILE,
not-a-repo, WSL translation declining). tsc + vite clean. i18n 1027 x 4.
Still NOT verified live — same open risk as before (on_navigation).

2026-08-18 — Project folders v4: a project folder IS a workspace
Branch: claude/pane-overflow-project-folders-a7353c
Commits: 8700032, e2d0f17, 804246a, 876d17f, b6f32a4 (+ docs)

WHY: v2/v3 were rejected on sight, twice, for the same reason. Yossi wanted a
SUB-WORKSPACE of the folder, with panes that open directly on it — not a
separate entity pinned beside the workspace. The ProjectFolder entity is gone;
a project folder is a Workspace with a cwd and one new field: parent_id.

SCHEMA (app/src-tauri/crates/winmux-types/src/lib.rs)
  + parent_id, is_project_root, is_collapsed  (all skip_serializing_if)
  - ProjectFolder, project_folder_id, worktree_path
  Workspace now derives Default; the 9 scattered initializers collapse to
  ..Default::default() so the next field is a two-line diff instead of nine.

MIGRATION (lib.rs::migrate_legacy_project_folders)
  v2/v3 pins are converted at load, not dropped. save_to_disk rewrites the
  whole file from the struct, so an unread `project_folders` key would have
  been silently deleted on the first write. Each folder becomes a child
  workspace under the first root on the same host; its worktree workspaces
  re-parent under it keeping their directory as cwd; no host match keeps it as
  a root. 7 tests, one of which caught a real bug: a worktree of the folder
  being migrated could be chosen as that folder's own parent.

CASCADE DELETE (lib.rs::workspace_delete)
  BFS the subtree under one short lock, run teardown_workspace_runtime per id
  with the lock dropped, then ONE retain + reassignment + persist. Active-id
  fallback now prefers a root — `first()` could land the user inside an
  unrelated repo's worktree. The frontend confirm names each descendant and
  how many are live. This reverses DECISIONS.md 2026-08-16 on purpose.

SIDEBAR — one recursive node (renderWorkspaceSubtree) replaces the flat list
  of four sibling kinds. Only roots bucket into groups. Emitted as a FLAT
  fragment: a wrapper carrying the parent's data-ws-id would make a hover over
  a worktree stub paint a drop line on the parent. All new consts sit above the
  component's return (cbef36e). Reorder is parent-scoped; drops are same-level.
  The repo root's own `git worktree list` entry is filtered out — it IS the
  workspace, and a stub for it would offer to open a child sharing its
  parent's directory.

MINI WIZARD — a folder-anchored workspace drops the Directory field and scopes
  the Claude-session list to that folder. Not cosmetic: --resume <id> now
  searches other projects too, so a foreign pick SUCCEEDS in the wrong repo.

TWO PRE-EXISTING BUGS FIXED IN PASSING
  * list_claude_sessions_local never got Phase 65's fix — project_path was the
    ENCODED directory name; invisible only because the caller guards on
    startsWith("/"), which C--Users-... fails by accident.
  * spawn_wsl_pty guards --cd with a Windows-side is_dir() that a Linux path
    always fails, so WSL silently ignored a workspace cwd. effectiveCwdOverride
    now injects the cd for WSL as well as SSH.

RESULT: 316 cargo tests pass, tsc clean, vite builds. NOTHING RUN LIVE — see
FOLLOWUPS P1 2026-08-18 for the 9-step smoke list.

ADDENDUM 2026-08-18 (same branch) — two bugs found by live testing
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. InsightsWindow's Claude-usage effect read state its own fetch writes. On
   success it settled; on FAILURE usage() stayed null and it re-fired forever.
   14,359 [USAGE] backoff lines in 43 min saturated the IPC channel, so
   unrelated clicks stopped landing ("the folder picker opens and nothing
   happens"). Reachable only because the v3->v4 migration produced a workspace
   with an SSH connection and no live session. ClaudeUsageIndicator already
   carried the fix AND the comment describing the trap; its sibling never got
   it. Now on([view, workspaceId]) + plain-let guards.

2. ssh_exec_capture broke on ChannelMsg::Eof, so when EOF preceded ExitStatus
   the exit code was lost and stayed 0. git_script folds stderr in with 2>&1,
   so a failing git came back as SUCCESSFUL output: "fatal: not a git
   repository" parsed to zero worktrees and a non-repo could be PINNED. Whether
   it failed was a race — /home/runner/build probed clean, then scanned dirty
   18 seconds later. Now collects until both EOF and ExitStatus (or Close), and
   git_probe_worktrees additionally rejects an empty listing, since a real repo
   always lists at least its own main worktree.

   Follow-on UX, per Yossi: a non-repo is demoted once (workspace_set_project_root
   -> false) instead of re-asking on every expand and restart, and a confirmed
   repo carries a small git badge on its folder glyph.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-12  Tickets land on the machine the agent runs on (SSH/WSL/Local)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Yossi: "למה תיקיית הכרטיסים היא מקומית ולא על השרת - הרי הסוכן קוד יושב על
השרת". Correct, and the previous entry's design was wrong: an SSH workspace
silently wrote to %APPDATA% and kept the remote path as metadata only. Claude
Code runs in the project dir ON THE SERVER; a ticket it cannot see is not a
ticket.

Six commits: 4f58002, 95c0f31, 76ec575, 12cf043, e0c32d6 (+ this).

THE CORE: a `Store` enum in tickets.rs whose Remote arm CARRIES a live SSH
  handle, so it cannot be constructed without an authenticated session.
  "Can we write there" is answered by the TYPE, not by a later check. That
  deleted the hardcoded `Connection::Ssh => unwritable` gate and, with it,
  three bugs at once.

NO SECOND SSH CONNECTION — this was Yossi's question and it is worth
  recording. `open_auto_forward` (lib.rs:3473), which builds the browser's
  port-forward, resolves its session with `find_ssh_handle_for_workspace`,
  the SAME lookup tickets now use, then opens a `direct-tcpip` CHANNEL on it
  (lib.rs:3500). SSH multiplexes: the forward is one channel, an exec is a
  second, SFTP is a third — one TCP connection, one login. lib.rs:2813 even
  notes clones of the Arc<Handle> may open channels concurrently. So if the
  browser is showing a tunnelled page, the handle to write the ticket is
  already there, and no `workspace_ensure_connected` call is needed (it is
  not a valid predicate anyway: lib.rs:5303 returns Ok(()) for password-auth
  hosts and unknown host keys without connecting).

THE LANDMINE, found by design review and verified against the crate source:
  **SFTP rename CANNOT overwrite.** russh-sftp 2.1.1's `Extensions`
  (client/session.rs:10-20) detects only hardlink/fsync/statvfs/limits — no
  posix-rename — so `SftpSession::rename` is raw SSH_FXP_RENAME, which fails
  when the destination exists. `tickets_update` rewrites an existing
  <id>.json, so a pure-SFTP implementation would have passed every create
  test and failed every update. Remote atomic write is therefore
  `remote_write_bytes(<dst>.<pid>.tmp)` + `mv -f` over an exec channel, with
  the tmp cleaned up if the mv fails so nothing dead is left in the repo.
  `mv -f` is rename(2) => never a partial file; it is NOT durability across a
  server power loss, and the comment says so rather than pretending.

THREE BUGS DIED IN `resolve`:
  1. project_override was applied then discarded for SSH — the escape hatch
     could not rescue the one case it existed for. The ladder (`pick_project`)
     is now pure and runs BEFORE a transport is chosen.
  2. WSL always missed the git rung: `git_root_of` runs on the Windows side
     and a Linux path never exists there, so tickets landed in a SUBDIRECTORY
     of the repo. Now the UNC translation happens BEFORE the walk, and
     project_path (Linux, what the agent sees) is a different variable from
     the store (UNC, what std::fs opens). Conflating them WAS the bug.
  3. tickets_dir_path hard-failed if mkdir failed; revealing a folder is
     navigational and is now best-effort.

LISTING IS ONE EXEC, not readdir + N reads: tens of tickets x 3 SFTP round
  trips at 50ms RTT is a multi-second panel load. `name<TAB><base64>` per
  line. base64 is NOT about screenshots (Yossi asked) — it is framing: ticket
  descriptions are free text that can contain tabs/newlines, `remote_exec`
  merges stderr into stdout (ChannelMsg::ExtendedData), and from_utf8_lossy
  would silently corrupt bytes. base64 makes a stray stderr line fail the
  tab-split and get skipped instead of poisoning a ticket. `tr -d` not
  `base64 -w0`, which is GNU-only and breaks on BSD/macOS remotes.

SCREENSHOT un-stubbed (76ec575) via SVG foreignObject — no library injected,
  which html2canvas would have meant (~200KB of third-party code into the
  user's page per capture). The non-obvious part: a node cloned into
  foreignObject inherits NOTHING from the document's stylesheets, so computed
  styles are inlined by hand or the result is unstyled black text. Everything
  degrades to null (tainted canvas, >400 nodes, >1000x700, >512KB, 1.5s
  timeout) rather than to a wrong ticket; the capture itself is never lost.

NOT CONNECTED => clipboard, not a fallback file. Yossi's call and better than
  what I planned: for an SSH workspace the browser only renders through the
  tunnel, so this is only reachable when a session drops after the page
  loaded. No Save button, no orphan file, no sync engine — Retry + "copy for
  later" so the typed description survives. Removed two fields I had designed
  (allow_local_fallback, stored_local_fallback).

TESTS: 209 lib tests, 22 of them new pure-layer tests written BEFORE any I/O
  existed. The Rule #3 guard feeds every command builder paths with a space,
  quote, `;rm -rf /`, `$(id)`, backticks, `&&`, `|`, newline, glob — and I
  mutation-checked it by removing shell_quote from cmd_mkdir_p, which made it
  fail as intended. A security test that cannot fail is theatre.

PROCESS NOTE worth remembering: shell heredocs silently ate backslashes twice
  — once mangling Rust string literals (`'\r'` became a literal CR inside a
  char literal) and once writing INVALID JSON into all four locale files. The
  JSON case was caught only because the drift check parses every file. Fix
  both times: write the patch as a script FILE and build backslashes with
  chr(92). Do not pipe backslash-bearing content through a heredoc.

STILL NOT VERIFIED LIVE. Nothing in the remote path has touched a real server.
  Smoke list is in docs/DECISIONS.md; the one that matters most is
  tickets_update, because it is the mv -f-over-existing case that SFTP rename
  cannot do — smoke only create and the bug ships.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-13  Dev-Mode tickets: LIVE SMOKE on Windows — the bridge WORKS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Built through the Tauri CLI (Rule #13: verified index-DiQMKBr1.js is embedded in
app.exe), launched it, Yossi drove the browser. Results:

CLOSED — the open risk since this branch started:
  on_navigation DOES fire for the unregistered `winmux-ticket:` scheme in
  WebView2. The capture modal opened with the element filled in (a <pre>, 74
  chars of HTML). The on_web_resource_request fallback is NOT needed. This was
  the gate on everything else in the feature.

FOUND LIVE — two bugs the unit tests could not have caught:
  1. The SSH workspace resolved to "no project folder" and fell into
     %APPDATA%\winmux\tickets\<ws>\ — the exact behaviour this branch exists to
     remove. Root cause: I assumed an SSH workspace carries a `cwd`. It does
     not. workspaces.json had cwd:"" and git_worktree:"" — the user connects and
     `cd`s inside the pane, and nothing writes that back.
     FIX (6612554): when cwd is empty, ask tmux where the pane actually is
     (`tmux display-message -p -t <session> '#{pane_current_path}'`) and start
     the git walk there. Verified against the live host: returns
     /home/runner/club, which is a real git repo. Chain is now
       no cwd -> pane cwd -> git root -> /home/runner/club/.winmux-tickets
  2. The no-project branch hardcoded transport "local", so an SSH workspace was
     described to the UI as local — visible in the screenshot as a bare Windows
     path with no host. Now reports the real transport + host.

VERIFIED against the live server (161.97.93.172) by running the exact command
shapes the Rust builds, over ssh:
  git rev-parse from a SUBDIR   -> /home/runner/wmx-smoke   (the git rung)
  mkdir -p                       -> ok
  mv -f to a fresh name          -> ok (create)
  mv -f OVER AN EXISTING FILE    -> ok, content changed open->resolved (update)
  bulk base64 listing            -> two `name<TAB>b64` lines, decoded fine
  missing directory              -> exit 0, no output
Fixture removed afterwards.

VERIFIED for WSL: new #[ignore]d live test drives the real distro —
/home/<u>/wmx-tk/app/src/components -> UNC -> walk up -> repo root -> back to
/home/<u>/wmx-tk. That is the subdirectory bug proven fixed against reality.

STILL NOT VERIFIED — do not claim these:
  - No ticket has actually been written to the server yet. Checked after the
    smoke: /home/runner/club/.winmux-tickets does not exist. So the russh-sftp
    write, the mv -f through Store::write_atomic_named, tickets_list over the
    remote, and tickets_update remain unexercised end-to-end. The command
    SHAPES are proven; the Rust that issues them is not.
  - Screenshot fidelity. In the smoke the preview rendered as an empty
    checkerboard, i.e. a transparent PNG. A <pre> with no background is a
    plausible reason, but it needs a coloured element to tell "correct" from
    "foreignObject drew nothing".

THREE CORRECTIONS I MADE TO MYSELF THIS SESSION (all were confident and wrong):
  - "the WSL share dies on a cold distro" — no. \wsl.localhost\<d>\home
    resolves even right after `wsl --terminate`. What actually happened: WSL
    wipes /tmp on every distro stop and my test fixture lived there. A vanished
    path is indistinguishable from a dead share. The retry stays (I did observe
    two transient misses) but the comment now states observations, not a
    mechanism I never established.
  - The sftp-CLI rename test proved nothing about russh-sftp: the OpenSSH
    client uses posix-rename@openssh.com and DID overwrite. russh-sftp does not
    negotiate that extension (checked client/session.rs), so `mv -f` is still
    required — but on crate-source evidence, not on that test.
  - Earlier in the branch: "the // URL form corrupts base64 case". It does not;
    the real reason for the opaque form is that the payload lands in host_str()
    and path() comes back empty.

2026-08-18 — Reconnect storm + SFTP bootstrap hardening (worktree follow-up-options-d63140)
TRIGGER: Yossi pasted a full transcript from his second machine — repeated
"sftp write: Timeout" against runner@185.237.252.130, followed by "unplugging
the laptop from AC closed every tmux session on the server".

WHAT I CHANGED

  app/src/i18n/{en,he,ar,ru}.json reconnect.*_multi + cli_skew.banner.*

                                  set_timeout(60), anchored pkill, Skew variant

RESULT: cargo check clean, cargo test 316 pass / 0 fail (6 new), tsc 0,
vite build OK. COMPILES AND TESTS — NOT YET RUN LIVE. The decisive checks
(4 panes surviving one network drop; exactly one SFTP stream on a
hash-mismatched host) both need a real build against a real server.

CORRECTIONS TO THE PREVIOUS SESSION'S CONCLUSIONS (from the pasted transcript)
  - "the server rebooted at 22:21, so this is not a winmux bug" — wrong
    premise. Yossi did that reboot BY HAND because he could not connect. The
    reboot is a consequence of the bug, not an independent cause.
  - "russh-sftp writes 32KB chunks, ~74 round trips" — no. MAX_WRITE_LENGTH is
    261,120. The 522,240-byte leftover on the server is exactly 2 chunks, which
    is what pinned the failure to the third write.
  - "the default timeout is ~15s" — it is 10s (rawsession.rs:183). The measured
    15s was wall-clock for the whole sequence.
  - "compare version instead of hash" — rejected. The desktop speaks the
    protocol of the binary it embeds; a version compare would pass protocol
    skew through silently.
  - "add backoff" — there is no retry loop to back off. The storm is one
    attempt per connect; a negative cache is the matching fix.

STILL OPEN — tmux vanishing server-side
  Yossi confirms `tmux ls` was already empty BEFORE his manual reboot. I could
  not find a mechanism. Ruled out by reading every kill path in the repo: the 4
  tmux kill-session call sites (all behind explicit user action),
  kill_session_inner (explicitly preserves tmux), the bootstrap pkill (tmux's
  cmdline does not contain the pattern), the port-watch reaper (scoped), the
  addons pkill (scoped), and the Go hygiene daemon (hygiene.go killPids only
  permits duplicate port-watchers by PID; orphan claude needs >24h and is never
  auto-killed). No destroy-unattached / exit-empty in the shipped conf.
  Remaining candidates need server evidence, not more code reading — see
  FOLLOWUPS P0 and docs/DECISIONS.md.

TASK: Phase 81.F - sessions get a STABLE name from their first prompt
  Problem with Phase 81 as shipped: the session's display name came from
  `claude_title`, which the stop hook re-reads from the transcript EVERY
  turn. So the name churns as Claude rewrites its own summary, and it
  carries no time anchor - useless as an identity in a picker full of
  winmux-p_<hex> rows.
  New: `auto_name` = "<two words> · <YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM>", derived ONCE from
  the session's first real prompt by the UserPromptSubmit hook. Shown in
  the tmux picker AND pinned on the pane header. claude_title is still
  written every turn; it just moves to the picker row's hover tooltip.

    STOPWORDS + is_stopword; derive_session_name; prompt_can_name;

    arm in handle_hook; stop arm now returns auto_name.or(title);
    MAX_TITLE_CHARS hoisted to module scope; +13 unit tests)

[2026-08-18 ~22:30 IDT] session=hook-pane-resolve (worktree)
TASK: Phase 81.G - the pane-header hook path never worked. Two bugs, found
  by Yossi actually RUNNING 81.F instead of trusting green CI.
  SYMPTOM: 81.F installed, session-meta.json provably correct (auto_name
  present for live sessions), pane header still showed the default name.
BUG 1 (Phase 81's, inherited - NOT introduced by 81.F):
  feed.push identifies the pane by WINMUX_PANE_ID, an env var the shell
  inherited at spawn. Evidence from the live box: `tmux show-environment`
  returns NO per-session value for any of 9 sessions; only the server-global
  one exists (last-connector-wins). A tmux session that outlives its creating
  pane pushes a pane_id the desktop no longer has -> find_workspace_for_pane
  misses -> update_pane_auto_title returned in TOTAL SILENCE. That silence is
  why this survived a month: Phase 81 shipped "compiles + unit-tested, NOT
  live-verified", and claude_title never reached a pane header either.
  FIX: resolve_hook_pane() - trust pane_id only when the layout contains it,
  else reverse-map from the tmux_session name the push already carries (the
  CLI resolves that by ASKING TMUX, exactly because the env var is stale-prone
  - resolve_session_name's own comment says so). Reverse lookup through the
  live pane_sessions/sessions maps, WSL panes included. Silent no-op now logs.
BUG 2 (81.F's, mine): auto_name is written ONCE into a last-writer-wins store.
  The file's own header says a lost read-modify-write "self-heals within one
  turn" - true for claude_title (rewritten every turn), FALSE for a write-once
  field. Observed live: an entry lost auto_name while its own updated_at never
  moved = a concurrent writer saved a stale snapshot.
  FIX: heal on `stop` - rebuild a missing auto_name from the transcript's first
  user message, stamped with THAT ENTRY'S timestamp rather than now(), or every
  heal would mint a new time and the "stable" name would churn.
FILES:
  M app/src-tauri/src/lib.rs (resolve_hook_pane, find_pane_by_tmux_session,
    log_warn on the silent no-op)
  M app/src-tauri/src/rpc_server.rs (both auto_title call sites resolve first)
  M app/src-tauri/cli/src/session_meta.rs (scan_transcript + TranscriptScan,
    recover_session_name, parse_stamp, compose_session_name shared by both

  M docs/DECISIONS.md, FOLLOWUPS.md

  "נעבור התהליך · 2026-08-18 22:28", auto_name is then WIPED on disk to

  "נעבור התהליך · 2026-08-18 20:47" - stamp taken from the transcript entry
