[PANE-OVERFLOW + PROJECT-FOLDERS] two features, plan functional-bouncing-ritchie
   2026-08-13 ~17:00 +0300. Branch claude/pane-overflow-project-folders-a7353c
   (worktree pane-overflow-project-folders-0fd4f0). 5 commits:
   6ee80f6, 568c905, 4138d33, 74472be, + docs.

   FEATURE 1 — pane header overflow (6ee80f6)
   Yossi: with many panes open the header buttons vanish. Cause: .pane-header is
   a no-wrap flex row, .pane-btn can't shrink below its 14px SVG, and
   .pane { overflow:hidden } clipped the overflow silently. Up to 9 buttons x
   ~34px ~= 306px of buttons.
   FIX: buttons moved into an actions() memo; a fitter decides how many render
   inline and the rest fall into a chevron dropdown. `close` stays outside the
   array and is always visible. Badges (tmux T, bg-count, status, agent-run) are
   not actions and never move.
   MEASUREMENT: sum of children offsetWidth + gaps, NOT scrollWidth — an RTL
   header overflows towards the inline start, where scrollWidth is the messy
   corner of the API. Verified in a throwaway Chromium harness served through
   vite: 7 widths x LTR/RTL, identical convergence, 0-8 iterations, no residual
   overflow above ~115px (below that even title+chevron+close can't fit; the
   floor degrades to clipping the title stub, and a pane that narrow is
   unusable anyway).
   fit() runs from rAF only (never inside the effect that writes the signal, or
   it loops), on ResizeObserver ticks and when the action set / a badge changes.
   agentRunLabel is deliberately NOT tracked — ticks every second, fixed width
   via tabular-nums.
   The disconnect split-button stays one atomic item: unchanged inline, expands
   to Detach / Kill session inside the menu. Zero behaviour change, only
   position. Also wired pane.tooltip.split_right/down, which were hardcoded
   English despite the keys existing in all 4 locales.

   FEATURE 2 — project folders + worktrees (568c905, 4138d33, 74472be)
   Yossi's framing: a project folder is a directory on a SERVER he works on
   often; pin it under the workspace in the sidebar like the groups logic, and
   since we already hold an SSH connection, listing its worktrees is the cheap
   way to see "what exists and what doesn't". One action per worktree: open a
   session there.
   MODEL (his call, from AskUserQuestion): Vec<ProjectFolder> on Workspace (not
   a global entity); open-session-here = new pane in the current workspace via
   cwdOverride; scan on expand + manual refresh, no polling; worktree creation
   IS in scope including over SSH.
   - worktrees.rs: parse_worktree_porcelain + transport dispatch on Connection
     (local arg-array / wsl_exec / exec channel on the workspace's LIVE ssh
     handle via pick_ssh_handle). No live handle = explicit "connect a pane
     first", never an empty list — an empty list reads as "no worktrees", a
     different and misleading claim. Rule #3: wsl/ssh are shell strings by
     construction, so every value goes through winmux_core::shell_quote; a test
     asserts a path carrying `'; rm -rf /` stays inert.
     sanitize_branch_name lifted OUT of workspace_create_worktree (was inline)
     so both worktree paths share one rule.
   - Schema: ProjectFolder{id,name,path,is_collapsed}, serde-defaulted,
     skip_serializing_if=Vec::is_empty so an existing workspaces.json
     round-trips byte-identical (test asserts the key is elided). 5 Workspace
     initializers + 3 test literals updated.
   - Commands: workspace_project_folder_add/update/remove (remove only unpins —
     winmux didn't create the directory), project_folder_list_worktrees,
     project_folder_create_worktree.
   - UI: collapsible sub-tree under the workspace row; lazy scan driven by ONE
     effect so every path to "open" is covered (click, freshly pinned, restart
     with it open). Failed scan shows git's own message. Open-session-here finds
     the new pane by diffing pane ids across the split rather than guessing the
     tree shape. ProjectFolderModal serves both dialogs; pinning validates via
     project_folder_list_worktrees first so a bad path fails with git's message
     instead of landing a dead row; the worktree target dir is suggested and
     shown BEFORE confirming. No remote folder picker exists, so ssh/wsl type
     the path and only Local gets the native browse button.

   KNOWN ts-rs 8 WART (docs/DECISIONS.md): it ignores multi-key serde attrs, so
   the generated TS types project_folders as REQUIRED even though the backend
   elides it. types.ts carries the warning; frontend must read
   `w.project_folders ?? []`.

   VALIDATION: cargo test --workspace green (207 + 94, 14 new); tsc clean; vite
   build clean. NOT VERIFIED LIVE — no Tauri build was run, so per Rule #14 this
   is "compiles, untested". Live smoke still owed: see FOLLOWUPS P1.

   WORKTREE ENV (both gitignored, no repo impact): this worktree had no
   node_modules (junctioned to the main checkout) and no
   resources/winmux-cli.exe (copied from the main checkout) — the latter is
   exactly the pre-existing FOLLOWUPS P3.

[PROJECT-FOLDERS v2] worktree = workspace (cmux model) + two overflow bugfixes
   2026-08-16/17 +0300. Branch claude/pane-overflow-project-folders-a7353c.
   Commits: eafd907 (overflow fixes), 94353d6 (backend rework), + this one.

   WHY v2: Yossi built+ran the v1 binary and rejected the model on sight.
   v1 hung ProjectFolder off a Workspace as metadata and "open session here"
   opened a PANE in that same workspace. He wanted a section whose children are
   workspaces. His FIRST message already said "like the groups logic" — that
   was read as "looks like a group" instead of "is shaped like a group".

   CMUX RESEARCH (fetched, not recalled — see DECISIONS 2026-08-16):
   - sidebar has switchable views; one is "Project Worktrees" (cmux#5173)
   - project row's `+` creates the worktree AND opens a workspace (cmux#5032)
   - so a worktree MATERIALIZES AS A WORKSPACE
   - cmux#5032 is a BUG report: they spawned the workspace with the one-shot
     setup command as the main process, so the tab flashed and died when
     `git worktree add` exited. We create the worktree first, then open a
     workspace whose first pane is a plain interactive shell.
   - nesting a workspace's TABS under it (cmux#1404) is requested-not-shipped
     there; not copied.

   SCHEMA
   - ProjectFolder moved off Workspace to WorkspacesFile root, beside `groups`,
     and gained its OWN Connection + sort_order. That is what makes a section
     standalone — pinning a server repo no longer needs a workspace pointing at
     the same host to already exist.
   - Workspace: dropped `project_folders`, gained project_folder_id +
     worktree_path (both skip_serializing_if).
   - Serde tests: the ABANDONED inline project_folders key must still LOAD (a
     branch build wrote it) — pinned so nobody adds deny_unknown_fields later.

   COMMANDS
   - project_folder_add/update/remove + project_folder_open_worktree (new)
     + project_folder_probe (new: validates a path against a connection that
     has no folder yet, for the pin dialog).
   - remove() unpins and NOTHING else: not the directory, not the workspaces
     opened from it. Those keep worktree_path and fall back to the flat list,
     so re-pinning re-adopts them.
   - SSH handle lookup moved from workspace-id to user@host:port. A folder
     belongs to a HOST. key_path is a credential, not an identity → not matched.

   *** BUG FOUND ON THE WAY, WIDER THAN THIS FEATURE ***
   Workspace.cwd is INERT OVER SSH. pane_connect's Ssh arm never forwards cwd to
   spawn_ssh — only the Local and WSL arms use it (lib.rs ~5804-5836). Every SSH
   pane has always started in $HOME and a hand-set workspace cwd was silently
   dropped. An SSH channel has no working-dir parameter; that is exactly what
   cwdOverride papers over via `cd '<dir>'` in build_smart_connect_script.
   connectPane now defaults cwdOverride to ws.cwd for REMOTE workspaces.
   Fixed generally, not narrowed to worktrees — the narrow fix would leave every
   other SSH cwd still broken. Behaviour change for anyone who set a cwd on an
   SSH workspace and got $HOME: they now land where they asked.

   ALSO IN eafd907 (two bugs in the pane-overflow work, both mine, found by
   Yossi testing the v1 build — the chevron appeared but clicking did nothing):
   1. `.pane-header { overflow: hidden }`, which I added FOR the fitter, clipped
      the dropdown (it's position:absolute; top:100% inside the header). The
      fitter never needed it.
   2. The fitter compared the child-width sum against clientWidth = the PADDING
      box, giving the run 20px of free slack, so ~20px stayed overflowing at
      every width. Bug 1's clipping is exactly what hid bug 2.
   Re-verified in Chromium against the real CSS, 7 widths x LTR/RTL: residual
   overflow 0px (was ~20px), dropdown measures visible everywhere it exists.
   Also added createLogger("SIDEBAR") — there was NO logger in Sidebar.tsx, so a
   failed worktree scan left nothing in debug.log and Yossi's "לא מציג" was
   undiagnosable. Scans now log start/ok+count/fail (metadata only).

   VALIDATION: cargo test --workspace green (207 + 97); tsc clean; vite build
   clean; tauri build --no-bundle + Rule #13 asset-hash check + Rule #14 launch.
   STILL NOT SMOKED LIVE against a real SSH repo — FOLLOWUPS P1 stands.

[SIDEBAR TDZ] the whole sidebar was an ErrorBoundary card
   2026-08-17 ~12:20 +0300. Commits cbef36e (fix), 9da0483 (log guard).
   Yossi's report was one word: "הSIDEBAR".
   CAUSE: the project-folder block (scans / pathKey / sortedFolders /
   byWorktreePath / renderProjectFolderSections) was placed AFTER Sidebar's
   `return (` statement. The `const`s therefore never executed and every read
   hit the temporal dead zone -> "Cannot access 'sortedFolders' before
   initialization" -> ErrorBoundary replaced the entire sidebar.
   WHAT HID IT: renderWorkspaceItem / renderWorkspaceRow live in that same
   after-return region and work fine, because FUNCTION DECLARATIONS HOIST and
   const does not. Putting new state beside them looked consistent and wasn't.
   tsc does not flag TDZ.
   ALSO EXPLAINS the previous round's "#3 לא מציג": there the crash was
   CONDITIONAL — renderProjectFolders short-circuited on
   <Show when={folders().length > 0}> before touching scans(), so the sidebar
   survived until a folder was actually pinned, then died exactly where the
   list should have appeared. In v2 the call is unconditional -> dead at boot.

   PROCESS FAILURES WORTH RECORDING (mine):
   1. I reported a build as "ready" that had FAILED. `npm run tauri build`
      returned exit code 0 while the output contained
      "Access is denied. (os error 5)" — a running app.exe blocked the link.
      Exit code is not a build result here; the output text is. Check both.
   2. cargo test + tsc + vite build + the Rule #13 asset-hash check + "process
      still alive after 14s" ALL PASSED on a binary whose sidebar was entirely
      replaced by an error card. "The process didn't die" is not "the UI works"
      — that is Rule #14's actual point and I treated the launch as if it
      satisfied it.
   3. A sidebar render crash wrote NOTHING to debug.log, so the on-screen card
      was the only evidence and nothing survived to diagnose afterwards. Fixed
      in 9da0483: the ErrorBoundary fallback now calls log.error. NOTE the new
      guard's FIRING path is itself untested — its absence from the log after a
      clean boot is consistent with the sidebar rendering, but was not proven by
      deliberately breaking it.

TASK
  Yossi asked to (a) replace WSL with Zellij as the Windows tmux, and (b) fix
  RTL "by taking inspiration from RTLTERMINAL, or reordering the BiDi so it
  prints reversed". Investigation changed both framings.

FINDING 1 — RTL was not missing, it regressed.
  Both proposed approaches already exist in the tree:
    - "reorder the BiDi"   = rtl_mode "bidi_reorder"  (app/src/bidi.ts, bidi-js)
    - RTLTERMINAL's trick  = rtl_mode "auto_per_line" (DOM renderer + per-row
      dir), in a smarter block-aware form (textDirection.ts protects tables and
      box-drawing; ar-terminal has a 30ms unshaped flash and mismatched
      selection, we have neither).
  The real cause of the reversed Hebrew: tuiOwnsBidi was silently dropped by
  merge bcaa330 (2026-07-31). Verified three ways —
    456f0ed ("round-10 diagnosis — TUI-owns-bidi restored, saga closed"): 9 hits
    45b6592 (other parent):                                                0 hits
    bcaa330 (merge result) and main ever since:                            0 hits
  The merge also deleted nextTuiOwnsBidi from textDirection.ts (27 lines) AND
  its 10 unit tests — which is why the suite stayed green through the loss.
  docs/DECISIONS.md still claimed "State: Open — feature IN on
  claude/integration". False since 2026-07-31.

FINDING 2 — collateral from the SAME merge.
  Phase 79's createLogger conversion in terminalInstance.ts was thrown away too.
  Logger sites / console.* per ref: 456f0ed 24/0, 45b6592 0/17, bcaa330 0/17,

  That would have destroyed readClipboardText() and the WebView2 paste fix
  (82b6c0c), which landed after. 7 hunks applied by hand, bottom-up. Only
  substantive change vs the original: log.info -> termLog.info, because `log`
  does not exist at HEAD (see FINDING 2) — copying verbatim is a compile error.

  plain "" literal, so \v was a vertical tab and \W \S \p were dropped; it
  asserted against a mangled string. Now String.raw.

NOT DONE, deliberately
  No set-titles in the tmux conf. The conf has none (zero hits repo-wide) and
  tmux defaults it off, so onTitleChange should never fire inside tmux — yet
  DECISIONS records it engaging on a live SSH pane. Instrument before designing
  around an unexplained observation: the restored termLog.info line now puts
  the answer in debug.log. TRAP if someone adds it later: tmux's default
  set-titles-string always contains the session name, so it is never empty,
  nextTuiOwnsBidi falls through to `return prev`, and the feature latches ON
  forever — strictly worse than the bug. Must be the pair `set -g set-titles on`
  + `set -g set-titles-string "#T"`, in build_tmux_attach_script (lib.rs), not
  the conf (the conf is skipped when use_winmux_tmux_config is off).

RESULT
  47/47 unit tests pass. tsc --noEmit clean. vite build clean (only the known
  1.4MB chunk-size warning, DECISIONS.md:97).
  COMPILES, UNTESTED LIVE — per CLAUDE.md that is not "verified". The smoke is
  docs/RTL-TEST.md "TUI-owns-bidi smoke", 8 steps; step 5 (two full cycles in
  one pane) and step 7 (inside tmux over SSH) are the ones that matter.

ZELLIJ (Part B) — no code written, spike gates everything.
  My first assumption was wrong: Zellij 0.44.0 (2026-03-23) DOES run natively
  on Windows, no WSL. And Yossi's goal is better founded than it sounds —
  LOCAL_CAPS.tmuxPersistence=false (types.ts:352) is the only gap; native Claude
  Code (local_setup.rs:1220) and winmux-cli.exe already exist, so persistence is
  the last thing WSL is needed for. Zellij also serialises sessions to disk,
  which survives a reboot — tmux does not.
  BUT the port is 5 months old and the open issues hit exactly what we'd buy:
  #5365 keyboard input dies after detach/reattach (the one feature), #5348
  "does not run properly on Windows shells", #5451 a pane renders never in a
  multi-tab layout, #5360 Ctrl+Alt dead. Manual spike outside the repo first;
  if detach/reattach or layout rendering fails, the idea is dead until upstream
  moves. Logged as an Open decision.

2026-08-19 — TUI-owns-bidi turned OFF by default: the premise expired
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Live test of v0.4.5 (the restore) produced the opposite of the expected result.
  Yossi: local pane broken, SSH fine. debug.log had exactly ONE pane with
  transitions:
    23:44:16 [UI:TERM] tui-owns-bidi on  pane=p_18ab17f9e2257cd8_1
    23:50:01 [UI:TERM] tui-owns-bidi off pane=p_18ab17f9e2257cd8_1
  The feature engaged exactly where Hebrew broke, and never engaged where Hebrew
  worked. SSH panes are spared only because tmux swallows the OSC title the
  detector needs (no set-titles in the bundled conf).

  Diagnosis: Claude changed. The feature was verified in 2026-07 against Claude
  2.1.74-2.1.210, which printed Hebrew ALREADY reversed for BiDi-less terminals.
  claude-fable-5 prints logical order like everything else, so the ordinary
  per-line RTL pass is correct and forcing dir="ltr" is what scrambles it.
  MY RESTORE MADE LOCAL PANES WORSE. Said plainly because the DECISIONS entry
  it was based on is what made it look safe.

  Fix: setting terminal.tui_owns_bidi, DEFAULT FALSE, Settings -> Terminal
  toggle, en/he/ar/ru. Not deleted — deleting it silently is how this loop
  started. Detection + the tui-owns-bidi log line still run unconditionally;
  only the LTR forcing is gated, via `bidiOwnedByTui` in terminalInstance.ts.
  That log line is the only reason this took minutes instead of days.

  FILES  settings.rs (field + Default), terminalInstance.ts (g_tuiOwnsBidiEnabled,
         setTuiOwnsBidiEnabled, bidiOwnedByTui getter, refreshRowDirections,
         3 effect sites regated), App.tsx (2 wiring sites), SettingsModal.tsx,
         settings.ts, bindings/TerminalSettings.ts (ts-rs confirmed my
         hand-written binding byte-for-byte), i18n x4, DECISIONS, PROGRESS.

  A SECOND BUG FOUND, NOT FIXED: dir="ltr" would not have worked even for the
  old visual-order Claude. `dir` sets only the paragraph base direction; UAX #9
  rule L2 still reverses Hebrew runs inside an LTR paragraph. Suppressing
  reordering needs `unicode-bidi: bidi-override`, which appears nowhere near
  .xterm-rows (checked: every unicode-bidi in App.css targets other surfaces).
  So the legacy path is likely broken for the Claude it was written for too.
  Left alone deliberately — it is behind a default-off flag now, and fixing an
  unverifiable path against a Claude version nobody here runs is speculation.
  Anyone enabling the flag should add bidi-override first.

  PROCESS: I confirmed the original symptom with a multiple-choice question
  whose first option was the answer I expected, and built a restore on it. The
  discriminating question — what breaks WHERE — took one sentence from Yossi to
  answer and would have redirected the whole session. Ask for the observation
  that splits hypotheses, not the one that confirms yours.

  CHECKS  247 cargo tests pass. 47/47 textDirection tests. tsc --noEmit clean.
  NOT VERIFIED LIVE — needs a local pane with Hebrew in Claude on the new build.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-19 — MEASURED what Claude emits, and fixed the neutrals bug in bidi.ts
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  THE MEASUREMENT that ended the guessing: Yossi set rtl_mode="off" (WebGL, no
  browser bidi, no reorder — the screen shows the PTY stream verbatim) and
  Hebrew came out REVERSED. So Claude Code emits Hebrew in LOGICAL order.
  Two consequences, both certain:
    - tui_owns_bidi=false (shipped in ac71bfb) is correct. Forcing LTR is wrong.
    - The `unicode-bidi: bidi-override` idea I floated is DEAD. It would render
      exactly what "off" rendered: reversed. Good thing it was never built.
  Rule #1 forbids logging PTY content, so this could only ever be measured by a
  human looking at the screen. Asking for it was worth more than any code I
  wrote today.

  WORKING CONFIG (Yossi's, after testing all three): rtl_mode=bidi_reorder
  (Legacy) + auto_direction on + mirror_arrows_rtl on + tui_owns_bidi off.

  BUG FIXED  app/src/bidi.ts:11 hard-coded the paragraph base direction:
      bidi.getEmbeddingLevels(line, "ltr")
  So neutrals (?, !!!, .) at the end of a Hebrew sentence resolved against an
  LTR paragraph and rendered at the RIGHT edge — the START of the sentence.
  Yossi: "characters show at the beginning of the line instead of the end".

  FIRST ATTEMPT WAS WRONG and the tests caught it: hard-coding "rtl" fixes the
  punctuation but mirrors Claude's status line, which is mostly Latin with one
  Hebrew word — the same layout flip auto_per_line produces. Replaced with
  bidi-js's own UAX #9 P2/P3 resolution (first strong character wins), correct
  for all three shapes: pure-Hebrew sentence -> rtl; "> " + Hebrew (neutral
  first) -> rtl; Latin-first status line -> ltr.

  NEW  app/src/bidi.test.ts — 11 cases, the file had none. Includes a written
  orientation note, because I got the first two assertions backwards: the
  output is VISUAL order painted L-to-R, so index 0 is the LEFTMOST glyph,
  which in an RTL sentence is its END. Sentence-final punctuation belongs at
  index 0. One test specifically pins the status line against mirroring.

  STILL OPEN, not fixed, genuinely hard: the caret pinned to the right and not
  tracking the text, and partial-repaint glitches ("part rewritten correctly
  and part in the middle rewritten again"). Both are inherent to transforming
  the byte stream for a TUI that repaints regions and positions the cursor in
  columns of the ORIGINAL text — two coordinate systems that diverge. This is
  what terminal-wg calls "the cursed cursor"; no xterm.js-based terminal has
  solved it. A real fix means reassembling whole visual lines before reordering
  and tracking cursor position through the transform. Logged in DECISIONS.

  CHECKS  11/11 bidi tests, 47/47 textDirection, tsc clean, vite clean.
  NOT VERIFIED LIVE.


2026-08-18 — winmux -> YMUX: full repo rename + compatibility layers
Branch: claude/app-rename-ymux-f8404e (worktree app-rename-ymux-f8404e)

TASK
Yossi renamed the app to YMUX ("it isn't limited to Windows any more") and
asked for the whole repo to follow. Two scope questions were put to him
before any sed ran, because the answers change the work materially:
  - depth   -> "full rename + compatibility layers" (over clean-cut / branding-only)
  - history -> "leave history, update live docs"
Both decisions and their reasoning are in docs/DECISIONS.md (Decided,
2026-08-18). This entry is the mechanical record.

BASELINE
cargo check --workspace --all-targets was run BEFORE touching anything and
exited 0, so every error after this point is attributable to the rename.

WHAT MOVED
  git mv: 8 crates (crates/winmux-* -> ymux-*), resources/winmux-{linux-x64,
    server-linux-x64, server-linux-arm64, tmux.conf}, server/cmd/winmux-server,
    docs/winmux-server/, winmux-tools/, sdk/kotlin .../dev/winmux/ (+ tests),
    WinmuxClient.kt -> YmuxClient.kt, app/src/WinmuxToolsTab.tsx.
  sed over 226 text files: WINMUX->YMUX, WinMux->YMUX, Winmux->Ymux,
    winMux->yMux, winmux->ymux.
  Excluded from sed, deliberately: PROGRESS.txt, docs/DECISIONS.md,
    docs/COMPETITIVE-SCAN.md, .claude/worktree-audit-2026-08-03.txt,
    manifest.json, Cargo.lock, package-lock.json, remote-manifest.json,
    and the three binaries (renamed + rebaked instead).

  GOTCHA, caught immediately: the sweep rewrote the worktree's own `.git`
  FILE (it holds "gitdir: .../programing/winmux/.git/worktrees/..."), which
  detached the worktree from the repo. Restored by hand in the next command.
  Any future repo-wide sed must exclude `.git` explicitly -- the usual
  -not -path './.git/*' does NOT match the file itself.

CASING
  ymux = machine-facing (paths, crate names, binaries, env-var stems,
         productName, bundle identifier)
  YMUX = human-facing (window title, tray menu + tooltip, index.html
         <title>, i18n VALUES, README)
  The i18n pass rewrites JSON values only, and skips any `ymux` glued to
  [~/.`-] or followed by [/-] -- so `~/.ymux/tmux.conf`, `ymux-cli` and the
  keys use_ymux_tmux_config / DeployYmuxCliToWsl / add_ymux_to_path all
  stayed lowercase. 69 values brand-cased across en/he/ar/ru; all four
  re-parsed as JSON afterwards.

COMPAT LAYERS ADDED (each guards a specific upgrade path)
  crates/ymux-core         config_dir(): renames %APPDATA%\winmux -> ymux
                           once, behind a Once -- the log_* calls re-enter
                           config_dir(), so without the latch a FAILING
                           rename recurses forever. WINMUX_CONFIG_DIR still
                           honoured. pipe_name_legacy() added.
  crates/ymux-bootstrap    migrate_legacy_remote_dir(): merges ~/.winmux
                           into ~/.ymux over SSH BEFORE the hash check, so
                           an unchanged CLI isn't needlessly re-uploaded.
                           cp -an, not mv -- both dirs can exist and
                           `mv A B` would nest A inside B. Drops the stale
                           winmux-* binaries the copy drags along.
  cli/src/main.rs          adopt_legacy_env(): promotes every WINMUX_* var
                           to YMUX_* once at startup, before any thread
                           exists. One function instead of 13 per-call-site
                           fallbacks -- and a pane outlives the app that
                           opened it, so a long-lived tmux session still
                           carries whatever set-environment -g put there.
                           migrate_legacy_home_dir(): local twin of the
                           bootstrap migration, for hosts it never touches.
                           Handshake + pipe connect: read/mirror either
                           dialect; legacy pipe tried as a fallback.
  ymux-tunnel + hookrpc    Handshake: accept YMUX-* or WINMUX-*, answer in
                           the dialect we were spoken to.
  lib.rs, tunnel, spawnEnv Dual-WRITE of the env trio (pane id, socket
                           addr, tunnel token) in all three injectors.
  settings.rs              #[serde(alias = "use_winmux_tmux_config")] --
                           without it a user who had turned the toggle OFF
                           silently gets it back on after upgrading.
  cli/src/hooks.rs         ${WINMUX_BIN} still expanded; stale winmux_meta
                           dropped when the new stamp is written.
  addons.rs                Insights detect / uninstall / install now also
                           see winmux-server + winmux-insights (unit,
                           binary, symlink, old home).
  cmd/ymux-server/main.go  Data-dir migration chain extended with
                           ~/.winmux/{server,insights} -- otherwise every
                           paired phone would have been silently unpaired.
  files/huma.go + client.ts  X-Winmux-Truncated sent + read.
  rpc_server.rs            Second listener pool on the legacy pipe name
                           (2 slots -- compat path, not the hot route).

WHY THE WIRE TAG DID NOT FLIP
A pre-rename winmux-linux-x64 does a literal strip_prefix("WINMUX-CHALLENGE ")
and hangs up on anything else. Emitting YMUX-CHALLENGE would break exactly
the already-provisioned remotes that the chosen option was picked to
protect. So both ends learned to read AND mirror either dialect, and the tag
we EMIT stays legacy for one release. The flip is one line per side and is
tracked as FOLLOWUPS P1.

TWO REAL BUGS THE RENAME SURFACED (neither is a compile error)
  1. cli/src/hooks.rs is_ymux_entry() matched the substring "ymux" -- and
     "winmux" DOES NOT CONTAIN "ymux". Left alone, setup-hooks would have
     kept every pre-rename entry and pushed a second one beside it: two
     permission cards per tool call, on every machine that ran the old build.
  2. addons.rs hooks_uninstall_script's Python filter had the identical
     flaw -- uninstall would have printed "removed" and removed nothing.
  Both now match either spelling.

VERSION BUMPS (lockstep, per the 2026-07-15 rebake precedent)
  core.Version + INSIGHTS_VERSION     2.1.4 -> 2.2.0
  ymux_hooks_version + BUNDLED_...    1.3.0 -> 1.4.0

BINARIES REBAKED (not just renamed)
  ymux-linux-x64            musl, via npm run build:linux-cli
  ymux-server-linux-x64     go build GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64
  ymux-server-linux-arm64   go build GOOS=linux GOARCH=arm64
  grep -a confirms the only winmux strings left are the deliberate compat
  literals. remote-manifest.json regenerated with the new sha256.

RESULT
  cargo check --workspace --all-targets   PASS
  cargo test --workspace                  PASS
  go vet ./... + go test ./...            PASS (all packages)
  tsc --noEmit                            PASS
  vite build                              PASS
  New tests: TestHookHandshakeAcceptsBothDialects,
             TestHookHandshakeDeniesInClientDialect

NOT VERIFIED LIVE -- Rule #14. The app was never launched. Every compat
layer above only executes on an UPGRADE, which no test in this repo can
reach. The ordered smoke list is FOLLOWUPS P1 (2026-08-18).

STILL OWED BEFORE RELEASE (FOLLOWUPS P0 x2)
  - Rename the GitHub repo to ymux. Every URL in the tree already says
    yyhezkel/ymux and 404s until then; rename FIRST, the redirect covers
    the rest.
  - Cut as 0.5.0, not 0.4.6: com.winmux.app -> com.ymux.app is the MSI/NSIS
    upgrade key, so the new package installs SIDE BY SIDE and the old one
    stays in Programs and Features. No shim exists for this -- the
    identifier IS the identity. Release notes need an uninstall step.

[2026-08-19] Diagnosis: "strange sudden disconnects" (log forensics only, no code changed)
  Source read: %APPDATA%\ymux\debug.log (10,093 lines), debug.flooded-2026-08-18.log, log-sync.json
  Files implicated: app/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:6253 + :3028 + :3544,
                    app/src-tauri/crates/ymux-tunnel/src/lib.rs:279 (write_remote_env_file),
                    app/src-tauri/cli/src/main.rs:1223,
                    app/src-tauri/src/claude_usage.rs:262
  Result: ROOT CAUSE IDENTIFIED, not fixed, not verified live.
    SSH itself never fails (20/20 connects ok=true). The reverse tunnel re-allocates a
    remote port 20 times but rewrites the remote ~/.winmux/run/last.env only 5 times, so
    the remote CLI hook dials dead ports -> 23 x "Connection refused (os error 111)" ->
    BRANCH=rpc-error-fallback -> Claude Code's own UI replaces ymux's permission card.
    That is what the user perceives as a disconnect. The headless workspace_ensure_connected
    path allocates port+token+watcher, then drops its handle without rolling any of them
    back; the clobbered token explains the 2 x "server closed before challenge".
    Race confirmed by two forwards 38ms apart + "stale slot" + 4 server-side
    "hygiene auto-reaped duplicate port-watchers".
  Also found (unrelated, filed P2): non-atomic log writes tearing ~120 lines;
    claude_usage backoff logging 15,477 identical lines.
  Filed: FOLLOWUPS.md — 1 x P0, 2 x P2. No fix applied — fix shape needs Yossi's decision.

[2026-08-19] Phase 80: sudden disconnects + a single owner for debug.log
  Branch: claude/strange-disconnections-logs-2c6a49 (worktree)
  Commits: 80.A TunnelRegistry / 80.B sticky port + connect lock / 80.C CLI retry /
           80.D log writer
  New files: app/src-tauri/src/tunnel_registry.rs
             app/src-tauri/crates/ymux-core/src/log_writer.rs
  Touched:   src/lib.rs, src/log_sync.rs, src/rpc_server.rs, src/tray.rs,
             src/updater.rs, crates/ymux-core/src/lib.rs,
             crates/ymux-tunnel/src/lib.rs, cli/src/main.rs

  DIAGNOSIS CORRECTION worth remembering: the first pass blamed the 20-vs-5 gap
  between `tcpip_forward got remote port` and `wrote ... last.env`. That was
  wrong. last.env was written CORRECTLY at 19:56:27 (44495) and the same pane's
  hook still dialed 34173 four seconds later — the port from 73 minutes earlier.
  A long-lived claude inherits YMUX_SOCKET_ADDR at spawn, its hooks inherit it,
  and nothing can rewrite a running process's environment; cli/src/main.rs:1026
  then skips last.env precisely because the variable IS set. The port has to
  stop changing, hence the sticky port. The missing env writes were a real but
  separate correctness bug, fixed alongside.

  Result: cargo test --workspace green (274 app / 39 ymux-core / 20 cli /
  6 ymux-tunnel), tsc --noEmit clean, release exe built through the Tauri CLI.
  18 new tunnel tests + 7 CLI env tests + 6 writer tests + a 16-thread x 500-line
  no-torn-lines regression.

  TWO TRAPS HIT AND WORTH KNOWING:
   1. russh tcpip_forward returns Ok(0) for a NONZERO port request ("returns 0
      otherwise" in its docs). Reading the return value as the granted port
      silently produces a dead tunnel with no error. request_reverse_forward()
      is the single place that knows this.
   2. Naming AppState::default() from a #[cfg(test)] module drags tauri's webview
      runtime into the test binary, which then dies with 0xc0000139
      STATUS_ENTRYPOINT_NOT_FOUND before any test runs. Build CoreState +
      TunnelRegistry directly instead. Cost ~30 min to find; the comment on
      tunnel_lease_tests::fixture() now says so.

  STILL UNVERIFIED (Rule #14): no live SSH run. Sticky-port grant/denial against
  a real sshd is the behaviour no test here covers. Repro C in FOLLOWUPS is the
  smoke test that settles it.

2026-08-19 -- Sidebar design pass (branch claude/sidebar-design-1985e3)
Yossi pointed at two screenshots of the rail in Hebrew/RTL light mode: it read
as confetti, not as a list. Root causes found, in order of blame:

  1. .ws-item[data-has-color] painted the workspace colour THREE times -- the
     dot, a 3px inline-start rule, and a 12% row tint. At 12% over dark that
     is a hint; over light mode's #ffffff surface every row became a different
     pastel bar. Deleted the strip and the tint; the dot alone carries colour
     now (Design Pass 01 P3 -- colour is meaning, not decoration).
  2. No meta column. .ws-name{flex:1} pushed each marker to wherever flex left
     room, so the pane-count badge landed at a different offset on every row.
     Added .ws-meta, flush to the inline end, fixed order
     ports -> waiting -> live -> badge. Badge LAST (the only always-present
     one) + min-width:1.7em, which is what actually makes it a column.
  3. .has-waiting::after was position:absolute at inset-inline-end:10px and
     painted OVER the badges. Now a real .ws-waiting-dot element in the
     cluster. Class stays on the row -- themes-redesign.css and the pulse
     key off it.
  4. Narrow-drag `full` mode was undesigned: names ellipsized at the wrong
     side and there was no tooltip at all (title was set only in icons mode).

FILES

2026-08-19 (later) -- Sidebar follow-up: name alignment regression
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Yossi ran the merged build and sent a screenshot. The confetti was gone and the
badge column was straight, but every LATIN name had drifted off the start edge:
`Local`, `WSL`, `boost`, `server1` hung in the middle of the row with a gap
between the dot and the name, while `שרת 9` stayed flush right.

CAUSE, mine: `unicode-bidi: plaintext` + `text-align: start` on .ws-name. Under
plaintext, `start` is resolved against the PARAGRAPH's per-string base
direction, not the container's -- so in a Hebrew rail a latin name resolves LTR
and `start` flips to the left. Fixing the ellipsis side had silently taken the
alignment with it.

FIX: keep plaintext (it is what puts the ellipsis on the right end), pin the
alignment explicitly to the rail:
  [dir="rtl"] .ws-name { text-align: right; }
  [dir="ltr"] .ws-name { text-align: left; }

MEASURED, A/B in the fixture (distance from the box's start edge to the whole
text run, 0 = flush):
  before   13 distinct gaps, 0-123px   <- the ragged gutter in the screenshot
  after    {0} in BOTH the RTL and the LTR rail
  ellipsis still per-string: clipped latin names keep firstGlyphFromLeft:0,
           i.e. the tail is cut, not the head
  row height {26} and badge column {8} unchanged

Also caught while re-reading the screenshot: `2- SERVER` renders as `2- SERVER`,
not mangled. The leading-digit names were fine; alignment was the only defect.

My first measurement of this was WRONG and said the fix had only half worked --
it measured the FIRST GLYPH, which for a right-aligned latin string sits at the
string's left end, so the "gap" it reported was just the name's width. Measuring
the whole run's near edge is the correct probe. Noted because the same mistake
is easy to repeat on any RTL alignment check.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-19 (later still) -- Sidebar masthead + collapse control
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Yossi confirmed the alignment fix live, then asked for two things: centre the
logo and let it fill / scale with the row, and sort out the navbar's
open/close button.

WHAT WAS THERE: .sidebar-header stacked two rows -- the brand row, then the
collapse arrow on a line of its own inside a 1px bordered box. That spent ~19px
of permanent vertical space on a control used about once a day, and the border
made a rare toggle read as a primary action. The brand was also pinned at 22px
logo + --w-fs-lg regardless of how wide the rail was dragged.

WHAT IT IS NOW: one row, a three-column grid -- [collapse] [brand] [badge].
Both side slots are the SAME fixed 26px, which is what keeps the brand
optically centred whether or not the badge is rendering; a flex row with
`margin: auto` drifts the moment one side appears. Grid tracks follow the
writing direction, so the arrow lands on the rail's OUTER edge in LTR and RTL
alike, pointing the way it collapses.

The masthead scales off --sidebar-w, the live width Sidebar.tsx now publishes
inline (the same widthPx already threaded for [data-narrow]). Everything in the
lockup is em-based off one clamped root, so the mark tracks the wordmark
instead of staying pinned:
  --sb-brand: clamp(15px, calc(var(--sidebar-w) * 0.115), 32px)

The collapse button lost its border. Quiet --w-text-faint at rest, surfaces on
hover; the global :focus-visible ring keeps it reachable.

MEASURED in the fixture (now 7 views, added a 420px wide rail to see the top of
the clamp):
  brand      19px @165  ->  26px @224  ->  32px @420 (clamp ceiling)
  logo       em-locked at 1.15em, so 21.8 -> 29.6 -> 36.8
  centring   centreOffset {0} in ALL seven views, badge present
  header     ~74px -> 49-63px in full; ~19px handed back to the list
  overflow   0 -- nothing spills the rail at 165px

REGRESSION CAUGHT BY THE MEASUREMENT, fixed before commit: moving the notif
badge out of .sidebar-brand-row gave it a row of its own in the 48px ICONS
strip, pushing that header from 74px to 99px. It rides the logo's corner as an
absolutely-positioned count bubble there instead -- which is what a narrow rail
wants anyway. Back to 75px.

Deliberately NOT changed: the wordmark's blue->teal gradient. It is existing
brand identity, not a call to make while fixing layout -- and themes-redesign
already neutralises it for the four design directions.

NOT VERIFIED LIVE (Rule #14) -- fixture + tsc + vite build only.

2026-08-19 (cont.) -- Masthead becomes the control; the notif badge dies
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Yossi: "why is there a notifications button on the right when nobody expects it
there?" He was right, and it was worse than the placement:

  - .sidebar-notif-badge was a <span>. No onClick, anywhere. Styled as an amber
    pill with a glow, so it read as a control and did nothing when clicked.
  - There was nothing to click TO. showNotifCenter was folded out (App.tsx:161).
  - The three-column grid I had just built made it WORSE: a fixed slot mirroring
    the collapse button implies a matched pair. One was a button, one was dead.
  - And my own ASCII diagram misled him -- I drew LOGICAL order and labelled it
    RTL. Under dir=rtl grid col 1 is on the RIGHT, so the arrow was right and
    the badge left, the opposite of what the diagram showed.

What it counted: paneNotified().size, panes with a pending OSC 9/99/777. That
signal already renders on the pane itself (LayoutView.tsx:171 isNotified), but
Sidebar never received it -- so for a workspace you were NOT looking at, an
undifferentiated number in the masthead was the only surface, with no way to
tell which workspace wanted you.

DECIDED (Yossi): fold it into the row's existing attention dot, then he went
further -- kill the collapse button too, one-column grid, DOUBLE-CLICK the logo
to collapse. "0 instead of another button."

  - .sidebar-header in full mode: one column. The wordmark spans the entire
    content width -- measured rowSpansHeader 207/207 at 224px, 148/148 at 165,
    403/403 at 420. That is the original "fill the row" ask, literally.
  - .sidebar-brand-row is now the <button>. Single click does NOTHING (the rail
    collapsing because you brushed its header would be a nasty surprise),
    dblclick toggles, Enter/Space toggles so the keyboard and screen readers
    keep a labelled path. Ctrl+B still works. It inherits the app's bare-element
    button styling, so background/border/padding/font all have to be stripped
    explicitly or the wordmark renders inside a surface-filled box.
  - notifiedWorkspaceIds derived in App.tsx on the liveWorkspaceIds pattern.
  - ONE dot, two intensities, no fifth marker: blocking = filled + pulsing
    (unchanged), activity = transparent with a 1.5px inset ring, no animation.
    Blocking outranks activity on a row that is both.

MEASURED (7 fixture views):
  header    full 47-63px; ICONS 74px -> 45px, the button row is simply gone
  brand     19 @165 -> 26 @224 -> 32 @420 (clamp ceiling)
  centring  centreOffset {0} everywhere; overflow 0 at the 165px narrowest
  button    tagName BUTTON, background transparent, border 0 -- chrome stripped
  dots      blocking: filled + ymux-attn-dot; activity: transparent + inset ring
            + animation none. Verified in both the tokyo and modernist palettes.
  leftovers 0 nodes matching .sidebar-collapse-btn / .sidebar-notif-badge

Checked rather than assumed: themes-redesign.css:95 puts a 3px double rule under
.sidebar-brand-row for broadsheet. My `border: none` is less specific AND loads
first, so the theme still wins -- toggled the preset live and confirmed
"3px double | pad 6px".

Also removed: the pendingNotifCount prop, .sidebar-collapse-btn and
.sidebar-notif-badge CSS entirely, and the two App.css breadcrumbs that named
them. 3 new i18n keys x 4 locales.

2026-08-19 02:00-03:00 | Build speed: measure first, then cut
  Branch claude/optimize-build-process-18f637, worktree optimize-build-process-18f637
  Commits: 6234dac (perf), b2217d0 (docs), + this entry

  TASK: Yossi -- "the build takes 12-15 min, let's go over it and make it faster."

  MEASURED BEFORE CHANGING ANYTHING. Most of the intuitive suspects were wrong.
  gh run view on two build-windows runs, same week, same workflow:
    run 32187049792 (branch hook-pane-resolve) "No cache found" -> 813s in
      the single "Build release bundles" step, 15m37s total
    run 32193464505 (branch that had already run)  ~696 MB restored -> 420s,
      9m24s total
  Everything else in both runs is noise (checkout 5-7s, npm ci 14s, uploads
  2-3s). So the spread IS the cold dependency graph, nothing else.

  CAUSE 1 -- app/src-tauri/Cargo.toml:22 built the lib THREE TIMES.
    crate-type = ["staticlib", "cdylib", "rlib"] is the Tauri MOBILE template
    default; there is no gen/android or gen/apple in this repo and the only
    mobile reference anywhere is lib.rs:7851. Desktop links the rlib only
    (main.rs is `app_lib::run()`). Verified in the existing target dir, all
    stamped 15:41 one minute before app.exe:
      libapp_lib.rlib   228 MB  needed
      app_lib.lib       215 MB  staticlib, nothing reads it
      app_lib.dll       456 KB  cdylib, a second full link of the whole
                                object graph, dead-stripped down to this
    Now ["rlib"]. AFTER the change a full release build emitted only
    libapp_lib-64fa7e8b379eeebd.rlib -- no .lib, no .dll (the Aug 13 ones are
    leftovers cargo does not sweep). Put the other two back if mobile lands;
    there is a worktree named winmux-mobile-version-4e7e1b, so say so out loud.

  CAUSE 2 -- GitHub cache reads are scoped to the current branch + the DEFAULT
    branch. build-windows.yml runs only on workflow_dispatch and v* tags, i.e.
    never on main, so it never wrote a cache any other branch could read.
    ADDED .github/workflows/warm-rust-cache.yml: push-to-main on Cargo.lock /
    Cargo.toml, plus Mon+Thu cron (GitHub evicts unused caches at 7 days),
    plus dispatch. Builds `-p app --lib` only -- rust-cache prunes workspace
    members before saving, so only the dependency graph is worth warming; no
    exe link, no bundlers. shared-key: windows-release, same key as
    build-windows.yml. ci-windows.yml gets shared-key: windows-dev so the
    dev-profile cache does not fight it.

  ALSO FIXED (each one was quietly forcing a full app-crate rebuild):
  - resources/ are pulled in with include_bytes!/include_str!
    (remote_bootstrap.rs:28-29, addons.rs:34-35, lib.rs:7659) and cargo's
    staleness check is MTIME-based, so `Copy-Item -Force` of a byte-identical
    binary recompiled the 9.4k-line lib every build. build-linux-cli.ps1 now
    compares SHA256 and leaves identical files alone -- including
    remote-manifest.json, whose built_at churn ci-windows.yml has a whole
    step apologising for.
  - build.rs + cli/build.rs baked SystemTime::now() into YMUX_BUILD_TIME, so
    no two builds of identical source ever agreed -> the staged musl binary
    differed every run -> see above. It is `git log -1 --format=%ct` now,
    which also makes build-linux-cli.ps1's "byte-identical regardless of who
    built it" claim true and pairs with YMUX_GIT_HASH. Consumer is
    rpc_server.rs:2152 (dev.get-state) only.
  - target/release/{wix,nsis} sit INSIDE the directory rust-cache prunes, so
    CI re-downloaded both bundler toolchains every run. actions/cache now
    keeps them, keyed on package-lock.json.
  - workflow_dispatch builds get --bundles nsis; v* tags still get both,
    because the published manifest.json advertises msi_url. updater.rs treats
    msi_url as Option throughout (48, 88, 675, 777-788), so nothing breaks.

  THE SCRUB PROTECTION WAS FICTION.
    build-release.ps1:33-35 deleted app.exe and deps/app-* "so the new remap
    is applied". `Get-ChildItem -Filter app-*` matches NOTHING (bin artifacts
    in deps/ are unhashed: app.exe, app.d, app.pdb), and release/app.exe is a
    hardlink cargo re-creates for fresh units without recompiling. The real
    guarantee is RUSTFLAGS being part of cargo's fingerprint. Block deleted;
    build-windows.yml now ASSERTS $USERNAME is absent from app.exe -- the
    check docs/RELEASING.md:56 has always documented and CI never ran.

  TRIED AND REVERTED: rust-lld for the MSVC host link. Same relink (touch
    main.rs, tauri build --no-bundle, warm tree): 199s with link.exe, 191s
    with rust-lld. 4%, inside the noise -- that window is dominated by
    recompiling app_lib at opt-level 3, not by linking. Not worth an unusual
    linker on a shipped binary for 7 seconds. Logged in DECISIONS.md so it is
    not re-litigated without a new measurement.

  TWO THINGS THE EXPERIMENT SETTLED, kept because they are load-bearing:
  - target.<triple>.linker IS in cargo's fingerprint on 1.95. Adding the
    section and removing it BOTH forced a large rebuild (6m56s, 6m47s), so
    toggling a linker carries no stale-artifact hazard here.
  - The last measurement build ran `npm run tauri build` DIRECTLY, bypassing
    build-release.ps1, and `grep -c -a mlastudent371 app.exe` came back 296
    where a wrapper build gives 0. That is the new build-windows.yml assert
    firing on exactly the case it was written for -- demonstrated, not
    assumed. It also shows the RUSTFLAGS fingerprint doing its job: a release
    cannot silently reuse that binary, because the wrapper's flags force a
    rebuild. (The app.exe left in target/release right now is that unscrubbed
    measurement build. Harmless -- gitignored, and build-release.ps1 rebuilds
    it -- but do not hand-copy it anywhere.)
  - remote-manifest.json showed NO git churn across five LOCAL builds.
    CORRECTION after run 32199948594: this does NOT quieten the CI drift
    notice, and I said it did. CI still emitted
      ##[notice]remote-manifest sha256 changed
    with sha fbf2c01c / 2,498,992 bytes against the committed 8ecb0623 /
    2,521,136 -- i.e. exactly the toolchain difference FOLLOWUPS P2 describes
    (CI takes dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable, this box is 1.95.0) and that I
    did NOT fix. The step only ever emitted ::notice on a real sha change, so
    the mtime guard was never what was firing it. What the guard does buy is
    local: no spurious rebuild of the app crate, and no working-tree churn
    between builds on one machine. The CI notice stays on until the toolchain
    is pinned (rust-toolchain.toml) or the binary stops being committed.

  LANDMINE FOUND THE HARD WAY: Windows PowerShell 5.1 reads BOM-less UTF-8 as
    ANSI. A UTF-8 em-dash inside a .ps1 STRING LITERAL decodes to a smart
    quote the tokenizer accepts as a string delimiter -- the file then fails
    to parse with a cascade of "Missing closing '}'" errors pointing at
    innocent lines 10-100 lines away. Em-dashes in # comments are fine, which
    is why build-linux-cli.ps1 (full of them) had never bitten.
    ci-windows.yml now parse-checks app/scripts/*.ps1 with `shell: powershell`
    in seconds. FOLLOWUPS P3 for the real fix (write them with a BOM).

  VERIFIED (Rules 13 + 14, on a real run -- not a compile):
  - Full release build from the worktree, BOTH bundlers: 374s. Produced
    ymux_0.4.5_x64_en-US.msi (23.4 MB) and ymux_0.4.5_x64-setup.exe (16.7 MB).
  - Rule 13: index-d2jVOVvY.js (this build's hashed asset) found inside
    app.exe -> production build, not dev-mode.
  - Scrub: `grep -c -a mlastudent371 app.exe` -> 0.
  - Rule 14: LAUNCHED IT. Yossi had 7 app.exe running, so it went up under
    YMUX_CONFIG_DIR pointed at a scratch dir (the isolation FOLLOWUPS
    recommends) rather than sharing %APPDATA%\ymux. Log reached
    `[UI:APP] longtask self 5752ms` -- a FRONTEND-originated line, which a
    dev-mode binary can never produce because no frontend ever loads. Killed
    by exact image path afterwards; Yossi's 7 untouched.
  - ci-windows.yml parse-check run locally against all three scripts: ok.

  branch (cold, then warm off the cache the first run saved), plus ci-windows:

    build-windows COLD  32199929496  16m05s  build step 798s (was 813s / 15m37s)

2026-08-19 — ROOT CAUSE: the terminal grid inherited the UI's text direction
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Found while running the diagnostic step of the local/remote-profile plan,
  before writing any of that feature. Worth more than the feature.

  CHAIN
    i18n/index.ts:75      sets dir on <html> from the interface language
    xterm.js              pins NO direction — verified, nothing in its CSS and
                          no setAttribute("dir") anywhere in its lib
    App.css               .terminal-container had no direction rule
    dir inherits          => a Hebrew UI flipped the entire character grid
    terminalInstance:971  ONLY auto_per_line set dir="ltr" on .xterm-rows

  So every rtl_mode EXCEPT auto_per_line rendered the grid RTL, from the UI
  language rather than from any terminal setting.

  EXPLAINS TWO THINGS THAT HAD RESISTED EXPLANATION
    - "off" looking identical to auto_per_line on a fresh local pane. They
      should differ sharply (WebGL, zero bidi vs DOM with per-row dir). Both
      ended up RTL by different routes.
    - "מיושר לשמאל במקום לימין" on SSH. Alignment was never a terminal setting;
      it was the grid's inherited paragraph direction.

  FIX  direction: ltr on .terminal-container, unconditional. A terminal is a
  fixed grid — column 0 is always leftmost. Per-row direction still varies via
  the row's `dir` attribute, which is a declaration on the element itself and
  still beats the inherited value, so auto_per_line is unaffected. The popout
  window reuses the same class and is fixed with it.

  WHY IT SURVIVED SO LONG: every RTL round in DECISIONS tested auto_per_line —
  the one mode that accidentally immunised itself by pinning the rows host to
  LTR. The bug needs a non-auto mode AND a Hebrew/Arabic interface.

  IMPACT ON THE PLANNED WORK: the per-connection-kind profile split is NOT
  started. Part of what looked like a local-vs-remote difference was this leak,
  which was never connection-specific. Re-measure both sides on this build
  before building profiles — the feature may shrink or may still be needed
  (SSH = Linux Claude inside tmux, local = Windows Claude, no tmux).

  CHECKS  tsc clean, vite clean, 47/47 + 11/11 unit tests. NOT VERIFIED LIVE.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-19 — RTL settings split per pane class (local Windows vs Linux SSH/WSL)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  MEASURED FIRST, then built. Same build, same session, both directions:
    remote (SSH -> Linux): auto_per_line CORRECT, off reversed
    local  (Windows ConPTY): bidi_reorder CORRECT, off reversed, auto reversed
  The two classes need OPPOSITE modes, so one global setting could only ever
  satisfy one of them. Yossi called this from the symptoms before I did:
  "ההגדרה הזו עובדת או למקומי או למרוחק - צריך לעשות הפרדה מובנית".

  WHY THEY DIFFER: a native Windows pane gets Hebrew ALREADY in visual order
  (the Windows console keeps RTL visually ordered in its screen buffer and
  ConPTY re-emits that buffer), so our logical->visual reorder is what makes it
  right and a browser bidi pass is a second reorder that breaks it. Linux just
  passes bytes through in logical order.

  SPLIT AXIS is ConnCaps.posixExec, NOT a local/remote boolean — types.ts:242
  warns against that boolean precisely because WSL falls through both. WSL is
  grouped with REMOTE: Linux, tmux, Linux Claude; local only geographically.
  Yossi confirmed: "מקומי הוא ללא WSL - טרמינל רגיל לחלוטין של ווינדוס".

  RUST   RtlProfile / RtlProfiles in settings.rs + TerminalSettings.rtl, as
         Option<> so ABSENT IS THE MIGRATION SIGNAL: a fresh install gets the
         measured per-class defaults, an existing settings.json has no `rtl`
         key, deserialises to None, and migrate_rtl_profiles seeds BOTH
         profiles from that user's own flat values — nobody's behaviour
         changes on upgrade. Hooked into the existing migrate_settings, which
         already persists. The 4 flat fields stay, deprecated, one release.
         4 new tests (seeding, idempotence, defaults-differ, absent->None).

  FRONT  terminalInstance.ts: the four scalar globals became
         g_rtl: Record<RtlProfileKind, RtlProfileSettings>; every read goes
         through this.profile. Constructor takes the profile.
         types.ts: profileFor(conn) beside capsOf, one line, reuses posixExec.
         PaneView already had the connection at the ensureTerm seam — it just
         wasn't passing it.
         App.tsx ensureTerm rebuilds an instance when the profile changes AND
         that flips the renderer (xterm.js has no DOM<->WebGL swap on a live
         Terminal), which covers a pane built before its connection was known.
         New hasStaleRenderer()/staleRenderer: the "re-open the pane" contract
         used to be a code comment only, so a mode switch looked like it did
         nothing.
  UI     Settings -> Text & Language -> RTL: a local|remote pill switching
         which profile the existing four controls edit. Reuses
         .settings-mode-toggle (the theme-mode pill in the same modal) — no
         new CSS. i18n x4, 1083 keys each, parity intact, JSON validated.

  CHECKS 253 cargo tests, 58 JS tests, tsc clean, vite clean. NOT LIVE YET.
  STILL OPEN: on a local pane auto_per_line renders reversed even though the
  stream is logical there — it should have worked. Adding a per-pane renderer
  log line next rather than inferring from screenshots. Also still open, and
  not from this change: caret pinned right and partial-repaint glitches under
  bidi_reorder (terminal-wg's "cursed cursor").

  FOLLOW-UP (same day, after Yossi verified the split live): the migration no
  longer carries the old flat `rtl_mode` across. Yossi: "זה צריך להיות מוגדר
  בDEFAULT שמקומי הוא על BIDI ומרוחק הוא על אוטומטי". Correct, and the earlier
  caution no longer applies — a single pre-split value was NECESSARILY wrong
  for at least one of the two classes, since they need opposite modes, so
  there was nothing worth preserving. Each profile now takes its own measured
  default (local=bidi_reorder, remote=auto_per_line); auto_direction,
  mirror_arrows_rtl and tui_owns_bidi still carry over, being orthogonal to
  the split. Test rewritten to assert exactly that. 253 cargo tests pass.
  Narrow gap accepted: anyone who ran the ONE intermediate build already has
  `rtl` written (both = their old value) and will not be re-migrated. That is
  a single build and a single session; not worth a second migration pass.

2026-08-19 — Zellij replaces WSL: persistence for native Windows panes

GATE FIRST. Yossi ran the manual spike: detach -> reattach -> TYPING WORKS.
That was the one thing that could have killed the idea (#5365 reports it
broken on Windows); it does not reproduce on 0.44.3. Tooling had already
confirmed the rest: `winget install Zellij.Zellij` -> official 0.44.3 at
%LOCALAPPDATA%\Zellij\zellij.exe, and every verb needed exists. The
third-party arndawg fork is NOT needed — upstream really ships windows-msvc.

CORRECTION to the 2026-08-18 note: the heise "0.44.0, native Windows, March
2026" story does not survive contact. The binary is dated 13 May and 0.44.3's
notes are ordinary patch notes. Windows support is real; that date/version
claim is not. I had planned around it; don't.

VERIFIED LIVE, END TO END — `zellij list-sessions` on Yossi's machine:
    ymux-p_18cd0fd918b299e0_1 [Created 3m 52s ago]
That prefix + pane id is ours. spawn -> 900ms -> `zellij attach -c` works.

COMMITS  a2d296d persistence for local panes
         12c6706 InstallZellij in the wizard
         e4f54dc wsl -> local workspace migration
         137c789 WSL out of the UI; wizard creates a local workspace
         ffce444 local RTL default corrected (below)

HOW  Types `zellij attach -c <name>` into the shell 900ms after spawn — same
     shape and timing spawn_wsl_pty already used. Deliberately NOT spawning
     zellij as the pane process: pick_default_shell + utf8_shell_args set up
     chcp 65001 for PowerShell, and losing that undoes the day's Hebrew work.
     Separate function from build_tmux_attach_script, which is pure POSIX and
     load-bearing for SSH and WSL both (Phase 65 records the cost of editing
     it carelessly).

BUG FIXED BEFORE IT COULD FIRE  pane_kill_session branches on the Session
     variant, so a Session::Local carrying a name mapped to KillTarget::Wsl
     and would have run `wsl.exe -- tmux kill-session` against the DEFAULT
     distro. Unreachable only because native-local sessions never had a name —
     populating tmux_session is exactly what would have made it live. Now
     split on the distro, which is what actually tells the two apart.

DATA SAFETY  Connection is #[serde(tag="type")] and lives in workspaces.json.
     Deleting the Wsl variant would make serde reject the WHOLE file, and
     load_from_disk then refuses every later save — the `load FAILED …
     persists will refuse` failure already in Yossi's log on 2026-08-18. So
     the variant stays as a deserialization shim and migrate_wsl_workspaces
     rewrites it to Local on load, walking PANE connections too (a pane can
     carry its own). 5 tests, incl. that a file containing "type":"wsl" still
     parses. Checked against the real file: 5 workspaces, no wsl, nothing to do.

A DECISION THAT LASTED HOURS, AND WHY  This morning a native Windows pane
     needed rtl_mode=bidi_reorder, because raw ConPTY hands over Hebrew
     already in VISUAL order. Zellij now sits in front of every local pane and
     NORMALISES the stream to logical order, like a Linux pty. Re-measured
     inside a live zellij pane: `off` reversed, `auto_per_line` correct. So
     the local default went back to auto_per_line, and both RTL profiles have
     converged. Said plainly: the per-connection split no longer needs
     different defaults, because zellij removed the difference it was built
     for. It still lets the two be tuned apart; it just isn't load-bearing
     any more. Comments updated rather than left claiming otherwise.

NOT A CODE BUG, MY HANDOFF ERROR  Yossi's InstallLocalHooks failure was the
     BARE app.exe: resolve_ymux_cli looks for resources/ymux-cli.exe beside
     the binary and the bare artifact ships without them. The error string
     already predicts it ("dev build without staged resources?"). The setup
     wizard needs the NSIS/MSI installer. Fixed the handoff, not the code.

STALE STRINGS FOUND BY THAT RUN  "No WSL workspace was created" survived the
     removal, and was gated on wsl_chain_ok instead of on whether a workspace
     actually came back. Both fixed, four locales.

STILL IN THE TREE, UNREACHABLE FROM THE UI  wsl_exec, the RPC bridge, the 8
     step arms, and the wsl branches in tickets/worktrees/addons. Deliberately
     a separate commit — they touch three modules unrelated to WSL and would
     have made this diff unreviewable.

CHECKS  267 cargo tests (14 new), 58 JS tests, tsc + vite clean, i18n 1088 x4.
        Installer sha256 15fc1236954e4b1a98530d6a806e9e3add225dfb6efca6d57056febfd71ef306

2026-08-19 (cont.)  RTL round 2 — the status bar — + the zellij verb surface

WHAT YOSSI SAW  A screenshot of Claude Code inside a local zellij pane: right
     aligned, and the Latin runs in the wrong order. "winget upgrade
     Anthropic.ClaudeCode :Update available! Run" instead of "Update
     available! Run winget upgrade Anthropic.ClaudeCode". The Hebrew itself
     was perfect. "צעד בכיוון הנכון - עכשיו בא נוודא שהכל מגיע כמו שצריך."

WHAT THE SCREENSHOT PROVED, FOR FREE  `צבר` rendered as ר-ב-צ left-to-right
     under dir=rtl. dir=rtl means the browser ALREADY reversed that run, so
     the bytes arriving must be LOGICAL order. That settles a question the
     morning's measurement had left inverted, without running anything.

DIAGNOSIS  Not a bidi bug — a scope bug. Claude's footer is ONE terminal row
     carrying ~64 Latin characters of layout plus the cwd. The rule was "any
     Hebrew char anywhere -> the row is RTL", so three letters flipped the
     row's paragraph direction and UAX #9 L2 mirrored every Latin run in it.
     The bordered box above it went the same way via the block rule. Such a
     row is POSITIONAL: the TUI already chose the column of every glyph, and
     bidi cannot reconstruct a layout it has just mirrored.

FIX  detectDirection is now a vote between STRONG characters: Hebrew wins
     unless Latin outnumbers it by more than RTL_DOMINANCE (4). Neutrals —
     digits, punctuation, box-drawing, whitespace — count for neither side, so
     a progress bar cannot outvote a Hebrew word. The block vote runs over the
     block's joined text instead of "does any row contain Hebrew".

     Paragraph direction ONLY. An LTR row still gets its RTL runs reversed by
     the browser, so `צבר` reads right either way; alignment and the neutral
     ends are what move.

     4 is calibrated, not derived — it has to keep `2. /opt/wa/.shared.env -
     הערה` (4 vs 14) RTL and let the status bar (3 vs 64) go LTR. Anything
     from ~3.6 to ~20 separates them. Written down at the constant so the next
     person tuning it knows what it is holding apart. All 47 existing
     direction tests pass unchanged; 7 new ones, incl. the real status row.

ZELLIJ VERBS  "צריך להגדיר את כל הפקודות שישלחו לZELLIJ" — they are one
     documented block now, each checked against `zellij 0.44.3 --help` on this
     machine, not from memory: attach -c, list-sessions -n, kill-session,
     delete-session. What is NOT sent is recorded with the reason at the same
     place: `attach -b` returns 0 and creates nothing without a tty (verified
     — twice); `action rename-session` would break the pane-id-derived lookup
     a cold start needs; kill-all/delete-all have no UI that means them.

REAL BUG FOUND WHILE DOING IT  parse_zellij_sessions threw away the
     "(EXITED - attach to resurrect)" marker, so a dead session looked exactly
     like a live one in the picker — and kill-session FAILS on a dead session,
     so the entry could never be cleared. Yossi's machine has one sitting in
     the list right now (ymux-p_18cd0fd918b299e0_1). `exited` is carried to
     the picker as its own badge, and a failed kill now falls through to
     delete-session. A live session still needs two kills to be purged.

CHECKS  270 cargo tests pass (3 new), 65 JS tests (7 new), tsc + vite clean,
        i18n 1089 keys x 4 locales.

REGRESSION, CAUGHT IN ONE BUILD  "עכשיו זה מיושר לשמאל - וגם במרוחקים זה מיושר
     לשמאל". The dominance vote shipped UNGATED, so it applied to every row in
     every pane: a Hebrew note on a long path, `ls` with one Hebrew filename,
     and the SSH side that had been working fine went left-aligned too. My
     regression, not a calibration miss.

     The discriminator was never the ratio. It is whether something PLACED the
     glyphs. `tuiOwnsBidi` — the title-based detection that already tells us
     Claude Code holds the pane — is exactly that signal, and it was sitting
     in the file the whole time; I reached for a global rule instead.

     So the vote is now gated on it: positional rows (TUI in front) get the
     dominance vote, text rows keep the pre-2026-08-19 "any Hebrew takes the
     row". Note this uses the DETECTED state, not `bidiOwnedByTui` — the
     legacy force-everything-LTR effect stays gated behind its setting and
     default-off. 3 new tests pin the ungated path, including one that asserts
     all 24 table cases are identical with the gate off.

2026-08-19 (cont.)  RTL round 3 — separating remote from local for real

THE INSTRUCTION  "הרי מקודם הכל במרוחק עבד כמו שצריך ... אתה יכול להשוות מול
     MAIN ... אתה רק צריך לוודא שיש הפרדה מוחלטת בין המרוחק למקומי - ואז
     השינויים שתרצה לעשות על המקומי לא יפגעו במרוחק." Right on both counts.

MEASURED INSTEAD OF GUESSED  `git diff origin/main...HEAD` over the four
     RTL-relevant frontend files: the entire delta in the shared direction
     module is ONE flag (`dominance`) on detectDirection, and bidi.ts is
     byte-identical to main. Remote's other profile defaults already match
     main's globals. So the regression was one line — applyRowDirections passed
     `this.tuiOwnsBidi` as that flag, and the OSC title propagates over SSH
     (that is HOW Claude Code is detected), so the vote fired on remote panes.

     Two rounds of reading screenshots; one `git diff` settled it.

ALSO ASKED, AND THE ANSWER WAS NO  "בקוד לפני כן התייחסו אליה בנפרד?" — did the
     code ever treat Claude's status bar as its own case? It did not. main has
     exactly one mechanism, `tuiOwnsBidi`, and it is per-PANE and default-off.
     The per-row separation was my invention, not a restoration. Dropped.

     Also asked whether the remote pane had had its settings changed while
     open — that would have made it the staleRenderer hole below. Answer:
     clean start, untouched. Ruled it out before writing any code.

FIX  `directionPolicy` is a per-profile field: "any_rtl" (the pre-2026-08-19
     rule, the default on BOTH) or "tui_dominance" (opt-in per profile, in
     Settings so it is reachable rather than dead code). Keyed on which pane
     CLASS this is, never on what runs inside it. Absent in an existing
     settings.json it reads as any_rtl — an upgrade cannot move a pane onto the
     newer rule silently.

     Local's right alignment comes back as a consequence, keeping its current
     letter ordering: it stays on auto_per_line, so DOM renderer + per-row dir
     are untouched.

GUARDS, BECAUSE A COMMENT IS WHAT FAILED  textDirection.test.ts now carries
     main's detectDirection VERBATIM as a reference implementation and asserts
     the default path equals it — all 24 table cases, the rows the vote was
     written for, and a whole screen with a table and a fence so the block
     rules are covered too. settings.rs asserts remote's default is any_rtl and
     that an absent field deserializes to it.

THE LOG LINE, FINALLY  FOLLOWUPS P2 said in the morning: "NOT diagnosed, and
     deliberately not guessed at: the next step is a log line stating, per pane,
     which renderer was chosen and what rtlModeAtConstruct was." It was never
     added and two rounds were spent guessing instead. Added now: per pane the
     profile, live mode, frozen rtlModeAtConstruct, the renderer ACTUALLY
     loaded, policy and staleRenderer — knowable nowhere else, since half are
     frozen at construction and half are read live. Plus a per-pass summary of
     strong-character counts and resolved directions, emitted only when the
     direction vector changes.

     Metadata only (Rule #1): a count cannot reconstruct the characters it
     counted. And it has to be a number in debug.log, because visual order
     cannot be written down in a chat message — the message is itself reordered
     by whatever renders it. That is why the screenshots kept being ambiguous.

FOUND, NOT LANDED  flushPending reads the LIVE rtl mode while the renderer
     choice and applyRowDirections read the FROZEN rtlModeAtConstruct. A pane
     built as bidi_reorder/off (WebGL) whose profile later becomes
     auto_per_line gets NEITHER — no reorder and no dir, i.e. zero bidi
     handling, silently. `staleRenderer` computes that state and only a
     Settings note mentions it. Yossi's clean start rules it out as today's
     cause, so it waits for the regression to be confirmed fixed before it
     lands as its own commit.

CHECKS  272 cargo tests (2 new), 71 JS tests (3 new), tsc + vite clean,
        i18n 1091 keys x 4 locales.

2026-08-19 (cont.)  RTL round 4 — the log answered it, and it was not the answer

THE MEASUREMENT THAT SHOULD HAVE COME FIRST  `zellij action dump-screen` on the
     live local pane returned the cwd as U+05E8 U+05D1 U+05E6 — צבר in VISUAL
     order. Claude Code on Windows writes RTL pre-reordered, exactly as the
     header of textDirection.ts has said since July. Every mode that reorders
     is therefore a SECOND pass, which is why all three read as broken:
       off            painted verbatim              -> CORRECT, left aligned
       auto_per_line  browser reverses the run      -> reversed, right
       bidi_reorder   our pass reverses again       -> reversed, left
     Yossi's own description sealed it: switch modes and the letters are right
     for one frame (old buffer, new direction) then flip (new bytes).

TWO OF MY OWN DEFECTS, BOTH FROM THE SAME HOUR  (1) `dir` sets a PARAGRAPH's
     base direction; UAX #9 L2 still reverses RTL runs inside it. So
     `tui_owns_bidi` forcing dir="ltr" never suppressed anything — only
     `unicode-bidi: bidi-override` does. (2) The migration copied the flat
     `tui_owns_bidi` into both profiles, and that field's serde default is
     false, so an ABSENT key was written as a decision and silently overrode
     the local default I had added minutes earlier. Both fixed.

AND THEN THE LOG SAID THE WHOLE MECHANISM IS DEAD  Every `rtl-dirs` line in
     Yossi's debug.log reads `tui=0`, on every pane and both profiles, and the
     file holds not one `tui-owns-bidi` transition. The detector reads the
     terminal title; inside zellij the title reaching ymux is zellij's own.
     `tui_owns_bidi` has never fired — the checkbox promised something that
     could not happen. FOLLOWUPS P1, with the fix sketched: the ymux Claude
     hooks already run per pane with YMUX_PANE_ID and are a signal that
     survives a multiplexer.

WHAT SHIPPED  local defaults to rtl_mode="off" — the only mode that applies no
     bidi at all, which is what a pre-reordered stream needs, and the mode
     Yossi confirmed works. REMOTE UNTOUCHED: it delivers logical order and is
     correct on auto_per_line. The two profiles now genuinely differ, and a
     test asserts they must.

THE COST, STATED NOT HIDDEN  Shell output is logical, so Hebrew at a plain
     PowerShell prompt renders reversed under "off". One static mode cannot
     serve both a logical shell and a pre-reordered TUI. Only the dynamic
     switch can, and that is the P1 above.

ALSO CONFIRMED FROM THE LOG  stripPaneFrame works (local rows=31 rtl=1, not
     rtl=all), and the live renderer swap works cleanly
     (`rtl-renderer ... now=off renderer=webgl`).

CHECKS  275 cargo tests, 76 JS tests, tsc + vite clean.

     set-pane-frame-style and toggle-no-ui-fullscreen, none of which exist in

     0.44.3 --help on this machine, not from the website:
       - what ymux sends today, with file:line, and the not-sent list with why
       - root options (incl. the -s-before-subcommand rule) + all subcommands
