2026-08-20  Zellij lockdown, part 1: one pane, no chrome, no keybinds
WHY  Yossi: "each of our panes should hold exactly ONE zellij pane" — and the
     frame was still on screen after the 2026-08-19 fix. Both true, and the
     second had a cause nobody had looked for: `pane_frames false` removes the
     border AROUND a pane, not the two PLUGIN ROWS zellij's default layout puts
     above and below it. Captured with `zellij setup --dump-layout default`:
       layout { pane size=1 {plugin "tab-bar"}  pane  pane size=1 {plugin
       "status-bar"} }
     No config key removes those. Only a layout of our own does.

     There was also no lock at all: zellij's default keybinds were live, so
     Ctrl+p n split the pane — with pane_frames off there was then no boundary
     between the two shells. And zellij ate Ctrl-o/p/n/t/h/s/g before the
     program inside ever saw them.

WHAT resources/layouts/ymux.kdl (new) — `layout { pane }`. No plugin rows, no
       tab node, no swap layouts. This is what gives every pane back 2 rows.
     resources/ymux-zellij.kdl — rewritten from 1 key to 7:
       pane_frames false / default_layout "ymux" / keybinds clear-defaults=true
       {} / default_mode "locked" / mouse_mode false / show_release_notes false
       / show_startup_tips false
     lib.rs — resolve_zellij_config now returns ZellijResources {config_file,
       config_dir} and accepts a directory only when BOTH files are in it
       (pick_zellij_resources, split out so a test can reach it).
       spawn_local_pty exports ZELLIJ_CONFIG_DIR alongside ZELLIJ_CONFIG_FILE.
     tauri.conf.json — bundles resources/layouts/ymux.kdl.

HOW THE LAYOUT IS FOUND  ZELLIJ_CONFIG_DIR=<resources> makes zellij's layout dir
     default to <resources>\layouts, so `default_layout "ymux"` resolves with NO
     absolute path in a bundled file (which ships identical bytes everywhere,
     and `zellij attach` has no --layout to pass one). Verified with
     `zellij setup --check`: [LAYOUT DIR] moved, [DATA DIR] and [PLUGIN DIR]
     did not.

TWO THINGS THE GATE CAUGHT BEFORE A BUILD
     1. `keybinds clear-defaults=true` with no children FAILS TO PARSE —
        "keybindings with no children". The empty `{ }` body is required.
        `zellij setup --check` is a real validator (exit 1 + a KDL diagnostic);
        it is now step 1 of the checklist and it paid for itself immediately.
     2. `setup --dump-layout <name>` only dumps BUILT-IN layouts — it printed
        nothing at exit 0 for both our layout and a nonsense name, so it is not
        a probe in either direction. Layout resolution needs the live run.

mouse_mode REVERSES AN EARLIER NOTE, deliberately. The old comment said
     mouse_mode was "drafted and removed" — that was about forcing it ON.
     Zellij's default IS on, so leaving the key out was leaving the mouse
     captured. `false` lines up with ymux-tmux.conf's `set -g mouse off`.

THE COST, written down rather than discovered: no scrollback inside a zellij
     pane. No keybinds = no scroll mode; mouse_mode false = zellij never sees
     the wheel; xterm.js's wheel scrolls its normal buffer, which sits behind
     zellij's alt screen. Accepted. Checklist item 8 measures it; if the wheel
     is dead the fallback is mouse_mode true (Shift bypasses capture for
     selection, which is where the tmux side landed — FOLLOWUPS:33).

OLD SESSIONS ADOPT THIS PARTLY, and that shape is in no design doc: keybinds
     and pane_frames are client-side and apply on the next attach;
     default_layout and mouse_mode are "(Requires restart)" and do not. So a
     session created before this comes back WITH BARS BUT WITHOUT KEYBINDS
     until it is killed once. There are 4 live ymux-p_* sessions on this box
     that will show it. Do not start a hunt over that screenshot.

TESTS  7 new in mod zellij_tests, all compile-time: the kdl and the layout are
     embedded with include_str! (same reason remote-manifest.json is, lib.rs
     ~8216 — a runtime read passes here and fails on a user's machine), so a
     renamed resource fails the BUILD. Plus tauri.conf.json parsed and asserted
     to bundle both, and pick_zellij_resources' all-or-nothing rule in four
     directions. 21/21 zellij tests green, cargo check clean.

NOT VERIFIED  Nothing here has run live. Zero chrome on screen, keys reaching
     the shell, and `default_layout` resolving at session CREATION are all
     checklist items, not claims. Rule #14.

2026-08-20  Zellij lockdown, part 2: Kill actually kills, and reports what it did
WHY  Yossi: "session disconnect doesn't really work today — it should be a
     delete command." Correct, and worse than it looked.

     pane_kill_session ran `kill-session` and fell through to `delete-session`
     ONLY IF THE KILL FAILED. On a live session the kill succeeds, so delete
     never ran: the session came back EXITED-and-resurrectable. The comment
     called this a deliberate two-step where a second click deletes — and that
     second click DOES NOT EXIST. The first click removes the pane from
     pane_sessions, so a second returns early having sent no verb, and the
     button is gated on isConnected/isTmux(), both false by then, so it is not
     even rendered. `zellij_args_delete` had exactly one call site in the repo
     and nothing could reach it. Net: a red danger-classed button labelled
     "no resume" that left a resurrectable session behind.

WHAT delete-session -f, one verb. Kills if running, then discards the saved
       copy. zellij_args_kill / zellij_args_delete are gone with the two-step.
     zellij_try + ZellijOutcome{Ok, Failed{code,stderr}, Missing}. zellij_run
       stays as a bool wrapper so the smart-connect write-chars call site is
       untouched. Before this, "the verb failed" and "zellij is not installed"
       were the same `false` — which is how a Kill on a machine with no zellij
       destroyed nothing and reported success.
     KillSessionOutcome{result,backend,session,detail} crosses Tauri IPC AND
       JSON-RPC. result ∈ killed|already_gone|no_session|multiplexer_missing|
       failed|attempted. Err(String) stays for "could not even try".
     kill_pane_session_inner extracted; the Tauri command and the RPC handler
       now call the SAME code.
     App.tsx killSession reads the payload instead of assuming success, and
       toasts on failure while KEEPING the restore hint.
     i18n en/he/ar/ru: the labels matched neither the old behaviour nor the
       new one.

THE RPC WAS LYING TOO  rpc_server.rs "pane.kill-session" carried its own copy
     that matched only Session::Ssh. A zellij or WSL pane fell to `_`, no
     multiplexer verb ran, the shell was killed — and it still answered
     {"ok":true,"killed":true}. So `ymux pane-disconnect --kill` reported a
     kill it had never performed on EVERY native Windows pane. Fixed by
     construction: the duplicate is gone, `killed` is now derived from the
     shared outcome.

CAPTURED, NOT GUESSED  `zellij delete-session -f <missing-name>` → exit 2,
     stderr `Session: "<name>" not found.` That is classified already_gone,
     not failed: nothing to destroy is the caller's goal. A LIVE delete cannot
     be exercised headlessly (attach -b exits 0 creating nothing), so it is a
     checklist item.

RESTORE DELIBERATELY NOT FILTERED  App.tsx builds its live set with no
     `.exited` filter, and that is the reboot-restore path: a Windows reboot
     leaves EVERY zellij session EXITED, and attach -c brings them back — the
     one thing zellij does that tmux cannot, and why it was adopted. The
     killed-session-resurrects bug was the KILL's fault and is fixed there.
     Added the comment saying so plus per-pane "(resurrecting a saved
     session)" logging, because the log could not tell the two apart, which is
     why this needed a code read to find.

CLOSE-PANE UNCHANGED, by Yossi's call: the session survives (symmetric with
     SSH/WSL, where X is a detach). Added the log line naming the surviving
     session, since nothing recorded it. The other half he asked for — keeping
     the restore hint — is NOT ACHIEVABLE: hints are keyed by pane id and
     prunePaneSessions reaps any whose pane is gone from every layout. The
     recovery route that does work is the Connect picker, which lists it.

VERIFIED-BEFORE-TOUCHING  A review flagged `PaneDisconnect{kill:false}` as
     wrongly routed to pane.kill-session. It is not — it calls pane.disconnect.
     Misread line. No change made.

CHECKS  289 cargo tests (6 new on this half), cargo check --workspace clean,
     npx tsc --noEmit clean.

NOT VERIFIED  Rule #14. Nothing here has run live. The whole checklist is in
     docs + the plan; the load-bearing ones are: kill a live session and
     confirm `zellij list-sessions -n` does not list it AT ALL; rename
     zellij.exe and confirm the failure toast + hint kept; reboot and confirm
     resurrection still works.

2026-08-20  Zellij lockdown, part 3: the session picker was append-only
WHY  Parts 1-2 left two decisions that both assume the user can get rid of a
     session: closing a pane deliberately leaves its zellij session running
     (Yossi's call, symmetric with SSH/WSL where X is a detach), and session
     restore deliberately does NOT filter EXITED rows (that is the reboot-
     resurrection path). Both are right, and both accumulate sessions the
     picker showed with a "resurrect" badge and NO way to act on. The list
     could raise the dead but never bury one.

WHAT zellij_delete_session(name) — same verb, same classifier as the pane
       path, no pane involved. Rejects an empty name. Registered in
       invoke_handler.
     PaneView picker: a trash button under the existing <Show when={s.exited}>,
       so it appears only for a row that is already dead.
     i18n en/he/ar/ru + .nc-resume-del styling (quiet until row hover).

THE HAZARD, named because it is one line away  The row's own onClick ATTACHES.
     Without e.stopPropagation() a delete would attach AND destroy in the same
     click. stopPropagation plus a confirm() are both there; the mis-click case
     (clicking the row body, not the button) is its own checklist item.

     The list refreshes by re-invoking pane_list_tmux_sessions rather than
     splicing the local array — the picker should show what is actually on the
     machine, including when the delete did NOT take.

ZELLIJ-ONLY ON PURPOSE  The button is gated on `exited`, and TmuxSessionInfo::
     exited is documented as always false on tmux. There is no tmux case.

CHECKS  26 zellij tests, tsc --noEmit clean, vite build clean.
NOT VERIFIED  Live: delete an EXITED row and confirm it is gone from
     `zellij list-sessions -n`; and click the row BODY to confirm that still
     attaches and destroys nothing.

2026-08-20  Zellij lockdown, part 4: the SSH kill was lying the same way
WHY  Kept separate on purpose — it touches a REMOTE path, and its failure mode
     is the opposite of the zellij one.

     `tmux kill-session -t <n> 2>&1 || true` forced exit 0, and the drain loop
     already received ChannelMsg::ExitStatus and threw it away. So the SSH arm
     could only ever claim success — the same shape of lie the zellij arm had,
     one layer down. It was carrying result:"attempted" as a placeholder since
     part 2 precisely because there was nothing honest to report.

WHAT Dropped `|| true`, KEPT `2>&1` (that is how tmux's own message reaches us
     instead of vanishing). The drain loop now keeps ChannelMsg::Data and
     binds ExitStatus. Four outcomes:
       transport error   -> failed   (could not reach the host; NOT a kill)
       exit 0            -> killed
       exit N + "can't find session" -> already_gone
       exit N otherwise  -> failed
       EOF/timeout, no ExitStatus -> attempted
     shell_quote stays: unlike every other verb here this one really does go
     through a remote shell, so Rule #3's argv rule has nothing to apply to.

"attempted" IS NOW A REAL THIRD STATE, and the frontend treats it as one:
     no error toast, and the restore hint is KEPT. Reasoning: if the session
     is really gone, the next start drops the hint when the name is not listed;
     if it survived, the hint is what brings it back. Self-correcting in both
     directions, which a toast would not be. The RPC still reports
     killed:false for it — an unproven kill must not print as a kill.

THE REGRESSION RISK, stated: if a server's tmux returns non-zero for a session
     that is ALREADY gone with wording other than "can't find session", this
     now reports `failed` where it used to report success — keeping the hint
     instead of dropping it. That is the safe direction (a stale [Connect]
     costs a click; a dropped hint costs the session), but it is a real
     behaviour change on SSH and needs the live check.

CHECKS  289 cargo tests, cargo check clean, tsc clean.
NOT VERIFIED  Live, and this one needs a real server: kill an SSH tmux pane
     (expect gone + killed), then kill one whose session is already gone
     (expect NOT `failed`).

2026-08-20  Release build + what the build actually proved (Rules #13, #14)
BUILT  `npm run tauri build -- --no-bundle` from app/ — 6m40s, exit 0,
       target/release/app.exe.

RULE #13 PASS  The current asset name `index-hIQeDN-r.js` IS present inside
       app.exe, so frontendDist is embedded and this is not a devUrl binary.

RESOURCES STAGED  target/release/resources/ymux-zellij.kdl AND
       target/release/resources/layouts/ymux.kdl both exist. Tauri PRESERVED
       the nested path rather than flattening it, so the `resources/layouts/*`
       glob fallback that was held in reserve is not needed. `setup --check`
       against the STAGED debug copy (not the source tree) exits 0 with
       [LAYOUT DIR] pointing at the staged layouts dir.

RULE #14 PARTIAL — and the partial is the point.
       Launched app.exe with YMUX_CONFIG_DIR pointed at a scratch dir so it
       could not touch Yossi's live install. It stayed up 20s and its log shows
       [UI:APP] longtask and [UI:SETTINGS] rtl-profiles lines — i.e. the
       FRONTEND RAN. That is the exact thing Rule #13 exists to catch: a devUrl
       build stops silently after "rpc server spawned" and never reaches a UI
       log line. This one did.

       Instance closed afterwards; it shares the RPC pipe name
       (\.\pipe\ymux-mlastudent371) with the running install, which is why it
       was not left up and why the RPC was NOT driven to create a pane.
       Verified after: Yossi's ymux.exe still running, and all 4 zellij
       sessions unchanged (same names, same ages, same EXITED marker).

STILL NOT VERIFIED, and none of it is inferable from the above:
       - zero chrome rows on a real pane (needs a pane on screen)
       - the `zellij config: … (+ layouts/ymux.kdl)` INFO line (only fires in
         spawn_local_pty, i.e. when a pane spawns)
       - Ctrl-o/p/t/s/g reaching the shell
       - wheel + drag-select under mouse_mode false  <- THE open measurement;
         if the wheel is dead the fallback is mouse_mode true
       - kill a live session -> absent from `list-sessions -n` entirely
       - rename zellij.exe -> failure toast + hint kept
       - reboot -> EXITED session still resurrects
       - picker trash button, and the mis-click case (row body still attaches)

2026-08-20  The wheel back, and Hebrew that pastes the right way round
THE MEASUREMENT THAT MADE THIS DECIDABLE — Yossi, side by side:

       pane              on screen     pasted elsewhere
       ----------------  -----------   ----------------
       plain PowerShell  REVERSED      correct
       Claude Code       correct       REVERSED

     Exactly inverted, which means the two panes hold OPPOSITE orders in the
     buffer. PowerShell's is LOGICAL — that is why its copy was already right.
     Claude Code's is VISUAL, and its display is right BECAUSE of that: when the
     Claude-in-front flag is set we render rows with `unicode-bidi:
     bidi-override`, which paints bytes verbatim, and verbatim visual bytes look
     correct. getSelection() hands back those same visual bytes.

     This killed a premise I had been carrying: "ConPTY delivers visual order on
     local panes". It does not — that is Claude Code, not the shell.

     It also VALIDATED the gate empirically: "Claude is in front" is precisely
     the bit that separates the two rows, so it is the right thing to flip on.

WHEEL  mouse_mode false -> TRUE. The `false` was half right: xterm.js does own
     selection, but its wheel scrolls the NORMAL buffer while zellij holds the
     alt screen — so with keybinds cleared there was no way to scroll back AT
     ALL. Not a trade, a missing feature. `true` is also what tmux already does
     here (`set -g mouse on`, decision O-3, docs/MOUSE-DEBUG.md), and the
     mouse-escape leak on the exit path is already handled by resetMouseModes()
     on connect and pty:exit — transport-agnostic, covers zellij.
     copy_on_select stays at its default true: with keybinds cleared it is the
     ONLY way to copy inside the pane. That is load-bearing, and commented.

COPY  app/src/copyBidi.ts (new) — visualToLogical(), pure, DOM-free.
     app/src/terminalInstance.ts — ALL FOUR copy paths now go through one
     `copyToClipboard(text, via)`: the OSC 52 provider, Ctrl+C-with-selection,
     Ctrl+Shift+C, and the right-click menu. They each called
     navigator.clipboard.writeText separately before, which is exactly how a fix
     lands for local panes and misses SSH ones.
     The OSC 52 provider was a MODULE SINGLETON with no pane context; it is now
     built per instance. Its write-only property (readText -> "") is preserved:
     a remote must never be able to answer-back an OSC 52 `?` query.

WHAT I GOT WRONG AND CORRECTED  I told Yossi that with zellij owning the mouse
     "it wrote to the clipboard over OSC52 and ymux never saw the text", and
     filed that in FOLLOWUPS. FALSE. ClipboardAddon has always routed OSC 52
     through our own provider (terminalInstance.ts). So mouse_mode was never a
     blocker for the clipboard work, and the wheel could come back for free.
     The FOLLOWUP is rewritten rather than quietly deleted.

THE BASE DIRECTION IS THE WHOLE TRICK, and it was measured, not guessed.
     An inverse only sees the REVERSED string, so the paragraph direction has to
     be decided in a way that gives the same answer for a line and its reverse.
     First-strong (UAX #9 P2/P3, what bidi.ts uses) does not: reordering can
     move a different strong char to the front. A COUNT does.
     Measured over a 12-line corpus, inverting output from a correct renderer:
       any-RTL-wins   9/12   (loses the mostly-Latin lines — Claude's own status
                              line "claude-fable-5 · ~ צבר·full", and
                              "Running שרת on port 80")
       majority vote 11/12   <- shipped, reusing strongCounts() from
                              textDirection.ts so "strong character" has ONE
                              definition in the app
     The 12th is provably impossible; see below.

PROVED UNFIXABLE, not hand-waved: two different logical strings share one visual
     form —
       "שלום עולם 123 abc"  -> "abc 123 םלוע םולש"
       "שלום עולם abc 123"  -> "abc 123 םלוע םולש"
     In logical order the digits and the Latin word are two level-2 runs split
     by a level-1 space; in visual order that space sits BETWEEN two level-2 runs
     and merges them. The encoding destroys the distinction. We return the first
     reading and pin it as a test so nobody "fixes" it later.

TWO BUGS FOUND IN THE EXISTING REORDER while writing the inverse:
     - bidi-js indexes CODE UNITS (`new Uint32Array(string.length)`), so
       Array.from() would have been silently off-by-N on any line with an emoji.
       copyBidi reverses in code-unit space and then repairs flipped surrogate
       pairs (a low surrogate followed by a high one can only have come from a
       reversal). bidi.ts's split("") has the same latent hazard, untouched here
       — it is the render path and out of scope for a clipboard commit.
     - niqqud: the forward pass moves a combining mark ahead of its base letter.
       The round trip restores it; pinned by a test, since it was unpinned.

ALSO  i18n en/he/ar/ru — the tui_owns_bidi checkbox still read "leave this OFF
     unless you run an old Claude" while the shipped local default is ON, and
     still said "winmux". Rewritten in all four to describe what it does.
     BACKLOG — the scrollback entry I appended on 2026-08-20 landed BELOW the
     "## Done" heading, so it read as done the day it was filed. Moved and
     genuinely closed, by the wheel rather than by the dump-screen viewer it
     proposed.

CHECKS  9 new JS tests + 125 existing (bidi 11, textDirection 69, mouseRtl 11,
     sessionRestore 15, fmPaths 19) all pass; tsc --noEmit clean; vite build
     clean; 26 zellij cargo tests pass (the include_str! config test caught the
     mouse_mode flip and was updated with it); `zellij setup --check` exit 0.

NOT VERIFIED  Rule #14 — nothing here has run live. The two that matter:
     the WHEEL actually scrolling in a new pane, and Hebrew from a Claude pane
     pasting correctly into Notepad WHILE Hebrew from a PowerShell prompt still
     pastes correctly (i.e. the gate held and we did not "fix" what was right).
     A pane created before this will not pick up mouse_mode — it is
     "(Requires restart)". Use a NEW pane.

STILL OPEN, and now isolated: the plain shell DISPLAYS Hebrew reversed. Its
     buffer is logical (proved by its copy being correct), so that is purely a
     renderer bug — auto_per_line's DOM renderer plus per-row `dir` should lay
     logical Hebrew out correctly and does not. FOLLOWUPS P2, now carrying the
     truth table as evidence. Same model as this fix, opposite sign.

2026-08-20  LIVE PASS on the wheel + copy build — verified, not just built
Yossi ran the 15:35 build (commit e40f427). Rule #14: this is the run, not the
compile.

WORKS
  - WHEEL SCROLLS BACK. This is the thing the whole mouse_mode reversal was for,
    and it is the one that could not be proved from here.
  - Copy on Shift+drag — copies the moment the selection is made.
  - Copy on Ctrl+C with a selection.
  - The copy paths agree with each other on the same text.
  - No mouse-escape garbage in the bare shell after leaving zellij, so the
    existing resetMouseModes() recovery does cover zellij's exit path.

DOES NOT WORK, filed P2 rather than fixed (Yossi: "less important for now")
  - RIGHT-CLICK Copy on a local zellij pane. Not a hole in the new plumbing —
    the other three paths share the same copyToClipboard and work. It is a
    consequence of `mouse_mode true`: zellij now owns the mouse, so either the
    contextmenu event never reaches our listener, or the menu opens with its
    Copy item disabled because `term.getSelection()` is empty (the selection
    belongs to zellij, not to xterm.js). Both causes are consistent with Shift
    being what makes the working paths work. See FOLLOWUPS.

NOT RE-TESTED THIS PASS  the Hebrew round trip itself (Claude pane pastes
  correctly AND PowerShell pane still pastes correctly). Yossi confirmed the
  copy MECHANISM on the paths above; the bidi correctness check is the next
  thing to look at, and it is the reason copyBidi.ts exists.

BUILD NOTE, worth remembering  the first rebuild FAILED with `failed to remove
  file ... app.exe / Access is denied (os error 5)` because Yossi had the
  previous build open. That is the documented case in CLAUDE.md — ask, do not
  retry. Also: `npm run tauri build | tail` reports TAIL's exit code, so the
  task notification said "exit code 0" on a failed build. Check the output text,
  not the status.

2026-08-20  Right-aligned Hebrew in Claude — normalise on the way IN
GOAL  Local Claude panes showed correct letters but pinned to the LEFT. Yossi
     wanted them on the right, and remembered it had worked once.

WHAT THE HISTORY SAID, and it was worth reading before writing code. His own
     words on 2026-08-18 23:08: "BIDI מציג אותיות ישרות מיושר לימין" — so the
     target state existed. But the git archaeology found it was reachable only
     by ACCIDENT: before bc12388 the renderer was frozen at construct while
     flushPending read the live mode, so a pane built as auto_per_line (DOM +
     per-row dir) and then switched to bidi_reorder got BOTH our reorder AND
     dir="rtl" — two reversals that cancelled. bc12388 closed that hole and the
     result became unreproducible.

THE MEASUREMENT THAT KILLED MY FIRST DESIGN  I had planned
     reorderRtlForDisplay(visualToLogical(x)) in flushPending. Probed it against
     four simulated Claude lines before writing any of it:
         claude    [םלוע םולש                     ]
         relayout  [םלוע םולש                     ]   right-placed=false
     reorder(unreorder(V)) === V on every case. **reorderRtlForDisplay reorders
     runs IN PLACE and does no padding — it can never move a line to the right
     edge.** The approved plan's Route B would have been a no-op. Deleted.

THE ACTUAL RULE, measured, three rows:
       buffer   render          letters  placement
       visual   dir="rtl"       wrong    RIGHT
       visual   bidi-override   RIGHT    left      <- what shipped before
       LOGICAL  dir="rtl"       RIGHT    RIGHT     <- this. And what REMOTE has
                                                     always done.
     Placement comes from the browser under dir="rtl", and the browser only gets
     the letters right if it is handed LOGICAL text. So: convert on the way in,
     then stop treating the pane as special.

WHAT  copyBidi.ts — visualToLogicalStream(), the ANSI-aware sibling of
       visualToLogical. The clipboard one is escape-BLIND on purpose (a
       selection has no escapes); hand it a PTY chunk and it permutes a CSI
       sequence into garbage. The new one walks escapes exactly the way
       reorderRtlForDisplay does. ANSI_RE is now exported from bidi.ts so there
       is ONE definition of "what an escape looks like".
     terminalInstance.ts —
       - normaliseIncomingToLogical getter (bidiOwnedByTui && auto_per_line;
         the other two modes are WebGL, which has no dir and so nothing to gain)
       - flushPending writes visualToLogicalStream(merged) for those panes
       - applyRowDirections stops forcing dir="ltr" + bidi-override for them —
         that pairing is what pinned Hebrew left, since right placement can only
         come from dir="rtl"
       - bufferIsVisualOrder subtracts the normalised case FIRST, or the
         clipboard would flip already-logical text and re-break the bug fixed
         this morning. That invariant said "the two never stack"; it now has to.

LEFT ALONE DELIBERATELY  isCurrentLineRtl still bails on bidiOwnedByTui. The
     obvious reading after this change is that it should not — wrong. Normalising
     changes what OUR buffer holds, not what CLAUDE thinks it holds; Claude still
     edits in visual columns, so mirroring arrows would still fight it. Comment
     expanded rather than the code changed.

THE ESCAPE HATCH, since there is no new setting: rtl_mode="off" gives WebGL
     verbatim = correct letters, left aligned = exactly today's behaviour. If the
     caret turns out unusable, that is the fallback — not a revert.

TESTS  6 new (15 total in copyBidi.test.ts): escapes byte-for-byte, an OSC
     sequence not permuted, no-RTL identity, runs split by escapes, a mid-line
     fragment stays lossless, and stream==clipboard when there are no escapes.
     All six JS suites green (140 total), tsc + vite clean.

NOT VERIFIED  Rule #14. The three that matter: Hebrew from Claude correct AND
     right; the CARET while typing Hebrew (the known-risky one); and Claude's
     box-drawing rows not flipping — 770013b is the regression to watch.

        off Windows instead of silently doing nothing.

RESULT: ci-windows green on 82.A+82.E (run 32376684962). The FIRST mac run
(32376684988) failed on one test — utf8_shell_tests::shell_kinds_route_to_
the_right_args — which had only ever been compiled on Windows, where it
passed vacuously. Real finding, not a test artifact: detect_shell_kind took
the stem with Path::file_stem, which honours only the host separator, so a
Windows shell path classifies as Posix off Windows and gets no UTF-8
preamble. Fixed by normalizing separators first, the idiom path_basename
already uses. 273 of 274 passed otherwise — the merge itself compiled clean
on macOS first try.
STATUS: compiles-and-tests-on-CI. NOT run live on a Mac — rule 14. The
Keychain refusal, chmod perms and hooks-over-socket have no CI test that
exercises them and need a real Mac before release.

2026-08-23 | fix(ci): the mac build's ad-hoc path could never bundle
Files: .github/workflows/build-macos-intel.yml

Run 32421631034 (claude/macos-universal-signing, workflow_dispatch) failed
in BOTH matrix legs at "Build (.app bundle)":

    Signing with identity ""
    error: The specified item could not be found in the keychain.
    failed to bundle project failed codesign application

So the per-arch signing branch has never produced a bundle without the
Developer ID secrets — i.e. the ad-hoc fallback it was written to provide
was broken on arrival. `main` is NOT affected: its copy of the workflow
predates the signing block (last dispatch on main, 32408062153, green).

Cause: the step's comment claimed tauri "falls back to the config's '-'
when APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY is empty". It does not. tauri-cli reads the
variable with env::var_os, so SET-BUT-EMPTY is Some("") and it wins over
"signingIdentity": "-" in tauri.macos.conf.json. A YAML `env:` entry
defines the variable even when the `... || ''` expression yields empty —
which is exactly the ad-hoc case — so tauri tried to codesign with an
identity literally named "", and codesign went looking for it in the
keychain. The log shows the mode step resolving correctly (HAVE_IDENTITY
empty -> adhoc, cert import skipped) and the empty var reaching the build
anyway, which is what makes the cause unambiguous.

Fix: the ad-hoc path now `unset`s the variable in the step's shell instead
of blanking it, and echoes which path it took. Blank-vs-unset is the whole
bug, so the step says out loud which one it is.

VERIFIED: run 32639916194, both legs green, first time the matrix has ever
completed.
  build (arm64)  macos-14        8m37s
  build (x64)    macos-15-intel  18m12s
The build step now prints `ad-hoc: APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY unset, tauri will
use signingIdentity "-"`, and codesign reports `Signature=adhoc`,
`TeamIdentifier=not set` — the fallback the workflow was written to provide
is finally the one that runs.

Everything downstream of the bundle ran for the first time on arm64 and
passed: cargo test 274, frontend embedded ('index-Cyk9sJto.js', Rule #13),
lipo on both the .app main executable AND the bundled ymux-cli -> arm64
only. So the anti-Rosetta claim behind the arch split is now actually
checked on the shipped bundle rather than asserted.

  ymux_arm64_macos13.dmg  ca837af1dc743c8df424a3a9750ccfcabe4ecb5435bc47079aafdef45a6bc968
  ymux_x64_macos13.dmg    5375016a65f47c4c10084c081229d629435db41026bb7331ae7a4f09f941c153

spctl rejects both, by design: no Developer ID secrets are configured on
the repo, so `Resolve signing mode` picks adhoc and the cert import is
skipped. The workflow downgrades that to a warning in the adhoc path and
would fail the run in the developer-id path. These dmgs are buildable and
runnable locally but NOT distributable — new followup.

Rule #14 still applies: green CI is not a launched app. Nothing here ran
the .app on a real Mac.

2026-08-23 | Phase 83 — the Go server enters the CI pipeline
Files: .github/workflows/ci-windows.yml, .gitattributes, CLAUDE.md,
docs/ymux-server/README.md, docs/DECISIONS.md, FOLLOWUPS.md

Yossi: "the SERVER should also go through BUILD if we changed something in
its code — no reason it's not in the CI pipe." Two holes, the second worse
than the first.

1. The server WAS compiled in CI, for the wrong platform. `go test ./...`
   runs on windows-latest, i.e. GOOS=windows. What ships is
   resources/ymux-server-linux-{x64,arm64}. A linux-only break — a syscall
   arm, a //go:build tag, a dep without windows/linux parity — was
   invisible until a human cross-compiled at release time.

2. Those two binaries are COMMITTED BLOBS baked by hand
   (docs/ymux-server/README.md § Build; the manual step is even minuted at
   docs/DECISIONS.md:298). src/addons.rs `include_bytes!`s them, so nothing
   in the desktop build ever reads the Go source. Change a handler, skip the
   rebake, and every job is green while every remote gets the OLD server.
   Nothing anywhere said so.

Added to ci-windows.yml:
  - `go vet ./...`
  - cross-build linux/amd64 + linux/arm64 with the release flags from
    README § Build (-trimpath -ldflags="-s -w", CGO_ENABLED=0). Built with
    `-o <dir>/ ./...` as well as ./cmd, so packages main does not import are
    compiled for linux too. ~30s, CGO-free.
  - upload both as the `ymux-server-linux` artifact, which makes a rebake
    "download and commit" instead of "install Go locally" (Rule #17).
  - THE GATE: fail if `server/**.{go,mod,sum}` (minus _test.go) changed
    between the base commit and HEAD while
    resources/ymux-server-linux-{x64,arm64} did not. Two-dot diff against a
    shallow-fetched base — the checkout is depth-1, and on a PR the checked
    out ref is already base-merged so two-dot IS the PR diff.
  - `sdk-gen/ci-check.mjs` (the SDK/OpenAPI drift-guard). Closes the root
    cause of the FOLLOWUPS P2 from 2026-08-18, where ~670 lines of stale
    generated output rode into the rename commit because nothing checked.

DELIBERATELY NOT a byte-compare of the committed blob against the fresh
cross-build. Go output is reproducible only for a fixed toolchain patch
version, and setup-go resolves `go 1.26` to whatever 1.26.x is current, so
that check would go red on a Go point release for a reason unrelated to the
change. The source-drift gate is exact and has no such failure mode.

Also, found while reading .gitattributes: `* text=auto eol=lf` covers the
whole tree and the three extensionless shipped binaries (ymux-cli,
ymux-linux-x64, ymux-server-linux-*) match no `binary` rule. They survive
today only because git sniffs a NUL in the ELF header. Named them
explicitly — a heuristic is not what should stand between us and a
corrupted 12MB blob.

Current state: the committed blobs are NOT stale. Both are from 90626bb
(2026-08-19, the rename) and `git log 90626bb..HEAD -- app/src-tauri/server/`
is empty. So this is a gate, not a fix.

VERIFIED ON CI (PR #14, ci-windows run 32640896350, SUCCESS). Every new
step green on the first try, including the two that could only be settled
by running them: `go vet ./...` is clean on the current tree (it had not
run since the rename), and sdk-gen/ci-check.mjs is green on a fresh Windows
checkout — it shells `go run ./cmd/ymux-server openapi` and diffs generated
output, and the .gitattributes LF normalisation held. The cross-build
produced the `ymux-server-linux` artifact (10,524,795 bytes zipped, both
arches). The drift gate reported "no shipping Go source changed" on this
PR's own diff, which is correct.

Rule #14 still applies to the SERVER ITSELF: nothing here ran the produced
binary. What is verified is that it compiles for linux/amd64 and
linux/arm64 and that the gate fires on the right inputs.

origin/main moved 70 commits under this branch while CI was running (the

2026-08-23 | Phase 84.C - Monitor > Analytics tab (historical metrics)
Task: Yossi asked for a screen like the dev-pipeline dashboard's /efficiency
page inside the Monitor card (the ניטור window, InsightsWindow.tsx). Ported the
SHAPE of that page, not its data: stat tiles + a hand-rolled SVG sparkline +
inline-bar rollup tables, no chart library, no build step of its own.

THE DATA WAS ALREADY THERE AND NOBODY READ IT. The insights daemon's sampler
writes samples / disk_samples / docker_samples to SQLite on every 5s tick and
sweeps to a 7-day window (internal/insights/store.go). `/history` was the only
reader and the FRONTEND NEVER CALLED IT ONCE. disk_samples and docker_samples
had no reader at all.

Why a new endpoint instead of /history:
  * /history is raw rows with LIMIT 2000 and ORDER BY ts ASC. At the 5s sample
    interval that is 2.8 hours. Ask it for 7 days and it silently answers with
    the OLDEST 2.8 hours of the range. Nobody would have noticed - the chart
    renders fine, it is just answering a different question.
  * Every desktop fetch is one curl over the workspace SSH session with
    --max-time 6 (addons.rs insights_fetch). Five series = five round trips.
    So the whole screen has to come back in ONE response.

Files:
  NEW app/src-tauri/server/internal/insights/analytics.go      (~370)
      GET /analytics?since=&until=&points= - totals + bucketed series +
      by_period / by_disk / by_container, all aggregated in SQL. Buckets by
      hour under 48h and by day above it. Every param CLAMPED, never rejected
      (the desktop drew the range picker; it should not have to render a
      validation error for its own widget). Timestamps go out as unix seconds
      so the desktop formats them in the VIEWER's timezone, not the server's -
      the reason there is no strftime() anywhere in this file.
  NEW app/src-tauri/server/internal/insights/analytics_test.go (~230)
      Seeded store with predictable ramps. The load-bearing one is
      TestAnalyticsLongWindowCoversTheEnd: 4320 rows (> the /history cap), and
      it asserts the LAST bucket lands at the window end. That is the bug this
      endpoint exists to prevent, and it is invisible by eye.
  MOD internal/insights/service.go     - route registered on legacy + /api/v2.
  MOD app/src-tauri/src/insights_local.rs - /analytics returns
      {"unavailable":"local"}. A local workspace samples on demand and stores
      NOTHING, so there is no history to roll up; the panel says so instead of
      leaking a raw "unsupported path" string.
  NEW app/src/InsightsAnalytics.tsx    (~600) - the tab.
  NEW app/src/insightsFmt.ts           - fmtBytes/fmtBps/fmtPct/fmtSpan,
      lifted OUT of InsightsWindow so the new tab can share them without
      importing back into its own parent (that would be a cycle).
  MOD app/src/InsightsWindow.tsx       - 6th tab, IconHistory, lazy-mounted.
  MOD app/src/App.css                  - .ins-an-* block.
  MOD app/src/i18n/{en,he,ar,ru}.json  - +50 keys each, all four at 1168.
  MOD docs/ymux-server/API.md          - documents /analytics and says plainly
      when NOT to use /history.

Design rules kept from the source page, on purpose:
  * NO POLL. Loads on tab open and on Refresh. Metrics owns the live view.
  * Aggregation server-side, one round trip. Never pull rows and sum here.
  * One colour for every bar (--w-accent), because a bar means MAGNITUDE.
    Amber is reserved for the two things that are actually a problem: a disk
    past 85%, and a container whose uptime over the window is under 95%.
  * Every number gets --w-font-mono + direction:ltr, or columns of digits
    jitter row to row and RTL flips them.
  * The sparkline has a <details> "as a table" twin. A bare sparkline is
    unreadable to a screen reader.

Two things worth knowing for next time:
  * The stroke needs vector-effect: non-scaling-stroke. preserveAspectRatio
    ="none" is what makes the chart responsive with no resize listener, and it
    stretches the line into a wedge without it.
  * Narrow-mode column dropping uses createNarrow (ResizeObserver on the
    panel), NOT a media query. This panel is routinely a 440px drawer on a
    2560px monitor - a viewport query would measure the wrong box.

Status: NOT VERIFIED LIVE (Rule 14). Not compiled either - Rule 17, builds run
on CI. Needs the server-blob rebake (see below) and then a real remote.
2026-08-23 — TMUX organization: workspace-scoped session lists + attach-only
Branch: claude/tmux-workspace-scope

TASK (Yossi, Hebrew): now that folders can be opened under a workspace, put
the TMUX side in order — show the TMUX sessions of the SPECIFIC workspace as
distinct from the server's; and stop the connect wizard from opening tmux,
sending commands and pressing ENTER when the pane is already MOUNTED to a
session, which either restarts an old session or drops a command into the
middle of a live one.

PART A — SCOPE
  - TMUX_LIST_FORMAT gained a 6th field, `#{session_path}`. TMUX_LIST_SCRIPT
    was a const that hand-repeated the format string while its own doc claimed
    "one const means the format string cannot drift again" — the two DID drift
    the moment the field was added, so it is now `fn tmux_list_script()` built
    from the const. `parse_tmux_sessions` reads `parts.get(5)`, so a five-field
    line from an un-rebootstrapped remote still parses (cwd = None = UNKNOWN,
    which is deliberately not the same as "elsewhere").
  - NEW `%APPDATA%\ymux\session-owners.json`, modelled line-for-line on
    tmux-labels.json (atomic .tmp+rename, Rule #7; corrupt file degrades to
    "nothing owned"). Keyed host_key -> session_name -> {workspace_id, cwd, ts}.
    Host first because a session name is only unique per multiplexer server.
    Written on every persistent connect INCLUDING a picker attach; released on
    kill (`killed`/`already_gone` only — `failed` keeps the claim because the
    session is still alive).
    It exists because zellij reports NO cwd, so on Windows ownership is the
    only workspace signal there is.
  - `pane_list_tmux_sessions` takes `project_path: Option<String>` — the same
    parameter name `pane_list_claude_sessions` already uses — and ANNOTATES
    each row with `owned` / `in_cwd` instead of filtering. `path_is_within`
    compares on a separator boundary so `/srv/app2` is not "inside" `/srv/app`.
  - Picker gained a This folder | Whole server toggle plus a count line.
    It opens on "This folder" only when that view has something in it.

PART B — ATTACH-ONLY
  - `resolve_effective_session_name` / `session_name_for_pane` extracted from
    pane_connect so the guard and the attach cannot check different names.
  - NEW `pane_target_session_state(workspace_id, pane_id, tmux_session_name)`
    -> {name, exists, attached, reachable}. `reachable` is not decoration:
    "we could not ask" must never read as "nothing is running".
  - `pane_connect` probes BEFORE spawning (the spawn is what creates the
    session; 200ms later the question is useless) and, for a persistent pane
    joining a live session, injects NOTHING — command and cd alike — emits
    `pane-connect-notice`, and logs an `attach-only:` line.
    The probe is gated on "would we inject anything at all?" so a plain
    connect does not pay for a list round-trip on the critical path.
  - The blind `sleep(1100ms)` for tmux is replaced by 900ms + a poll for the
    session to APPEAR (the same fix zellij got on 2026-08-19), so a genuinely
    new session's command lands inside tmux instead of racing the attach line.
  - Wizard: stops hard-coding `ncType("tmux")`; probes on open; banner names
    the session, command controls grey out, and "Start a new session instead"
    picks a free `<name>-N` off the live list so the command can run for real.

FOUND AND FIXED, NOT DRIVE-BY: `is_local -> list_zellij_sessions()` was
ungated in BOTH `pane_list_tmux_sessions` and `pane_probe_tmux_sessions`.
Both arms return unconditionally for a local workspace, so they shadowed the
`cfg(not(windows))` macOS branches below them: a Mac asked zellij (not
installed there), got [], and never reached its own tmux server. The new
picker is built on that call, so on macOS it would have been silently empty.
Now cfg-split. Pre-existing on main since 2026-08-19.

ALSO: deleted 14 dead `tmux_picker.*` i18n keys x 4 languages (zero refs in
app/src, left over from a picker UI removed long ago). New keys added to all
four locales.

TESTS ADDED (they run on CI, per Rule #17 — nothing was built locally):
  format_has_six_pipe_separated_fields, list_script_is_built_from_the_format_const,
  sixth_field_is_the_session_cwd, five_field_lines_still_parse_with_unknown_cwd,
  empty_sixth_field_reads_as_unknown_not_root, folder_scope_stops_at_a_separator,
  session_name_precedence_is_explicit_then_title_then_pane_id,
  pane_fallback_name_matches_the_legacy_auto_name.

STATUS: compiles-untested until CI is green, and Rule #14 (not "verified")
until the FOLLOWUPS P1 smoke list runs on a real machine.

2026-08-23 (session close) | worktree + branches removed
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Containment re-checked at deletion time, not against the morning's plan
-- which is the exact mistake the P2 above records: working tree clean,
`origin/main..HEAD` empty, and both branches ancestors of `origin/main`.
Deleted: worktree .claude/worktrees/mac-build-broken-b59d67, local
branches claude/mac-build-broken-b59d67 + claude/macos-universal-signing,
and remote origin/claude/macos-universal-signing (47dfab0, contained in
main a87c764). Two worktrees from that P2 remain, untouched: another
session may still be live in them.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-23 (session close) | worktree cleanup, and one mistake worth recording
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Done: branch claude/macos-universal-signing deleted local + remote, and
claude/mac-build-broken-b59d67 deleted locally. The worktree was
deregistered and emptied; only the locked empty DIRECTORY remains (P3).

THE MISTAKE, because it will otherwise be repeated. A cleanup command was
written as `cd <worktree> && ... && git add -A && git commit`. By the time
it ran, `git worktree remove` had already emptied that directory, so the
`cd` landed in a now-nonexistent path, git walked UP to the enclosing main
checkout -- .claude/worktrees/ lives inside it -- and `git add -A` staged
every untracked file there: four local test binaries, ~264MB, committed to
local `main` under a docs commit message.

Caught before any push. `git reset --mixed origin/main` un-did it with the
files untouched on disk, then FOLLOWUPS/PROGRESS were restored from HEAD
after verifying the on-disk copies were merely stale (identical to
e7c6e90) and not someone else's uncommitted work.

Two rules earned:
  1. Never `git add -A` in a shared checkout. Stage explicit paths. `-A`
     has no blast radius limit and the main checkout is not clean-by-
     construction -- it holds the user's own untracked artifacts.
  2. A `cd` that fails does not stop a `&&` chain when the shell falls
     back to another directory. Verify the working directory INSIDE the
     command that mutates git state, not before it.

2026-08-23 | Phase 84.D - "Copy for Claude" on the Analytics tab
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Yossi's follow-up, and the right question: if everything is already stored, is
there a simple place to hand it to Claude and ask what happened? There is now -
one button, top-right of the Analytics tab.

  NEW app/src/insightsReport.ts       - PURE, DOM-free. Owns the /analytics wire
      types (moved out of the .tsx, which now imports them) and flattens a
      report into fixed-width plain text: header + totals + the full bucketed
      series + the three rollups.
  NEW app/src/insightsReport.test.ts  - 12 node:test cases, ALL RUN AND PASSING
      locally (a sub-second pure-function test, not a build - Rule 17 is about
      cargo/vite/tsc occupying the machine and faking the matrix).
  NEW app/src/clipboardText.ts        - copyText(): navigator.clipboard with the
      execCommand fallback older WebView2 needs.
  MOD InsightsAnalytics.tsx / InsightsWindow.tsx / App.css / i18n x4 (+5 keys
      each, all four now at 1173).

THE TESTS EARNED THEIR KEEP IMMEDIATELY. First run, 3 failures, and one was a
real defect rather than a wrong expectation: the report was calling the UI's
`fmtPct`, which rounds away the decimal above 10% so live columns stop jittering
between refreshes. Correct on screen; in a report meant to be REASONED about,
"23%" instead of "23.4%" is precision thrown away for nothing. The report now
has its own one-decimal `pct()` and the comment says why it is not shared.

Deliberate choices:
  * WYSIWYG. The button copies the range you are looking at and does not
    quietly fetch a different one.
  * Absolute `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM` stamps, hand-rolled rather than Intl - the
    string is machine-read as much as human-read, so it must not change shape
    with the UI language. The header says out loud that they are the LOCAL time
    of the DESKTOP, not the server; without that line every spike gets misdated.
  * The whole series ships (120 rows ~ a few KB). The shape over time IS the
    question; summarising it here would be answering it for the reader.
  * The report states what is NOT in it - no command output, no file contents,
    no credentials - so nobody has to guess whether pasting it leaks a terminal.
    Rule 1 holds by construction: this path only ever sees machine metrics.

Status: report formatting VERIFIED by unit test. The button itself is NOT
verified live - clipboard-write inside WebView2 for this panel is exactly the
kind of thing that only a real run settles. Rolled into the FOLLOWUPS P1.

2026-08-23 | Phase 84.E - Claude usage + cost analysis (the thing Yossi
                          actually asked for)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Yossi asked whether the Claude usage/cost/API analysis had been added. Checked
the code rather than guessing. IT HAD NOT, AND IT DID NOT EXIST:

  * Monitor > Claude ran `claude -p /usage` and showed SUBSCRIPTION QUOTA
    PERCENTAGES only. No cost, no tokens, no call counts - and NO HISTORY at
    all (5-min cache, one point in time; yesterday's number was simply gone).
  * The closest thing to data was a free-text line, "Last 24h - 3466 requests -
    10 sessions". A string, not a dataset.
  * Phase 84.C (Analytics) had answered "what did the MACHINE do". The question
    was "what did CLAUDE cost".

Same story as the metrics DB, one layer over: THE DATA WAS ALREADY ON DISK AND
NOBODY READ IT. claude_log.rs (Phase 24.A, dormant since 24.D removed its UI)
already SFTP-mirrors ~/.claude/projects/**/*.jsonl - and its parser reads
text/tool_name/timestamp and THROWS THE usage BLOCK AWAY. Verified against a
real transcript on this machine, not from memory: every assistant line carries
message.model plus message.usage with input/output/cache_read, the
cache_creation 5m-vs-1h SPLIT, service_tier and speed, alongside sessionId,
cwd, gitBranch and isSidechain.

Files:
  NEW server/internal/insights/claudeusage.go (+ _test.go, 9 tests)
      GET /claude-usage - walks the transcripts, rolls up by hour / model+speed
      / project / session, splits main-loop vs subagent. The mtime prune is the
      whole trick: a transcript mtime is its LAST append, so a file older than
      the window cannot hold an in-window line and is never opened. That is what
      makes 240 MB affordable inside a 6s curl.
  NEW src/claude_usage_local.rs (+ 4 tests) - the local mirror, same JSON shape,
      same clamping. Two implementations of one aggregation is real duplication
      and it is the CHEAPER option: the alternative is SFTP-pulling hundreds of
      MB to the desktop every time the tab opens.
  NEW app/src/claudePricing.ts (+ 16 tests) - THE ONLY PLACE PRICES LIVE. Both
      backends count tokens and deliberately do not price them.
  NEW app/src/InsightsClaudeCost.tsx - the panel, under the quota bars.
  NEW app/src/insightsCommands.ts - "copy investigation commands" for BOTH tabs.
  MOD insightsReport.ts - a second pasteable report, for usage.
  MOD App.tsx - passes `local` down so the command block names the right paths.

DECISIONS THAT MATTER, and why:
  * COST IS AN ESTIMATE AND THE UI SAYS SO THREE TIMES. Claude Code on a
    subscription is NOT billed per token. Every figure is API-list-price applied
    to real token counts: right for comparing projects/models/sessions, WRONG
    for predicting a charge. Getting this framing wrong would make the whole
    feature a lie, so the disclaimer LEADS the panel rather than trailing it.
  * Prices came from the claude-api skill, not from memory - that skill exists
    precisely because a training-prior price is a plausible wrong number.
  * The 5m/1h cache-write split is carried end to end. A 1h write costs 2x base
    input against a 5m write 1.25x; collapsing them understates any long
    session, and an agentic workload is mostly cache writes.
  * Sonnet 5 intro pricing is time-bounded IN THE TABLE (until 2026-08-31), and
    cost is computed from WHEN the tokens were spent. Pricing a week-old call at
    today's rate would overstate it by 50%.
  * An unknown model costs 0 and is FLAGGED as unpriced. A wrong dollar figure
    is worse than an honest blank.
  * Only by_model knows its model; day/project/session rows can mix several, so
    they use a token-weighted BLENDED rate and are marked "~". Exact with one
    model in the window, honest approximation with several.
  * Local days are rolled up client-side from hourly server buckets - the only
    way a day boundary lands where the reader clock says it does.

Status: 28 frontend tests + 9 Go tests + 4 Rust tests, all written; the
frontend ones run and pass here. NOT VERIFIED LIVE (Rule 14).
2026-08-23 | Phase 82.E | macOS: site JS dead in the in-app Browser - DIAGNOSTIC
================================================================================
Symptom (Yossi): on macOS the workspace Browser renders a page but the site's
own JavaScript does not run. Windows is fine. No logs, no Web Inspector, and
Rule #17 means no local mac build - so the diagnosis had to ship in the binary.

RULED OUT BY READING, BEFORE ANY CODE:
  - Entitlements. Entitlements.plist already has allow-jit +
    allow-unsigned-executable-memory (same commit as hardenedRuntime, 048f1b2),
    and the MAIN window is itself a WKWebView that renders - JSC works.
  - CSP. tauri.conf.json is "csp": null, and Tauri CSP does not touch an
    external URL anyway.
  - javascript_disabled. Defaults false in tauri-runtime-2.10.1.
  - workspace_browser.rs has ZERO cfg(target_os) arms - the WebView2-shaped
    creation lock, the 3x 0x8007139F retry, the shared user-data-dir, all of it
    runs unchanged under WKWebView.

WHAT SHIPPED (a probe, not a fix):
  app/src-tauri/src/browser_diag.js  NEW, 9.1 KB, strict ES5, pure ASCII.
    Document-start probe. Four beacons: boot (userAgent, readyState, globals
    baseline), feat (feature + `new Function` syntax probes for the Safari-16
    level), scripts (per <script src>: scheme/host/loopback/same-origin + a
    header-only fetch, no-cors for cross-origin - that is what catches an ATS
    block), report (globals delta, body length, first 3 errors, script resource
    timings). ES5 is not a style choice: modern syntax here would be a parse
    error on old WebKit, i.e. the probe would manufacture the evidence for its
    own bug. Feature probes therefore go through new Function in try/catch.
  app/src-tauri/src/workspace_browser.rs
    - initialization_script(DIAG_SCRIPT), on_document_title_changed,
      on_page_load (Started/Finished, zero JS involved), a Rust-side eval probe
      300 ms after Finished, and an 8 s watchdog that logs when NOTHING arrives.
    - handle_diag_title + decode_diag, beside the existing ticket bridge, which
      is untouched. Beacons are re-serialized through serde_json (raw newlines
      are legal JSON whitespace - a page could otherwise forge log lines),
      truncated on a char boundary at 900 bytes, and capped at 24 per webview
      because the PAGE controls document.title.
    - 6 unit tests in diag_title_tests: ordinary titles inert, btoa-style
      padded base64 round-trips through base64_decode, single-line
      normalisation, non-JSON rejected, char-boundary truncation, budget
      saturates at zero without wrapping.
    - Corrected a wrong comment: Tauri 2.10.3 DOES inject __TAURI_INTERNALS__
      into external-URL webviews. The capability layer (Origin::Remote vs a
      Local execution context) is what denies the tunneled page, not the
      absence of injection.
  app/src-tauri/Cargo.toml, app/src-tauri/src/lib.rs
    - tauri feature "devtools" added: the ONLY thing that makes wry call
      setInspectable(true), i.e. the only way Safari can attach in release.
      DANGER, and the reason the three edits are one unit: tauri-runtime-wry
      reads it as devtools.unwrap_or(true), so the feature alone would make
      EVERY webview inspectable - including main, which renders live PTY output
      (Rule #1). Explicit .devtools(false) on the main and popout builders;
      only the Browser child opts in, and only on macOS.
  .github/workflows/build-macos-intel.yml
    - The verify step's two `|| true` prints became assertions: hardened-runtime
      flag present, all six entitlement keys == true, app-sandbox absent,
      Info.plist merged (NSAppSleepDisabled). Closes the plumbing half of the
      FOLLOWUPS P1 "hardened runtime has never been launched" gap - a build that
      silently dropped Entitlements.plist used to go green.

WHY document.title AND NOT THE EXISTING ymux-ticket: BRIDGE (read out of
wry 0.54.4, not assumed):
  - fetch/<img> cannot reach on_navigation: it is decidePolicyForNavigationAction,
    frame navigations only. Subresources never touch it.
  - an iframe beacon works on mac but is silently dead on Windows - webview2
    hooks add_NavigationStarting, never FrameNavigationStarting. That would have
    cost us the Windows control run.
  - location.href is a main-frame navigation fired in the exact window we are
    trying to measure.
  - title change is symmetric: KVO on the `title` keypath on mac
    (document_title_changed_observer.rs:41), add_DocumentTitleChanged on Windows
    (webview2/mod.rs:623), exposed as WebviewBuilder::on_document_title_changed.
  The probe restores the original title on the next task, and emits one beacon
  per task turn because WebKit coalesces title notifications within one task.

NOT cfg-gated to macOS, deliberately: a mac-only probe would first execute on
the one machine we cannot debug, making "no beacon" ambiguous between "engine
dead" and "probe broken". Windows is the control run.

STATUS: compiles-untested until CI says otherwise; per Rule #14 the real
verification is Windows first (capture the baseline), then the Mac. Verdict
table and the exact user steps are in the plan file and docs/DECISIONS.md.

Files: app/src-tauri/src/browser_diag.js (new), app/src-tauri/src/workspace_browser.rs,
app/src-tauri/src/lib.rs, app/src-tauri/Cargo.toml,
.github/workflows/build-macos-intel.yml, docs/DECISIONS.md, FOLLOWUPS.md

2026-08-23 | Phase 82.F | DevTools on the Browser webview, both platforms
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Yossi: "so maybe add DEVTOOLS - so we can see everything including errors."

It was already half there: 82.E enabled the tauri `devtools` feature (the only
thing that makes wry call setInspectable(true)) but wired it to
`.devtools(cfg!(target_os = "macos"))` with no UI, so on Windows there was no
inspector at all and on macOS you had to walk Safari -> Develop -> machine ->
webview. Both fixed.

  workspace_browser.rs  .devtools(cfg!(target_os = "macos")) -> .devtools(true).
                        A Windows control run you cannot inspect is worth much
                        less than one you can, and the whole point of not
                        cfg-gating the probe was to make Windows the baseline.
                        New command `workspace_browser_open_devtools`: the
                        command always exists, only its body is cfg'd, so the
                        frontend needs no knowledge of the build shape.
  lib.rs                registered in generate_handler!.
  BrowserWindow.tsx     `openDevtools()` + a terminal-icon button beside the
                        Dev Mode bug toggle. No lit state - the inspector is its
                        own window, there is nothing to advertise.
  App.css               .bw-devtools-btn (spacing only).
  i18n en/he/ar/ru      "browser.devtools.open".

SCOPE HELD DELIBERATELY: main window and popouts keep their explicit
.devtools(false), and the F12 / Ctrl+Shift+I blocker at App.tsx:3184 stays.
The feature makes tauri read devtools.unwrap_or(true), so every webview is
inspectable unless it opts out - that is the trap, and those two calls are the
whole guard. A new webview added later inherits `true`.

Justification for the Browser being the exception: it renders a tunneled
third-party service, not ymux's own surface. An inspector there exposes nothing
of ours, and on macOS isInspectable only means the app appears in Safari's
Develop menu on the same machine.

STATUS: compiles-untested until CI. This does NOT replace the 82.E beacons -
devtools is the live console when you are sitting in front of the machine; the
beacons are what work when you are not, which is the actual situation with the
Mac.

2026-08-23 | Phase 84 | panes as tabs (84.A) + Claude traffic light (84.B)
branch claude/pane-tabs-status-indicator-57b38c | PR #18

REQUEST (Yossi): users want to open panes as TABS instead of splitting the
screen -- always full-screen, nicer to look at -- and a "traffic light" on the
tab itself: green "Claude running", yellow "finished", red "waiting for
input / NEEDS INPUT". Explicitly: check the current Claude Code docs if unsure.

WHAT THE EXPLORATION CHANGED ABOUT THE PLAN
Both halves were ~90% built already, which is the whole reason this is small:
  - Phase 55-A/65.T "maximize" has been swapping the split tree for one leaf
    since it shipped, with the hidden panes' PTYs alive in the terms registry.
    Tabs mode = that same swap with the target always set. No new render path.
  - The per-pane turn state machine (issue #4) already runs hook -> CLI -> RPC
    -> Rust -> Tauri event -> Solid signal -> pane header, painted as the
    "clock M:SS . avg Ns" ticker. Green and yellow needed NO new hooks at all.
  - Red needed Notification, which v0.4.4 had deliberately deleted.
Fetched the live Claude Code hook docs rather than guessing: Notification
carries notification_type with permission_prompt / idle_prompt /
agent_needs_input / elicitation_* -- exactly the red signal.

84.A -- PANES AS TABS
  ymux-types/src/lib.rs   Workspace.tabs_mode: bool, skip_serializing_if
  src/lib.rs              workspace_set_tabs_mode command
  PaneTabs.tsx (new)      the strip; owns no state
  App.tsx                 tabsMode() / setTabsMode / focusPane / newTab /
                          closeTab / Ctrl+Tab / Ctrl+1..9 / render-site swap
  LayoutView, PaneView    tabsMode prop (drops the dead maximize button)
  App.css + i18n x4       ~90 lines of CSS cloned from the browser tab strip

Model choice: a bool, NOT a LayoutNode::Tabs variant. The variant makes every
match on LayoutNode non-exhaustive (~44 sites in the app crate alone), is lossy
both ways (ratios cannot come back), and an older ymux cannot render a Tabs
node out of workspaces.json -- which is what workspaces_merge exists to survive.

84.B -- THE TRAFFIC LIGHT
  cli/src/hooks.rs        ymux_entry_is_runnable (the P1 dead-hook fix) +
                          Notification in the bundled spec + v1.5.0
  cli/src/main.rs         notification branch: filter by type, push the TYPE
                          ALONE, return before the shared feed dispatch
  hooks/claude-code.json  Notification registered, empty matcher, 1.5.0
  src/lib.rs              PaneAgentState + apply_hook transition table + 9
                          unit tests + pane_agent_states hydration command
  src/rpc_server.rs       state machine arms; notification returns EARLY
  paneAgentState.ts (new) pure trafficLight(); 8 node tests, all passing
  AgentLight.tsx (new)    disc / ring / triangle -- shape, not just hue

THREE BUGS FOUND AND FIXED ALONG THE WAY (none of them the assigned task):
 1. P1 dead hook. is_ymux_entry matches on command TEXT, so a settings.json
    entry naming the pre-rename binary still counted as "already present" and
    setup-hooks skipped it forever. Notification is a NEW event with no entry,
    so it would have installed while Stop/UserPromptSubmit stayed dead --
    shipping a light that can only ever show red. Worse than the silence.
 2. Stale pane id. Phase 81.G established that YMUX_PANE_ID goes stale and
    added resolve_hook_pane, but wired it only into the auto-title path.
    agent_runs kept the raw value: for the Ticker that is a timer on a dead
    key, for a light it is the WRONG PANE lighting up.
 3. Frontend clear condition. `!running && avg_ms == null` was read as
    session-end, but an ordinary `stop` on a pane whose turns are all under
    the 2s ticker minimum produces exactly that -- it deleted the entry and
    would have taken the yellow light down on short interactive turns, the
    ones where "your turn" matters most. Clear is now state === "unknown".

Also fixed in passing: .pane-agent-run used margin-left (physical), pinning
the ticker to the visual LEFT in he/ar while the rest of the header mirrored.

DELIBERATE NON-DECISIONS (full reasoning in docs/DECISIONS.md):
  - No PTY-output scraping fallback. No documented stdout marker exists;
    scraping TUI chrome is locale/theme/version dependent and its failure mode
    is the light STICKING ON GREEN with no error. A wrong light is worse than
    no light -- that is the whole thesis.
  - Yellow does not decay on a timer. idle_prompt promotes it to red when
    Claude is actually waiting. Yellow does not clear on focus either.
  - PermissionRequest not registered: total overlap, and it is a BLOCKING hook
    on the tool-call critical path.
  - manifest.json hooks version NOT bumped here (would banner 0.4.5 users
    before a CLI that can honour 1.5.0 exists) -> FOLLOWUPS, release checklist.

KNOWN LIE, STATED: a turn that dies on an API error shows yellow "your turn",
because StopFailure is not registered. FOLLOWUPS P2.

STATUS: ci-windows GREEN on 84.A and on the 84.B backend (cargo test + tsc +
vite + go). NOT VERIFIED LIVE -- Rule #14. Smoke steps in FOLLOWUPS P1.

--- Phase 84 addendum: what CI actually caught, and one review catch ---

CI earned its keep twice, both on things Rule #17 makes unverifiable locally:

 1. ts-rs maps u128 -> `bigint`, not `number`. app/src/bindings/
    PaneAgentSnapshot.ts was hand-written (Rule #17 bans running the
    generator), and the guess was wrong. CI regenerated the file, tsc read
    the real one, three type errors. Fixed with #[ts(type = "number | null")]
    -- the idiom FeedItem::created_ms already uses. It mattered beyond the
    type error: the pane:agent-run event carries the same values as plain
    JSON numbers and the frontend compares the two, so bigint on one side
    would have been a silent runtime comparison bug.

 2. Caught reading the diff back, not by CI: the early return that keeps
    notifications off the feed sat INSIDE `if let Some(pane) = resolved_pane`.
    Reads naturally, wrong -- the guard is about whether there is per-pane
    state to update, the return is about what a notification may BECOME. On
    an unresolvable pane the code skipped both and fell through to the feed,
    reinstating the v0.4.4 noise on the one path nobody would test. Hoisted
    out, with a comment saying why it is outside so it doesn't get tidied
    back in.

Also noted: app/src/bidi.test.ts fails in this worktree with
ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND for `bidi-js` -- a missing dep in this checkout's
node_modules, pre-existing and unrelated to Phase 84 (which adds no deps).
sessionRestore (15) and fmPaths (20) pass, as do the 8 new paneAgentState
tests.

FINAL CI: ci-windows GREEN on the final HEAD (cargo test incl. 14 new Rust
tests, tsc, vite, go). build-macos-intel GREEN. PR #18 ready for review.
STILL NOT VERIFIED LIVE -- Rule #14. Smoke matrix: FOLLOWUPS P1 and
docs/RELEASE-0.5.0-CHECKLIST.md section 13b.

2026-08-23 | CI: off the deprecated node20 runtime, and three pins that were
             never being watched
Trigger was the runner warning that checkout@v4 / setup-go@v5 / setup-node@v4
are being force-migrated to Node 24. The warning was the smaller half of the
problem: all four workflows also pinned `node-version: 20`, and **Node 20 hit
EOL on 2026-04-30** — nearly four months of building the shipped frontend on a
runtime that gets no security fixes.

Files: .github/workflows/{ci-windows,build-windows,build-macos-intel,
warm-rust-cache}.yml, .github/dependabot.yml (new), app/package.json,
CLAUDE.md, BACKLOG.md.

WHAT CHANGED
  26 `uses:` lines, verified against each action's real action.yml rather than
  guessed from the changelog — every target below reports `runs.using: node24`:
    actions/checkout       @v4 -> @v7   (x4)
    actions/setup-node     @v4 -> @v7   (x4)
    actions/setup-go       @v5 -> @v7   (x1)
    actions/upload-artifact@v4 -> @v7   (x6)
    actions/cache          @v4 -> @v6   (x1)
  node-version: 20 -> 24 (x4). Node 24 is Active LTS to 2026-10-20, then
  maintenance to 2028-04-30. Chose 24 over 22 for the extra year at equal cost.
  Compatibility floor in the tree is marked@18.0.4 (">= 20") and vite@6.4.2
  ("^18 || ^20 || >=22") — 24 clears both. No native/node-gyp deps, so npm ci
  needs no rebuild.
  app/package.json gained `"engines": { "node": ">=22" }` so the requirement is
  declared in the repo and not only in four YAML files. >=22 not >=24 on
  purpose: do not break a dev still on 22 locally; CI is the one that pins 24.

SHA-PINNED THE TWO THIRD-PARTY ACTIONS
  Swatinem/rust-cache    @v2     -> @6323deb1... # v2.9.2 (already node24)
  dtolnay/rust-toolchain @stable -> @4360b525... # stable branch @ 2026-08-05
  `@stable` was a BRANCH pin: anyone with push access to it ran code in our CI
  with the repo checked out. rust-toolchain is a composite action and never had
  a Node runtime, so this is supply-chain hygiene, not deprecation work.

  THE TRAP, verified against the GitHub API and worth not rediscovering:
  that repo's ONLY tag, v1, points at 6c977a6c = master head — a different
  commit from the stable branch head. On master `toolchain` is
  `required: true` WITH NO DEFAULT, while on the stable branch it defaults to
  `stable`. So a dependabot "upgrade" from our stable-branch SHA onto v1 would
  fail every job on a missing required input. Mitigated two ways: an explicit
  `toolchain: stable` at all four call sites (works on either branch), and a
  comment in dependabot.yml telling the reviewer to check the branch. Pinning
  the SHA does NOT freeze the Rust version — the stable branch still resolves
  to current stable.

  actions/* stay on floating majors on purpose. GitHub owns them; SHA-pinning
  there buys noise, not safety.

TWO HOLES FOUND IN PASSING, BOTH CLOSED
  1. Not one workflow had a `permissions:` block, so GITHUB_TOKEN got the repo
     default. Added `permissions: contents: read` to all four. Verified safe by
     grep over .github/workflows for `gh release` / `gh api` / `git push` /
     `git commit` / `softprops` / `packages:` / `id-token` — zero hits; release
     publishing is manual per docs/RELEASING.md. upload-artifact authenticates
     with ACTIONS_RUNTIME_TOKEN, not GITHUB_TOKEN, so artifacts still upload.
     The Apple secrets in build-macos-intel are untouched (permissions only
     governs GITHUB_TOKEN).
  2. No .github/dependabot.yml existed — nothing was watching the pins, which
     is exactly how the whole set drifted onto node20 together and surfaced as
     a runner warning instead of a PR. Added, github-actions ecosystem only,
     monthly, grouped into one PR. npm/cargo deliberately left off (would open
     a wave of PRs against unreviewed trees); queued in BACKLOG.md.

BREAKING CHANGES CHECKED ONE BY ONE, NONE APPLY HERE
  checkout@v7 blocks fork-PR checkout for pull_request_target / workflow_run —
    we only use push / pull_request / workflow_dispatch.
  checkout@v6 persists credentials to a separate file — no step reads
    .git/config by hand.
  setup-node@v5 auto-caches when package.json has a `packageManager` field —
    app/package.json has none (confirmed), and `cache: npm` is explicit anyway.
  upload-artifact@v7 added an `archive` input, default 'true' = v4 behaviour;
    name / path / if-no-files-found unchanged, so the ymux-server-linux-* glob
    step is unaffected.
  cache@v5+ and upload-artifact@v6+ need runner >= 2.327.1 — self-hosted only;
    we are on GitHub-managed runners.
  Went straight to the newest major rather than the first node24 one because
  none of the above touches this repo and checkout@v7 is itself the fix for a
  fork-PR checkout issue. Stopping halfway would just book a second bump.

STATUS: all YAML + package.json parse locally. Per Rule #17 nothing was built
or tested on the dev box — CI is the verification step. Result recorded below
once the runs land.

  MOD  CLAUDE.md                                  (vault first under "Where to start"; Rule #18)
  MOD  docs/CONTRIBUTING.md                       (reading order; § Updating the vault)
  MOD  docs/MODULES.md                            (replaced by a pointer stub)
  MOD  docs/DECISIONS.md                          (Decided entry)
  MOD  README.md                                  (doc index)

THE GATE — two checks, both must pass
  1. HASH  sha256 every file a page `covers:` vs .vault-lock.json. Catches drift
           however it arrived: an old commit, a merge, another worktree.
  2. DIFF  (--diff-base <sha>) if a covered file moved in this change, the
           owning .md must have moved too. The hash check alone can be satisfied
           by running --write and changing nothing; this one cannot.
  Escape:  [vault-skip] in the PR title or head commit → skips the DIFF half
           only, with a ::notice:: in the log. Included on purpose: unlike the
           server-rebake gate, a docs gate trips on every typo fix inside a
           covered file, and a gate people route around is worse than one they
           use. The PR title/commit message reach bash via `env:`, never `${{ }}`
           inline — attacker-controlled text on a fork PR.
  Local:   `node scripts/vault-check.mjs` is the pre-merge command. No deps, so
           it runs on a fresh checkout, which is exactly when it matters.

COVERAGE
  88,558 of 92,568 tracked .rs/.ts/.tsx/.go/.mjs lines = 96%, 210 files, 16 pages.
  Uncovered, by design: generated SDK (1,706), generated ts-rs bindings (1,063),
  test files (1,239), app/src/vite-env.d.ts (2). A test edit must not trip the
  gate, and where the tests ARE the spec (the four RTL modules) the vault says
  to read them rather than paraphrase them.

RESULT — verified locally, all four failure modes exercised
  clean tree                       → exit 0, prints coverage
  append a comment to lib.rs       → exit 1, names
                                     "app/src-tauri/src/lib.rs (changed since the
                                     vault file was written)" under backend-core.md
  commit that + --diff-base HEAD~1 → exit 1 on BOTH checks, with the "update it,
                                     run --write, commit both — or [vault-skip]"
                                     message and `changed: app/src-tauri/src/lib.rs`
  restore + --write                → exit 0

NOT YET VERIFIED (Rule #14)
The CI step itself has not run — the [vault-skip] branch and the base-sha
handling are shell logic that has only been reasoned about, not executed. Push
and read the ci-windows run before treating this as done.

The gate proves a page CHANGED, not that it became CORRECT. Nothing automated
can prove the latter. The acceptance test is human: answer a real question
("where is the SSH auth chain", "what writes workspaces.json") from
docs/vault/INDEX.md alone without opening a .rs file. If you cannot, the page
is too thin — fix the page, not the gate.

Why nothing killed the watchers (read, not guessed): the desktop keeps no
remote PID and only aborts its local tokio task; the `!still_alive` branch in
the session-end task never runs because the `__headless__<ws>` session is
never removed; the Go reaper only kills duplicates-per-workspace, a lone
orphan is never `Duplicate`; the bootstrap pkill pattern is
`[w]inmux-linux-x64$` which never matched the renamed cmdline. The watcher's
own docstring ("the SSH exec channel dies ... terminates us") was false — no
PTY, never reads stdin, never writes stdout.
