2026-08-23 | Phase 84.F - a pane title could inject a command into its own shell
Files: app/src-tauri/src/lib.rs, FOLLOWUPS.md, FOLLOWUPS-ARCHIVE.md
Closes FOLLOWUPS P1 of 2026-08-20 ("A PANE TITLE CAN BREAK ITS OWN SESSION NAME")

The entry's own best evidence was a comment. `build_zellij_attach_command`
carried "session names come from `sanitize_session_name`, which emits only
[A-Za-z0-9_-], so there is nothing here to quote or escape". No function by
that name has ever existed in this tree. What actually feeds that line is
`sanitize_tmux_session_name_for_title`, which substituted `.`, `:` and
whitespace and nothing else - so `;`, `&`, `|`, `$`, backtick, quotes, `>` and
`^` survived a user's pane title into `format!("zellij attach -c {name}\r\n")`,
which is typed VERBATIM into cmd.exe or PowerShell 900ms after pane spawn.
A pane titled `work; calc` ran calc.

Self-inflicted (own title, own shell), so never a privilege boundary - but the
mundane half is the expensive one and is why this was P1: the session gets
created under a different name than the one ymux tracks, which is a live
candidate for "Kill session did nothing".

FIXED AT THE SOURCE, not by quoting at the use site. There is no quoting that
is correct in cmd.exe and PowerShell simultaneously - they disagree about `^`,
`%`, backticks and single quotes - and this line must parse in both.
  session_name_char_is_safe(c): ASCII alphanumeric, `_`, `-`, or any NON-ASCII
    char that is neither control nor whitespace.
  The non-ASCII clause is what keeps Phase 23.I's promise that a Hebrew title
    becomes a session of the same name. Every metacharacter in all three
    shells is ASCII, so Hebrew/Arabic/CJK need no special case and niqqud
    (combining marks, NOT alphanumerics) survive rather than being punched out
    of the middle of a word. Controls/separators are excluded first, which
    also catches U+0085 / U+2028 / U+2029 - non-ASCII line breaks that an
    is_ascii() test alone would wave straight past.
  build_zellij_attach_command now returns Option and REFUSES a name it cannot
    type. Refusing beats mangling: mangling attaches to a session that is not
    the one picked, silently, while refusing leaves the pane in a plain
    working shell - the failure mode this function already accepts for a
    missing zellij. Only a picker-supplied name (a session made outside ymux
    by hand) can reach the refusal.

Audited the neighbours rather than assuming: the tmux/SSH attach script
(build_tmux_attach_script) and `tmux kill-session` already use shell_quote,
and zellij_args_delete_force was already argv-only. The Windows zellij line
was the single hole. Two other comments repeating the `sanitize_session_name`
claim were corrected.

TESTS: six, five of them hostile input. Worth recording WHY the old one missed
this - `zellij_attach_command_is_a_single_safe_line` fed the function
`ymux-p_1a2b_0`, a name that could not fail, so it passed for the entire life
of the hole. The new ones drive the real path (title -> sanitizer -> attach
line) and assert the command stays ONE line; plus a Unicode test pinning the
Phase 23.I promise, and a regression test for the parts of the old contract
that were already right (dot/colon, run collapsing, trimming, None on an
all-junk title, the 100-CHAR-not-byte cap).

STATUS: per Rule #17 nothing was built or tested here - CI is the verification
step. Rule #14 still applies after that: the live check is titling a pane
`work; calc` on Windows, confirming no calc, and confirming Kill session still
finds the session.

2026-08-23 | Phase 84.G - workspaces.json schema gate (the P1's option (b))
Files: app/src-tauri/src/lib.rs, FOLLOWUPS.md, FOLLOWUPS-ARCHIVE.md, BACKLOG.md
Closes FOLLOWUPS P1 of 2026-08-18 ("TWO WINMUX BUILDS SHARE %APPDATA%...")

workspaces.json has carried a `version` field since forever and NOTHING has
ever read it - it was written as a literal 1 and ignored on load. It is
load-bearing from here on.

  WORKSPACES_SCHEMA_VERSION = 2, stamped on every write through
    `serialize_with` rather than by assigning the field. The invariant is
    "what we WRITE is always current", and serialization is the one place
    that cannot be bypassed; an assignment would have to be repeated at every
    construction site and would drift the first time one was added.
  schema_gate(on_disk, last_written) extracted as a pure function - the same
    reason resolve_effective_session_name was extracted - because the
    interesting cases need two binaries sharing a config dir and no test can
    arrange that. Three outcomes:
      Refuse        on-disk newer than this build. Hard Err out of
                    save_to_disk. Merging cannot help: we would have to
                    understand keys we have never heard of.
      WarnDowngrade on-disk older than what we last wrote - an old build got
                    here first. Write ANYWAY. Refusing would leave the user
                    unable to save for as long as that build is open, and the
                    three-way merge is the actual repair.
      Write         everything else.
  schema_version_of is deliberately tolerant. Absent, empty, malformed,
    version-less, string, negative and float all read as None = carry on. A
    refusal hangs off this function, so a stray byte in the file must never
    become "you cannot save". BOM stripped first - one already bricked this
    file once.

THE LIMIT, AND IT IS NOT SMALL: this does nothing about the 0.4.x builds
already on disk. They never read the field and will write v1 back over a v2
file, so the incident as REPORTED is not prevented in the backwards direction.
What covers that case is workspaces_merge, which already re-reads and merges
rather than dumping, and treats a field that vanished on the other side as an
absence rather than a deletion. What the gate adds for that case is the LOG
LINE - and that is exactly the half that was missing, because the entry's own
words were "NOTHING appears in the log of the new build, because it did
nothing". Option (a) from the entry, the pid lock, is the only mechanism that
can stop an already-shipped binary; it is NOT done and went to BACKLOG rather
than being left implied, together with the note that it should be solved with
the tauri-plugin-single-instance P2 about two ymux.exe racing on log rotation,
not twice.

Deliberately NOT extended to settings.rs, which has the same atomic-write +
load-poison shape: the loss mode there is smaller, and a lock would cover both.
Recorded in the BACKLOG entry so the omission is a decision, not an oversight.

TESTS: seven. The stamping across a carried 0/1/current, a legacy file with no
version key still loading as v1, the tolerant reader across nine junk inputs,
the gate in all three directions, and the case that a legacy file we have not
written over yet is NOT a downgrade. An earlier draft of the gate test asserted
`ours + 1 > ours` - arithmetic, not the code - which is why the decision was
extracted into a function instead.

STATUS: per Rule #17 nothing was built or tested here; CI is the verification
step. Live check after that: run two builds against one config dir and confirm
the older one now says REFUSING instead of silently winning.

2026-08-23 | Phase 84.H - fonts: the install finally has an uninstall
Files: app/src-tauri/src/fonts.rs, app/src-tauri/src/lib.rs (command reg),
       app/src/settings.ts, app/src/SettingsModal.tsx, app/src/App.css,
       app/src/i18n/{en,he,ar,ru}.json, FOLLOWUPS.md, FOLLOWUPS-ARCHIVE.md
Closes FOLLOWUPS P1 of 2026-08-03 ("Install has no matching UNINSTALL")

font_uninstall(id) mirrors font_install(id). 27 MB and six files no longer
need hand-deleting from %LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\Windows\Fonts and HKCU.

THE DESIGN DECISION THAT MATTERS: which files belong to an entry is derived
from the CATALOG, not recorded at install time.
  A zip asset wrote every entry matching its ZipFilter::name_prefix; a bare
  asset wrote exactly its save_as. The same catalog data identifies them
  again, so entry_owns_file() needs no history.
  A manifest written at install time was the obvious alternative and is
  WRONG here: it would only know about installs done after it shipped, and
  every font already on a user's machine - exactly the ones this entry was
  filed about - would stay unremovable forever.
  Accepted cost, written down rather than hidden: a user's own hand-installed
  copy of the same family, in the same per-user directory, also matches. It is
  the family they just asked to remove, and the alternative cannot see the
  past.

Two orderings that are easy to get backwards, both chosen deliberately:
  DELETE THEN UNREGISTER. The reverse leaves a font still on disk and still
    loadable with no registry entry - present but invisible to the picker,
    which is a worse state than either end of the operation. Only paths that
    actually went are unregistered.
  MATCH REGISTRY BY DATA, NOT BY NAME. register_font builds the value name
    from the font's internal name table with a fallback to the file stem;
    rebuilding it at uninstall would mean reproducing that decision from a
    file that has just been deleted. The path is the thing we know.

A face held open by a running app is a REPORTED outcome (`failed`), not an
error - the rest of the family still comes out and the UI names what did not.
That is the everyday Windows case, not an edge one.

UI: font_catalog grew an `installed` flag (read from the directory per call),
and a new "Installed by ymux" list sits under both font pickers with a Remove
button per row, rendering nothing when nothing is installed. It is a LIST and
not a button beside the picker for a concrete reason: FontMissingNotice only
renders while a family is MISSING, so an installed font had nowhere to hang a
control. window.confirm guards it - deleting files, and the way back is a
multi-MB download. Noted for later: the codebase has been moving destructive
WORKSPACE actions off window.confirm to a styled component; this could follow,
but a font removal is not that weight. i18n in all four locales.

TESTS: four. The interesting one is entry_prefixes_do_not_collide, which walks
the whole catalog rather than today's pair - `FiraCode-` and
`FiraCodeNerdFontMono-` share a stem, and a future entry could silently make
one swallow the other. Also: an unknown id is refused BEFORE anything is
deleted (the id selects files for deletion, so an unchecked one turns this
into "delete arbitrary things from the font directory"), the JetBrains
no-ligature sibling that the install filter excludes is not claimed by
uninstall either, and every bare asset's save_as is really a font file.

STATUS: per Rule #17 nothing was built or tested here; CI is the verification
step, and CI could not even be reached this session - `git fetch` failed on a
network timeout, so the branch is unpushed and NOTHING here has compiled.
Rule #14 after that: install a font, remove it, confirm the picker flips back
to the warning row and that the files and HKCU values are really gone.

2026-08-23 | Evaluated a candidate vault-check.sh; kept ours, fixed 4 stale cites
Files: docs/vault/backend-core.md, docs/vault/.vault-lock.json, FOLLOWUPS.md

Yossi shared an Obsidian-style `vault-check.sh` (98 lines, zero deps) and asked
whether it solves the vault drift problem. It does not, and the reasons are
worth recording so nobody re-evaluates it later.

What it checks: [[wikilink]] resolution, reachability from moc.md, that
file:line cites point at existing files, and that the cited line is within the
file. What it does NOT have: hashes, a lock file, a diff base, or any notion of
"covers". So it has no way to answer "did the code move since this prose was
written", which is the entire failure that turned main red today, and no way to
answer "is there code no vault claims", which is the blind spot filed this
afternoon. It would have printed "vault OK" through both.

Format mismatch is total, not cosmetic: our vault has ZERO wikilinks and no
moc.md, so check 1 passes vacuously and check 2 hard-fails on the first line
("MISSING: moc.md — the vault has no root index").

THE ONE GOOD IDEA IN IT is verifying file:line cites, which our gate genuinely
does not do - so it was worth taking seriously. But its implementation cannot
work here, and that is measurable rather than a matter of taste: it checks the
line is IN RANGE, and lib.rs is 13,272 lines. Measured our six cites:
  AppState       cited lib.rs:145   really 146
  save_to_disk   cited lib.rs:514   really 838
  pane_connect   cited lib.rs:7419  really 7949
  emit_data      cited lib.rs:1960  really 2370
  BIND_ERROR     rpc_server.rs:37   correct
  dispatch       rpc_server.rs:646  correct
Four of six were wrong, and all four are comfortably "in range" - the script
would have passed every one. Fixed by hand here.

Note why the hash gate does not cover this either: it fires when the file
changes, which is exactly when someone re-reads the PROSE - and a line number
inside that prose is the thing they do not re-derive. Filed as P3 with the fix
that would work: an IDENTITY check (cite the symbol with the line, verify the
symbol is on or near it) rather than a bounds check.

Verdict: keep vault-check.mjs, do not adopt or fork the shell script.
=======
2026-08-23 | Phase 87: every keyboard shortcut is recordable, not typed
Trigger: the voice (push-to-talk) hotkey in Settings -> AI was a FREE-TEXT box.
The accelerator had to be typed by hand, and a typo produced a hotkey that
silently never fired (parseShortcut returns null, nothing reports it). Yossi
asked for click-to-record there and across all shortcuts; when told the
Shortcuts tab already records and only the voice field did not, he chose the
larger scope: make the ~20 hardcoded bindings configurable too.

What changed
- 20 new accelerators moved out of App.tsx's hardcoded if-chain into
  settings.shortcuts: command_palette, toggle_sidebar, toggle_sidebar_soft,
  toggle_maximize, focus_zoom, reset_terminal, distribute_evenly,
  split_horizontal/_vertical, close_pane, split_or_move_{l,r,u,d},
  quadrant_{tl,tr,bl,br}, tab_next, tab_prev. 8 -> 28 configurable rows.
- App.tsx handleKey: ~250 lines of sequential ifs -> one ordered keyBindings
  table, first-match-wins. Two semantics preserved on purpose: `when` false
  means SKIP-AND-KEEP-SCANNING (plain Ctrl+B must reach the PTY, tmux prefix),
  and `run` owns its own preventDefault (copy must not consume the event when
  the terminal had no selection).
- Ctrl+1..9 and bare Escape stay hardcoded (numeric family; contextual
  dismissal) but are now LISTED read-only in Settings, which is what Yossi
  asked for when told they exist. Editor/browser-pane keys listed too.
- Voice hotkey: free-text input -> ShortcutRow. Stayed in settings.stt per
  Yossi's choice (no migration); conflictingAccels() is fed it explicitly so a
  clash with a table binding is still reported across the two schemas.
- Conflict detection: canonicalAccel + conflictingAccels in shortcuts.ts,
  red outline + tooltip in the tab. This is the class of bug that made
  Focus/Zoom move off Ctrl+Shift+M by hand in Phase 65 (bug V).
- shortcuts.ts now OWNS ShortcutsSettings/DEFAULT_SHORTCUTS/SHORTCUT_ACTION_IDS
  /SHORTCUT_GROUPS and has ZERO imports, so it is testable under bare
  `node --test`; settings.ts re-exports them (and needed its own local import,
  since `export ... from` creates no local binding). Event-reading fns take a
  structural KeyLike instead of KeyboardEvent for the same reason.
- Rust: Shortcuts got container-level #[serde(default)] backed by the existing
  impl Default, so 20 new fields needed ZERO new default fns and old
  settings.json files still load. bindings/Shortcuts.ts hand-written to match
  the ts-rs emit (Rule #17 - cannot run cargo test locally); CI regenerates it,
  DIFF IT against the run and correct if it drifted.
- Dropped the `e.key === "+" || e.code === "Equal"` hack for Ctrl+Alt+=:
  matches() already falls back to the physical key, which is what it worked
  around. Covered by a test.

Deliberate behaviour tightenings (matches() is exact on all four modifiers,
four old branches were looser) - say so if a bug report arrives:
  Ctrl+Alt+Shift+P no longer opens the palette (old branch had no !alt/!meta)
  Ctrl+Alt+Shift+= no longer distributes evenly
  Ctrl+Enter and Ctrl+Alt+I/O/K/L no longer fire with Meta held

Bug found and fixed en route: ShortcutRow never called stopPropagation, so the
combination being RECORDED also fired the action bound to it. Harmless-ish
before (recording Ctrl+Shift+N toggled notes); with the table grown it means
recording Ctrl+Shift+W closes the active pane. One line.

Test infrastructure: app/src/*.test.ts (9 files, since Phase 80) had NO runner
- no npm script, no CI step, nothing ever executed them. Added `npm test`
(node --test) and a ci-windows step. New shortcuts.test.ts: 23 cases incl. the
Hebrew-layout physical-key path, the four tightenings, formatEvent<->parse
round-trip, "the shipped defaults contain no conflicts" (the Phase-65-bug-V
regression guard), and SHORTCUT_GROUPS covering every id exactly once.

Files: app/src/{shortcuts.ts,shortcuts.test.ts(new),settings.ts,App.tsx,
SettingsModal.tsx,App.css,bindings/Shortcuts.ts}, app/src/i18n/{en,he,ar,ru}.json
(+32 keys each, all four still in lockstep at 1251 lines),
app/src-tauri/src/settings.rs, app/package.json, .github/workflows/ci-windows.yml

updated + re-stamped. The lock was re-stamped SELECTIVELY - only the six source
files this change touched. The six pages that were already stale on main

[2026-08-23] CLAUDE.md: added Absolute Rule #19 — before every push, sweep the full worktree-vs-origin/main diff and update docs/vault/ + re-stamp .vault-lock.json. Files: CLAUDE.md. Result: doc-only, no code touched.
2026-08-23 | Third merge of origin/main (Phase 87), and the "should the vault be
             Obsidian-style?" question, measured
Files: docs/vault/frontend-{flows,lib}.md, .vault-lock.json, PROGRESS, FOLLOWUPS

MERGE. origin/main moved again (PR #28, Phase 87 recordable shortcuts). Two
vault conflicts, and this time BOTH SIDES WERE RIGHT - my fonts prose and their
Phase 87 shortcuts prose describe different additions to the same section, so
both were kept rather than one chosen. The line counts in the headers were the
only true conflict, and neither side's number was correct after the merge:
recomputed from the merged tree (SettingsModal 1,868, settings.ts 751). That is
the trap in a doc that carries line counts - a merge produces a file neither
parent describes.

THE OBSIDIAN QUESTION. Yossi asked whether the vault should be Obsidian-shaped,
which would make the shared vault-check.sh the right tool rather than the wrong
one. Measured rather than argued:

  - 32 cross-vault references already exist in the prose (`backend-panes.md`,
    `crates.md`, …). All 32 resolve today. They are PLAIN TEXT and nothing
    checks them, so a renamed vault file breaks every one of them silently.
    This is the one genuine Obsidian property we are missing, and we are
    already using it informally - the links exist, they just are not typed.
  - Every vault is reachable from INDEX.md. The moc.md check would pass; we
    have the concept under a different name.
  - 16 files, 130-220 lines each, 2,557 total.

So the answer is not "yes, convert" and not "no, irrelevant". The reader here
is an AGENT, not a human browsing: it reads whole files into a context window
rather than clicking through a graph, so atomic notes plus dense linking - the
thing Obsidian is FOR - buys navigation we do not use and costs round trips and
repeated frontmatter. 130-220 line subsystem files are the right chunk for that
consumer.

What IS worth taking is the checkability, not the format: make those 32
references machine-checkable so a rename cannot rot them, without splitting
files or adopting wikilink syntax. Filed as P3 alongside the cite-rot entry,
since it is the same class of problem - a pointer in prose that nothing
verifies - and the same fix pass should do both.

   mounts the REAL <PaneTabs> against the REAL App.css. Rule #17 bans a cargo
   build here and the bug was never in Rust, so this is how the fix was
   actually proven. Measured off the live DOM, same markup, two states of
   App.css:
     before  .pane-tabs display:block, tabs at y=0/22/44/66, all x=0, w=1215

   Clicking was verified too: activePaneId p2 -> p4, and the active tab's

CLAUDE.md says phase numbers are stable and never reused. One got reused, by
two sessions working in parallel, and this records it rather than pretending
otherwise:

  d201023 on main  "Phase 84.F: restore the pane tab strip's CSS, deleted by
                    a merge"          <- landed on main FIRST, keeps 84.F
  ffc25d3 here     "fix(pty): a pane title could inject a command into its own
                    shell"            <- its PROGRESS entry also said 84.F

The other session's commit reached main first, so 84.F is theirs. This
session's three are re-designated in the record as 84.J (pane title / session
name), 84.K (workspaces schema gate) and 84.L (font uninstall). The commit
SUBJECTS are unchanged and are the reliable way to find them - git history is
not being rewritten for a numbering label, and Rule #15 would forbid it on a
pushed branch anyway. Sub-numbers G and H were also used by this session and
do not appear to collide, but they are folded into the same re-designation so
the three stay contiguous.

The lesson is the same one the worktree sweep taught earlier today: parallel
sessions do not see each other's numbering, and PROGRESS.txt is append-only
precisely because two of them will write at once. Check `git log --all
--grep='Phase 84'` before claiming a sub-number.

2026-08-24 — TMUX picker: mark the sessions that belong to another folder
Branch: yos/tmux-folder-files-display-785d6f

TASK (Yossi, Hebrew): now that tmux sessions are mounted to a folder and the
connect picker has a "This folder / Whole server" toggle, show IN THE WHOLE-
SERVER view what we already know belongs to a DIFFERENT folder, to prevent
messes.

WHY IT WAS MISSING, NOT AN OVERSIGHT: the 2026-08-23 change computed `owned`
as `o.workspace_id == workspace_id` and THREW THE workspace_id AWAY when it did
not match. The information was already on the machine, one comparison away —
"Whole server" was a flat list in which a free session and a session already
mounted on another project looked identical.

WHAT LANDED
  - TmuxSessionInfo gained ONE field: `foreign: Option<ForeignScope>`, where
    ForeignScope = { kind: workspace|folder, label?, path? }. Facts, not a
    sentence: every picker string is i18n'd in four languages, so the backend
    supplies the data and PaneView composes the words. `kind` is what picks
    between "belongs to workspace X" and "runs in folder Y".
  - `annotate_session_scope` was SPLIT: it now loads session-owners.json and
    delegates to `annotate_scope_with`, a disk-free core holding every rule.
    That is the first time any of the 2026-08-23 scope logic became unit-
    testable — the old one did file I/O inline, which is why it had no tests.
  - New `last_path_segment`, sibling of `path_is_within`: both separators,
    because one picker list mixes POSIX paths off a Linux host with Windows
    paths out of a zellij-era ownership row.
  - `pane_list_tmux_sessions` now also collects workspace_id -> name off the
    workspaces lock IT ALREADY TAKES. No new lock, no second round trip.
  - PaneView: a folder badge on the row, left of the transient state flags
    (attached / resurrect) because this is a property of the session, not of
    right now. Tooltip carries the sentence plus the full path.
  - i18n: 3 keys x 4 languages. Hebrew/Arabic wrap {name} in U+200E, mirroring
    what connect.tmuxPick.deleteConfirm already does, so an LTR path inside RTL
    prose does not scramble the sentence.
  - App.css: .nc-resume-foreign — amber, not red. Attaching is ALLOWED and
    sometimes exactly right; this marks, it does not block.

TWO RULES, BOTH ASSERTED IN TESTS
  (1) `foreign` is NEVER Some while `owned || in_cwd`. The picker's "This
      folder" view is exactly the complement of this field, so the badge cannot
      appear there by construction and the frontend needs no scope conditional.
      This also settles the two-workspaces-on-one-folder case: both belong
      there, and warning about it would be a lie.
  (2) An unknown cwd with no ownership row is NEVER foreign. Straight from the
      2026-08-23 decision: `cwd: None` means we do not know, never "somewhere
      else". Every zellij session and every five-field tmux lands here. This is
      the regression that matters most and it has its own test.

DECIDED BY YOSSI, ASKED BEFORE BUILDING
  - Badge only. Not a confirm-on-click, not a re-sort of the list.
  - A session we cannot place shows NOTHING, not a muted "unknown folder" chip.

TESTS (lib.rs, mod tmux_list_parse_tests, run on CI): basename_stops_at_either_
separator, a_foreign_badge_is_impossible_inside_the_workspace_view, a_claim_by_
another_workspace_names_that_workspace, a_claim_survives_on_windows_where_there_
is_no_cwd_at_all, a_claim_by_a_deleted_workspace_still_warns, an_unclaimed_
session_outside_the_root_is_flagged_by_folder, an_unknown_cwd_is_never_evidence_
of_elsewhere, no_root_means_no_folder_verdict. Fixtures build sessions through
the REAL parser, so a format change breaks these too rather than leaving them
asserting a shape tmux no longer emits.

NOT TOUCHED ON PURPOSE: `pane_probe_tmux_sessions` still does not annotate, and
session restore still calls with projectPath: null. Both must see everything —
a pane whose session lives outside the folder has to come back on the next boot.

[2026-08-23] Phase 85.A + 85.B — Browser: DevTools button + the stuck-webview ghost
  85.A  The DevTools button never worked in ANY shipped build. workspace_browser.rs
        wrapped open_devtools() in #[cfg(any(debug_assertions, feature = "devtools"))],
        which reads like tauri's own gate but resolves `feature = "devtools"` against
        the `app` crate's feature set — and app/src-tauri/Cargo.toml has NO [features]
        section. The `devtools` at Cargo.toml:52 is a feature of the tauri DEPENDENCY.
        So in release (debug_assertions off) the #[cfg(not(...))] arm compiled and the
        command returned Err("this build was compiled without devtools support") 100%
        of the time. Right-click -> Inspect kept working the whole time (.devtools(true)
        on the builder is not gated), which is why it read as a UI bug.
        Fix: call open_devtools() unconditionally. tauri::Webview::open_devtools is
        gated on the TAURI crate's feature, which is on, so it compiles in release and
        breaks loudly at build time if anyone strips it. Did NOT add [features] to the
        app crate (would mean threading --features through 3 workflows for zero gain).
        Grepped the whole crate: those two were the only `feature = "..."` cfgs in
        app/src-tauri/src/.
        Also: the failure was invisible because the frontend catch was a bare log.warn.
        Added a `chromeError` transient strip in the port bar (App.css .bw-chrome-err)
        + i18n key browser.devtools.failed in en/he/ar/ru. It has to live in the port
        bar row: the native child webview paints over the slot, so an overlay there
        would never be seen. navError can't cover it either -- it only renders when NO
        url is loaded.
  85.B  X closed the frame but left the native webview painted over the panels in every
        workspace. Root cause: all three hide paths passed p.workspace?.id -- the ACTIVE
        workspace -- never the one whose webview was on screen. Open Browser in ws A,
        switch to ws B: the switch effect hid B (no-op, B has no webview) and nobody
        ever hid A. X then hid B too. Ghost survived until app exit.
        Fix: `shownWsId` -- one writer (show effect's .then), one reader (hideShown()),
        called from the workspace-switch effect (hides the OUTGOING ws before forgetting
        it), the show effect's no-URL branch, and the falling-edge close effect. Dropped
        the `&& id` guard on the close effect -- a close with p.workspace momentarily
        undefined was a second leak.
        Kept hide-not-close semantics (page state across X is deliberate, App.tsx:4365).
        Left the modal broadcast-hide at App.tsx:534-551 alone -- that path is correct.
  Files: app/src-tauri/src/workspace_browser.rs, app/src/BrowserWindow.tsx,
         app/src/App.css, app/src/i18n/{en,he,ar,ru}.json,
         docs/vault/{backend-panes,frontend-panes}.md + .vault-lock.json
  Result: NOT verified. No local builds (Rule #17) -- pushed for CI. 85.A specifically
          MUST be smoke-tested on a RELEASE build: the bug is invisible in a debug build,
          where debug_assertions makes the broken cfg true.
  Next: 85.C -- pop the Browser out into a real OS window, full ymux chrome.

[2026-08-23] Phase 85.C - Browser pops out into a real OS window, with full ymux chrome
  Decisions (Yossi): full chrome in the popped-out window (not a bare browser); its X
  just CLOSES (no re-attach into the panel); the mode PERSISTS across restarts.
  Reused the Phase 63.A schema that shipped and was never wired: settings.rs
  FloatingWindowMode::PopOut + popout_rect + popout_display (63.E never landed;
  DECISIONS.md:921 still listed it as open).
  Rust
    - workspace_browser_show gains window_label: Option<String> (default "main"); the
      hardcoded get_window("main") is now the host lookup. Read on the SLOW path only.
    - browser_popout_open: async (WebviewWindowBuilder DEADLOCKS from a sync command on
      Windows), clean index.html URL (asset protocol serves blank for any suffix; the ws
      id rides the LABEL browser-popout-<ws>), 3-attempt 0x8007139F retry, focus-if-exists.
      .devtools(false) on the WINDOW -- it renders ymux UI, Rule #1, and the devtools
      feature flips wry's default to true for every webview. The CHILD keeps .devtools(true).
    - A child webview CANNOT be re-parented. So popping out = destroy + respawn: drop the
      child under main, the popout's own frontend spawns a fresh one under itself. THE PAGE
      RELOADS. Inherent to the shape; said so in the button tooltip + the vault.
    - Destroyed handler (not CloseRequested): saves the rect in LOGICAL px (outer_position
      /inner_size are PHYSICAL and the builder takes logical -- a straight round-trip would
      rescale the window by the scale factor every cycle on HiDPI), drops the child webview,
      clears the mode, emits browser-popout:closed.
    - teardown_workspace_runtime closes the popout window too, or it outlives its workspace.
    - settings.rs: set_browser_popout + browser_popout_geometry. floating_windows is now
      Rust-owned and settings_save CARRIES it over from the stored copy -- same wipe shape
      as the terminal.rtl bug (a save from any Settings screen would have reset the mode).
    - capabilities/browser-popout.json: needed its own file, "popout-*" does NOT match
      "browser-popout-*" (globs are prefix-anchored).
  Frontend
    - Extracted BrowserChrome.tsx (801) from BrowserWindow.tsx (792 -> 216). Split as a
      FACTORY + body component, not one component: the effects must outlive the JSX --
      the floating panel stays mounted while closed and its falling-edge effect is what
      hides the webview, so inside the <Show> it would unmount before firing.
      Injected seam = slotRect() + windowLabel. Nothing else differs between hosts.
    - components/PopoutBrowser.tsx (193): separate JS context, so it self-serves ports via
      workspace_ensure_port_watcher / list_detected_ports / forward_port_start + the
      app-wide port-* broadcasts filtered by ws id (PopoutTerminal's pty:data pattern).
      anyModalOpen is hardcoded false -- ymux modals live in main and cover nothing here.
    - index.tsx: routes browser-popout-<ws> BEFORE popout-<sid>. No prefix collision.
    - App.tsx: the modal broadcast-hide now SKIPS popped-out workspaces, or opening
      Settings would blank a window on another monitor with nothing to re-show it.
      popOutBrowser closes the panel BEFORE awaiting the command -- ordering matters, see
      the comment: after the await, the panel's hide races the new window's show on the
      same ws id and a slow hide blanks the new window permanently. Error path restores.
      Boot restore: mode == "popout" -> reopen the window instead of the panel.
    - i18n browser.popout.open / browser.popout.failed in en/he/ar/ru. CSS
      .browser-window-popout + .popout-browser.
  Files: app/src-tauri/src/{workspace_browser.rs,settings.rs,lib.rs},
         app/src-tauri/capabilities/browser-popout.json,
         app/src/{BrowserChrome.tsx,BrowserWindow.tsx,App.tsx,index.tsx,App.css},
         app/src/components/PopoutBrowser.tsx, app/src/i18n/*.json,

                     frontend-shell}.md + .vault-lock.json

          out, same monitor/size), delete the workspace while popped out.

[2026-08-23] RTL: added `force_rtl` — a fourth rtl_mode that forces dir="rtl"
on EVERY row with no per-line detection, for remote panes. Yossi's ask:
"בא נוסיף אפשרות של RTL מלא - ולא שורה שורה - במרוחק".

What it is: same DOM renderer as `auto_per_line`, but `rowDirections()` returns
"rtl" for every row and reads none of `auto_direction` / `direction_policy` /
`suppress`. The dominance vote, block/table grouping and stripPaneFrame are all
bypassed, not tuned. The grid stays LTR (`rowsHost` keeps dir="ltr",
`.terminal-container { direction: ltr }` untouched) — only each row's paragraph
direction moves, so mouse/selection/caret column math is unaffected.

Opt-in: appears in the radio list for both profiles, but RTL_FIELD_DEFAULTS and
default_rtl_mode are unchanged (both profiles still `auto_per_line`), so the
local/remote separation invariant holds and nothing reaches a local pane unless
picked by hand.

Refactors this needed:
 - Six separate `=== "auto_per_line"` gates collapsed into one type guard,
   `usesRowDir(m): m is RowDirMode`. That scatter is what previously let a pane
   land in a combination that was none of the modes and did no bidi at all.
 - The row-direction decision lifted out of the DOM-bound
   `TerminalInstance.applyRowDirections` into a pure `rowDirections()` in
   textDirection.ts, so it is testable under `node --test`.

Two traps found and closed, both of which reverse letters:
 - `normaliseIncomingToLogical` stays pinned to `auto_per_line`. It asks whether
   the incoming BUFFER is visual order (a local/ConPTY condition), NOT whether
   the mode paints a dir. A remote stream is logical; normalising reverses it.
 - `unicode-bidi: bidi-override` is now gated `suppress && mode ===
   "auto_per_line"`. On `suppress` alone, force_rtl would pair bidi-override
   with dir="rtl" over a logical buffer = every Hebrew word reversed.

Fixed in passing: installRtlMouseCapture ran once in finishConstruction, so a
pane built on WebGL (`off`/`bidi_reorder`) and switched LIVE to a row-dir mode
got dir="rtl" rows with no coordinate mirroring — clicks and drag-select landed
on the mirrored column until the pane was reopened. Now idempotent
(`rtlMouseInstalled`) and re-run from applyRenderer.

Known + accepted cost: positional rows (tmux status line, zellij frame, vim,
htop, Claude Code) are full-width, so UAX #9 rule L2 reverses their run order
and the layout renders mirrored. Documented in docs/RTL-TEST.md as the deal
being struck, not as a bug.

Files: app/src/textDirection.ts (+48, rowDirections/RowDirMode),
app/src/terminalInstance.ts (RtlMode +force_rtl, usesRowDir, 6 gates, override
gate, isCurrentLineRtl, mouse-capture idempotency),
app/src/textDirection.test.ts (+78, 6 new cases), app/src/settings.ts (unions),
app/src-tauri/src/settings.rs (doc only), app/src/bindings/{RtlProfile,
TerminalSettings}.ts, app/src/SettingsModal.tsx (+1 radio),
app/src/i18n/{en,he,ar,ru}.json (+2 keys each).
Docs: docs/DECISIONS.md (Decided entry), docs/RTL-TEST.md (force_rtl section +
matrix), docs/vault/{frontend-lib,frontend-flows,backend-wizards}.md,
docs/vault/.vault-lock.json re-stamped.
Vault: green — `node scripts/vault-check.mjs` reports up to date.
Tests: node --test textDirection.test.ts = 75 pass / 0 fail (69 pre-existing +
6 new). mouseRtl.test.ts 11 pass. bidi/copyBidi tests need node_modules, which
this worktree does not have — unrelated to this change.
Status: NOT VERIFIED LIVE (Rule #14). Needs a real SSH pane: switch the remote
profile to Full RTL and check Hebrew, pure-Latin `ls`, drag-select, copy,
arrows, and that a local pane open alongside is untouched.

2026-08-26 — Phase 89: ws-header declutter — one ⋯ menu instead of four buttons
Trigger: Yossi — "ההגדרה של הטאבים היא כפתור נפרד למעלה, וזה יוצר עומס".

The workspace header carried seven buttons in a row: + diff, the split⇄tabs
toggle (Phase 84.A), Browser, Files, Insights, Tickets, notification bell. The
tabs toggle was the complaint, but it was one symptom of the row.

Decision (asked): an overflow menu, not a relocated toggle. Four items moved
behind a single ⋯ at the header's trailing edge — view mode, + diff, Insights,
Tickets. Browser, Files and the bell stay visible (the bell because of its
unread badge). Seven buttons -> four.

Every menu item calls the SAME handler its standalone button called
(setTabsMode / splitPane(pid,"horizontal","diff") / openPanelConnected("monitor")
/ openPanel("tickets")), so the menu, the palette command pane.viewMode.toggle
and the keyboard paths cannot diverge. No new dropdown component: .ws-header-menu
reuses .diff-pane-menu's CSS block, with inset-inline-end:0 so it opens inward
from the header edge (correct in RTL too). Click-away is a createEffect on
wsMenuOpen() listening for pointerdown, not click — a press landing in a
terminal pane never bubbles a click back to the header.

Files: app/src/App.tsx (signal + click-away effect, 4 buttons removed, ⋯ menu
added), app/src/icons.tsx (IconMore = lucide Ellipsis), app/src/App.css
(.ws-header-more / .ws-header-menu folded into the diff-pane-menu rules),
app/src/i18n/{en,he,ar,ru}.json (+1 key: ws_header.more).
Docs: docs/vault/frontend-shell.md (header paragraph), .vault-lock.json re-stamped.
Vault: green.
Status: NOT VERIFIED LIVE (Rule #14). CI is the type/build check; needs a real
run to confirm the menu opens, the four items work, click-away closes it, and
it doesn't overflow the window in Hebrew or in compact header mode.

================================================================================
2026-08-28 — manifest.json: stale winmux URLs + a dead field name
================================================================================
Found while checking whether a build off main was safe to cut. Two real bugs,
both survivors of the winmux -> YMUX rename; the third thing I first suspected
turned out to be already fixed.

1. Every URL still pointed at `yyhezkel/winmux` — notes_url, msi_url, nsis_url
   and all three hooks[].url. The code had already moved: DEFAULT_MANIFEST_URL
   (settings.rs) and the releases API (updater.rs) both say `ymux`. GitHub
   redirects a renamed repo so nothing was 404ing, but the file was lying about
   where it lives. Rewrote the repo path only — the ASSET FILENAMES stay
   `winmux_0.4.5_*`, because that is genuinely what the v0.4.5-beta.1 assets are
   called (verified with `gh release view`; sha256 + size in the manifest match
   the release exactly, so they were left untouched). All three rewritten URLs
   verified 200 before committing.

2. `min_winmux_version` is a dead key. `ManifestHook` (updater.rs:77) declares
   `min_ymux_version`, and the field is `#[serde(default)]`, so the old spelling
   was silently swallowed to None rather than failing loudly. Renamed. Note the
   field is declared but read nowhere yet — this makes it correct, not live.

NOT a bug after all: hooks.claude-code.version read 1.1.0 against a bundled spec
of 1.5.0, which would have meant the hooks-outdated banner never fired for a
remote sitting between those. PR #25 (Phase 86.E, merged as f69eb8d) had already
taken manifest, hooks/claude-code.json and BUNDLED_CLAUDE_VERSION to 1.6.0
together. Re-verified all three agree on main. Nothing to do.

DELIBERATELY UNTOUCHED: `version` stays 0.4.5-beta.1. Editing it IS the act of
publishing an update (RELEASING.md step 5), and 0.5.0 installs SIDE BY SIDE
rather than upgrading in place — different bundle id — so it is gated on release
notes that walk through install -> launch -> verify workspaces -> uninstall old
winmux. Yossi's call, asked and answered: bugs only, no publish. Consequence
that stands: 0.5.0-beta.1 and beta.2 remain unannounced to every 0.4.5 install.

Also spotted, not fixed: RELEASING.md tells you to write `msi_size` / `nsis_size`
and the Rust `Manifest` struct declares neither. serde ignores them (no
deny_unknown_fields), so they are harmless documentation-only fields. -> FOLLOWUPS.

Files: manifest.json (7 lines).
Vault: green — manifest.json is not a covered source file.
Status: verified by construction (JSON parses, no unknown hook keys, the three
rewritten URLs return 200). The hooks banner itself is NOT verified live.
